### [1] [Applying Deep Learning for cockpit segmentation in the context of mixed reality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06520)
*Alexandre Leles Sousa,Pedro de Oliveira Nielson,Erick Oliveira Rodrigues,Rafael Francisco dos Santos,Giovani Bernardes Vitor*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper uses camera images from a CAT793F truck simulator and compares U-Net vs. DeepLabV3+ for foreground-background segmentation in mixed reality; both reach about 90% accuracy, and the better model is selected.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to improve user immersion in mixed reality by enabling better real-time fusion of virtual environments with physical-world objects, which requires accurate foreground-background segmentation.

Method: The study captures real user images in the CAT793F off-highway truck simulator through a camera and applies two convolutional neural network segmentation architectures, U-Net and DeepLabV3+, to classify foreground versus background for mixed-reality compositing.

Result: Both U-Net and DeepLabV3+ were evaluated for image segmentation, producing performance metrics of approximately 90% accuracy, and the best model was identified based on these results.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that AI-based image segmentation can effectively separate foreground and background in mixed-reality scenarios for an off-highway truck simulator, and among the tested models, the best-performing architecture achieved around 90% accuracy.

Abstract: Computer vision is an area that has been growing continuously. With the advance of technologies with a first-person view, new development opportunities have emerged inside the area. Mixed reality promotes virtual environments with objects from the physical world shown in real time. For that, it's necessary to be concerned with the immersion of the user in this simulated environment, increasingly seeking to bring it closer to a possible desired reality. This paper proposes the development of image processing in order to perform the segmentation of images to identify what is foreground and background in order to facilitate the union of virtual and real images. Thus, the present work obtain real images of the user using the off-highway truck simulator CAT793F, through a camera, to be able to perform the segmentation of such images with artificial intelligence techniques.The convolutional neural network architectures "U-net" and "DeepLabV3+" are applied to perform image segmentation. As a result, metrics with around 90% accuracy were presented and and the best model was determined.

</details>


### [2] [STREAM: Stochastic Riemannian Flow Matching with Anisotropic Decoder for Digital Histopathology Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07036)
*Won June Cho,Daeky Jeong,Hyeongyeol Lim,Hongjun Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Instead of conditioning on pathology foundation-model features, STREAM uses those features as the latent space itself and applies Riemannian flow matching plus an anisotropic decoder, improving histopathology image reconstruction and generation SOTA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing histopathology image generators often use pretrained Vision Foundation Models only as conditioning signals, which can cause conditioning collapse: the conditioning overwhelms the latent space and reduces image quality and diversity. The authors aim to avoid this while also addressing privacy concerns and the need for scalable training data in computational pathology.

Method: The paper proposes STREAM, a two-stage framework. First, it models pretrained histopathology VFM patch-token embeddings as latent variables on the unit hypersphere and trains a Diffusion Transformer in this latent space using a bridge-type stochastic perturbation for per-token rectifiability under Riemannian flow matching. Second, it uses a novel anisotropic decoder that is robust in low-energy directions of the velocity-field Jacobian while preserving fidelity in high-energy directions.

Result: Empirically, the authors show that histopathology VFM patch-token features are l2-normalized, lie on the unit hypersphere, and exhibit angular structure and intrinsic curvature that motivate a Riemannian approach. On breast and colorectal cancer datasets, STREAM achieves state-of-the-art performance for both reconstruction and image generation.

Conclusion: STREAM demonstrates that using pretrained histopathology VFM patch-token features directly as the latent space, together with a Riemannian flow-matching formulation on the unit hypersphere and an anisotropic decoder, yields state-of-the-art reconstruction and synthetic image generation performance on breast and colorectal histopathology datasets.

Abstract: Synthetic histopathology image generation addresses critical challenges in computational pathology, including patient privacy and the growing need for large-scale training data for foundation models. Latent diffusion models have dominated the image generation domain, with recent works emphasizing that the choice of latent space is critical to the quality of generated images. Existing state-of-the-art generative models in histopathology use pretrained Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) as conditioning signals, and we observe that this leads to "conditioning collapse," where the conditioning signal dominates the latent space and lowers the quality and diversity of generated samples. Therefore, we instead use pretrained histopathology VFMs as the latent space itself, leveraging their patch-token features that encode rich semantic information. We empirically show that these features are $\ell_2$-normalized and lie on the unit hypersphere $\mathcal{S}^{d-1}$ with strong angular dominance and intrinsic curvature, making them naturally suited for a Riemannian formulation. We therefore present STREAM, the first framework to apply Riemannian flow matching in the pathology domain. STREAM consists of two stages: 1) a bridge-type stochastic perturbation that establishes per-token rectifiability on $\mathcal{S}^{d-1}$ for training a Diffusion Transformer (DiT) in latent space, and 2) a novel anisotropic decoder that allocates robustness to low-energy directions of the velocity-field Jacobian while preserving fidelity along its high-energy directions. Together, STREAM achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction and generation performance on breast and colorectal cancer datasets. The code will be publicly released upon acceptance.

</details>


### [3] [GOPAgen: Motion-Aware and Efficient Agentic Long-Video Understanding with Structural Memory and Hierarchical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06532)
*Haozhe Chi,Yang Jin,Yadong Mu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GOPAgen uses video codec structure to model motion more precisely and stores/retrieves that information efficiently, leading to better long-video VQA performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing agentic long-video understanding methods still lack fine-grained motion comprehension and efficient memory architectures, especially for capturing detailed local motion over long temporal spans.

Method: The paper proposes GOPAgen, which introduces a motion agent trained on codec-level Groups of Pictures (GOPs), a GOP tree reasoning algorithm for local motion understanding, a structural memory mechanism combining local motion with detailed captions, a coarse-to-fine zoom-in strategy for memory exploitation, and a motion vector database for efficient multi-granularity retrieval.

Result: The method achieves superior Video Question Answering performance across multiple video understanding benchmarks, including MotionBench and EgoSchema, indicating better long-video reasoning and motion understanding.

Conclusion: GOPAgen demonstrates that integrating video codec structure, GOP-based motion reasoning, structural memory, and motion vector retrieval can substantially improve long-video understanding and achieve strong VQA results on benchmarks such as MotionBench and EgoSchema.

Abstract: Despite significant progress in agentic long video understanding, existing methods still lack detailed motion comprehension coupled with an efficient memory architecture. In this paper, we propose GOPAgen, a novel approach that first integrates video codec into the video understanding framework via a meticulously designed motion agent trained on Groups of Pictures (GOPs) from video codec. We further develop a GOP tree reasoning algorithm, which is naturally aligned with video codec and enhances the model's ability to understand local detailed motions in videos. Additionally, we carefully design a structural memory mechanism that integrates local motion information with detailed captions in structural pages, and propose an efficient coarse-to-fine zoom-in algorithm to fully exploit the structural memory. Furthermore, we incorporate a motion vector database into the framework to enable efficient retrieval of motion vectors at different granularities. Overall, our method achieves superior Video Question Answering (VQA) performance on various video understanding benchmarks, including MotionBench and Egoschema, thereby demonstrating the superiority of our proposed framework.

</details>


### [4] [Attention-Guided Autoencoder Fusion for Insulator Defect Detection Using UAV Transmission-Line Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06536)
*Malak Allam,Khaled Shaban,Ali Hamdi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AE-YOLO adds attention and autoencoders to YOLO to better preserve defect cues during multi-scale fusion and improve rare-defect sensitivity, delivering clearly better insulator defect detection in UAV images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing automated insulator defect detection in UAV imagery is difficult because defects are rare, vary greatly in scale, and occupy very small regions, while backgrounds are complex and class imbalance is severe.

Method: AE-YOLO integrates lightweight bottleneck autoencoders into the FPN-PAN neck, applies CBAM attention in the backbone, uses variance-maximizing autoencoder regularization, trains with focal loss + CIoU loss + autoencoder regularization, and performs inference with WBF over YOLOv8/YOLOv10/YOLO11 plus autoencoder-guided confidence boosting.

Result: On the Insulator-Defect Detection dataset, AE-YOLO with EfficientNetV2 achieves 95.10% mAP@0.5, 96.40% precision, and 93.80% recall, exceeding the strongest YOLO-family baseline by 5.0 mAP@0.5 points and 6.7 recall points.

Conclusion: The proposed AE-YOLO framework effectively improves UAV-based insulator defect detection under class imbalance, scale variation, and tiny-defect conditions, outperforming strong YOLO baselines and providing a practical scalable inspection solution.

Abstract: Automated defect detection in high-voltage transmission-line insulators remains challenging due to severe class imbalance, large scale variation, and the small spatial extent of defect instances in Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) imagery. To address these challenges, this paper proposes AE-YOLO, an Attention-Guided AutoEncoder-Enhanced YOLO framework for robust insulator defect detection. The architecture integrates lightweight bottleneck autoencoders within a Feature Pyramid Network-Path Aggregation Network (FPN-PAN) neck. This preserves anomaly-sensitive information during multi-scale feature fusion. Convolutional Block Attention Modules (CBAM) are used throughout the backbone, enhancing feature discrimination and suppressing background interference. The framework also introduces a variance-maximizing autoencoder regularization strategy, which encourages diverse, defect-discriminative latent representations. The network trains using a unified objective that combines focal loss, Complete IoU (CIoU) loss, and autoencoder regularization to address foreground-background imbalance and improve localization accuracy. During inference, Weighted Boxes Fusion (WBF) combines predictions from YOLOv8, YOLOv10, and YOLO11. An autoencoder-guided confidence boosting mechanism improves sensitivity to rare defect categories. Experiments on the Insulator-Defect Detection dataset show that AE-YOLO with an EfficientNetV2 backbone achieves 95.10 percent mAP at 0.5, 96.40 percent precision, and 93.80 percent recall. This performance surpasses the strongest YOLO-family baseline by 5.0 points in mAP at 0.5 and 6.7 points in recall. These results confirm the effectiveness and adaptability of the framework. The model is a practical and scalable solution for UAV-based transmission-line inspection and defect monitoring.

</details>


### [5] [WorldBench: A Challenging and Visually Diverse Multimodal Reasoning Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06538)
*Yida Yin,Harish Krishnakumar,Chung Peng Lee,Boya Zeng,Wenhao Chai,Shengbang Tong,Wenhu Chen,Hu Xu,Xingyu Fu,Gabriel Sarch,Aleksandra Korolova,Zhuang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WorldBench is a highly visually diverse benchmark for multimodal reasoning, built from a large taxonomy of visual concepts and hard manually crafted questions; results show current MLLMs still struggle significantly, highlighting a major gap in visual understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multimodal benchmarks expand task categories but do not adequately capture the visual diversity needed for reliable performance in real-world, open-ended settings. The work is motivated by the need for a benchmark that better represents the visual world and stresses visual understanding.

Method: The paper builds a taxonomy of thousands of visual concepts across multiple domains, curates a broad and diverse image collection from search engines and existing datasets, and manually designs difficult reasoning questions through structured trial-and-error to create a benchmark that strong frontier MLLMs struggle to solve.

Result: WorldBench achieves higher visual diversity than existing diverse benchmarks in both quantitative and human evaluations. Testing 15 MLLMs shows that even the best model only reaches 64.0% accuracy, while some models perform only slightly above chance.

Conclusion: Visual diversity is essential for evaluating MLLMs, and current benchmarks underrepresent the open-ended variability of real-world visual inputs. WorldBench addresses this gap and exposes substantial weaknesses in current models.

Abstract: In real-world applications, models are expected to perform reliably across diverse settings. Yet, many existing multimodal benchmarks expand task types without capturing the visual diversity needed to handle open-ended visual inputs. We present WorldBench, a challenging and visually diverse reasoning benchmark to evaluate Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). We build a taxonomy of thousands of visual concepts across multiple domains (e.g., living things). Guided by this taxonomy, we curate a broad collection of images from search engines and existing datasets to comprehensively represent the visual world. Through structured trial-and-error, we manually design challenging questions that frontier MLLMs fail to answer. On quantitative and human evaluations, WorldBench achieves higher visual diversity than any existing diverse benchmark. Evaluating 15 MLLMs on WorldBench reveals weaknesses in visual understanding: even the strongest model reaches only 64.0% accuracy, while some models perform marginally above chance-level. We hope our work highlights the importance of visual diversity in building multimodal benchmarks.

</details>


### [6] [Synthetic Benchmarks Overstate Forward-Forward Scaling: Real-Data Limits of Layer-Local Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06539)
*Yucheng Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DTG-FF is a strong Forward-Forward variant and the best FF-family method reported here, but the paper argues that FF still falls behind backpropagation on realistic image tasks, that synthetic benchmarks overstate its scalability, and that its memory advantage is not convincing under fair hardware comparisons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work asks whether Forward-Forward learning is becoming a viable alternative to backpropagation at realistic scale, since prior FF-CNN results on 32x32 benchmarks appeared promising but had not resolved whether the method transfers to larger, real-world settings.

Method: The paper develops DTG-FF, a Forward-Forward variant that combines dynamic temperature goodness, decoupled normalization, and multi-layer fusion. It evaluates this instrument against architecture-matched backpropagation baselines on nine real-data benchmarks, synthetic teacher-student tasks, and a systems-level memory/throughput audit.

Result: DTG-FF reaches 91.8% on CIFAR-10 and provides the first FF baseline on ImageNet-100 224x224, but architecture-matched BP-DeepSup still outperforms it by 2.40 points on CIFAR-10 and 5.93 points on CIFAR-100, with the gap widening as class count increases. On 224x224 it reaches only 49.4%, far below typical BP performance above 75%. Synthetic K-sweeps favor FF as class count grows, but real-image experiments show the opposite trend. In systems tests on 8 GB hardware, BP with gradient accumulation uses less memory and is faster than DTG-FF, undermining a memory-efficiency justification.

Conclusion: DTG-FF improves the Forward-Forward family and sets new state of the art across several benchmarks, but the paper concludes that layer-local training does not yet scale competitively to realistic real-data settings and does not deliver a convincing memory advantage under fair comparisons.

Abstract: Forward-Forward (FF) learning [Hinton, 2022] replaces backpropagation with strictly layer-local goodness updates. Recent FF-CNN work has narrowed the gap to BP on 32x32 benchmarks, raising the question of whether layer-local training is becoming a viable alternative at realistic scale. To probe this rigorously, we develop DTG-FF -- dynamic temperature goodness, decoupled normalization, and multi-layer fusion -- as an instrument that sets FF-family state of the art across nine real-data benchmarks (91.8% CIFAR-10 and the first FF baseline at ImageNet-100 224x224), and use it to audit how far layer-local training actually scales.
  (1) Real-data scaling. Under identical recipe and backbone, an architecture-matched BP-DeepSup baseline beats DTG-FF by 2.40/5.93 pp on CIFAR-10/CIFAR-100, and the gap widens with class count. At 224x224 the same instrument reaches only 49.4% -- the first FF baseline at this scale, versus typical BP above 75% [Tian et al., 2020] -- exposing a real-data ceiling invisible at 32x32.
  (2) Synthetic vs. real K-conflict. DTG-FF increasingly outperforms BP as class count K grows on synthetic teacher-student tasks, yet on real images the FF-BP gap reverses sign and widens with K. A within-dataset CIFAR-100 coarse vs. fine probe isolates label-hierarchy from image distribution: synthetic K-sweeps confound output dimensionality with fine-grained discrimination difficulty and thereby overstate FF transferability.
  (3) Systems audit. FF can be implemented without storing depth-wide activations, but on commodity 8 GB hardware standard BP+gradient-accumulation reaches 4.18 GB / 157 imgs/s versus DTG-FF's 7.90 GB / 138 imgs/s, so a memory-based justification for FF at this scale is not supported under fair baselines.

</details>


### [7] [Direct 3D-Aware Object Insertion via Decomposed Visual Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06601)
*Jingbo Gong,Yikai Wang,Yushi Lan,Yuhao Wan,Ziheng Ouyang,Rui Zhao,Ming-Ming Cheng,Qibin Hou,Chen Change Loy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DIRECT 是一种可控目标插入方法：把外观、3D 姿态和场景上下文分开建模并分别注入生成过程，从而比传统 2D 修补式方法更能精确控制姿态且生成质量更高。 / DIRECT is a pose-controllable object insertion framework that separately models and injects appearance, 3D pose, and scene context, achieving better control and higher visual quality than conventional 2D inpainting-style insertion methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有基于扩散的目标插入方法通常将任务视为 2D 修补，虽然视觉质量高，但缺乏对目标 3D 姿态的显式控制，限制了实际应用。 / Existing diffusion-based object insertion methods treat the problem as 2D inpainting: they generate high-quality images but lack explicit 3D pose control, which limits practical usability.

Method: 该方法提出 DIRECT 框架，将插入条件分为三部分：来自参考物体的外观引导、来自用户调整 3D proxy 的几何引导、以及来自目标背景的上下文引导；三者通过独立路径注入扩散式图像合成模型，同时配合自动化数据构建流程提升训练数据多样性与质量。 / The paper proposes DIRECT, which decomposes insertion conditions into appearance guidance from the reference object, geometry guidance from a user-adjusted 3D proxy, and context guidance from the target background; these are injected through separate pathways in a diffusion-based synthesis framework, along with an automated data construction pipeline to improve training diversity and quality.

Result: 实验表明，DIRECT 在保持参考外观、遵循用户指定姿态、以及与目标场景融合方面表现更好，并在几何可控性和视觉质量上超过以往方法。 / Experiments show that DIRECT better preserves reference appearance, follows user-specified pose, and adapts to the target scene, outperforming previous methods in geometric controllability and visual quality.

Conclusion: DIRECT 通过将外观、几何与上下文条件解耦并分别注入生成过程，实现了可控姿态的目标插入，并在几何可控性与视觉质量上优于现有方法。 / DIRECT shows that decomposing appearance, geometry, and context guidance into separate injection pathways enables pose-controllable object insertion and surpasses prior methods in both geometric controllability and visual quality.

Abstract: Object insertion aims to seamlessly composite a reference object into a specified region of a background image. Recent diffusion-based methods achieve high visual quality but formulate insertion as a simple 2D inpainting task, providing no explicit control over the object's 3D pose and limiting their practical applicability. We propose DIRECT (Decomposed Injection for Reference Composition and Target-integration), a novel framework that integrates interactive pose manipulation with high-fidelity 2D image synthesis to enable pose-controllable object insertion. Our method decomposes the insertion conditions into three complementary components: appearance guidance capturing visual details from the reference object, geometry guidance derived from the user-adjusted 3D proxy, and context guidance from the target background. By injecting them through separate pathways, DIRECT avoids feature entanglement and simultaneously preserves reference appearance, follows the user-specified pose, and adapts the object to the target scene. We also introduce an automated data construction pipeline to improve the diversity and quality of training data. Experiments show that DIRECT outperforms previous methods in both geometric controllability and visual quality.

</details>


### [8] [RigPAPR: Rig-Based Animation of Static Neural Point Clouds from a Fixed-Viewpoint Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06685)
*Shichong Peng,Yanshu Zhang,Ke Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RigPAPR turns a static PAPR point cloud into a rigged 3D avatar from one fixed-view video, avoiding LBS joint artifacts seen in splats/meshes and improving novel-view quality by 3+ dB PSNR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing animation methods for static neural reconstructions often deform Gaussian splats or mesh proxies with LBS, which creates artifacts near articulated joints. The authors argue that the root cause is the representation itself: splats have fixed per-primitive shapes fitted in canonical pose, so they cannot bend naturally under articulation. This motivates using PAPR, where surfaces are recomposed at render time from deformed point positions.

Method: The paper proposes RigPAPR, which starts from a static neural point reconstruction using Proximity Attention Point Rendering (PAPR), automatically rigs the point cloud, and drives it with direct linear blend skinning from a single fixed-viewpoint driving video. Unlike prior methods, it does not rely on mesh proxies, pose-dependent correction, or category templates.

Result: On synthetic subjects, RigPAPR matches the strongest baseline on the supervised input view and outperforms mesh-based and Gaussian-splatting baselines on novel views by more than 3 dB PSNR. It also produces cleaner joint-boundary renderings on both synthetic and real subjects.

Conclusion: RigPAPR demonstrates that auto-rigging a static PAPR point cloud and animating it with direct LBS from a monocular fixed-view video can avoid the joint-boundary artifacts common in mesh-based and Gaussian-splatting approaches, while improving novel-view rendering quality and recovering a rigged, re-posable 3D asset.

Abstract: Static neural point reconstructions capture a subject at high fidelity from posed images. Given such a reconstruction, we aim to animate it to follow a monocular fixed-viewpoint driving video of the subject, whether captured or produced by image-to-video (I2V) generation, and to recover a rigged, re-posable 3D asset. Existing methods deform Gaussian splats through direct linear blend skinning (LBS) or mesh proxies, both of which are prone to joint-boundary artifacts under articulation, even with per-primitive corrections. We trace the artifact to the representation: each splat carries an individual shape calibrated in the canonical pose to tile with its neighbours. Under rigid LBS, each splat moves with its bone but cannot bend, so the canonical tiling breaks at joint boundaries into gaps and spikes. Proximity attention point rendering (PAPR) instead carries no per-primitive shape; each pixel is recomposed at render time from the deformed primitives' positions, so the surface re-forms naturally with the articulation. We present RigPAPR, which auto-rigs a static PAPR cloud and drives it under direct LBS from a single fixed-viewpoint video, without mesh proxy, pose-dependent correction, or category template. On synthetic subjects, RigPAPR matches the strongest baseline at the supervised view and exceeds mesh-based and Gaussian-splatting baselines at novel views by 3+dB PSNR, with cleaner joint-boundary renderings of both synthetic and real subjects.

</details>


### [9] [From Pixels to Newtons: Predicting In Vivo Joint Contact Forces from Monocular Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06631)
*Jessy Lauer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A transformer-based, physics-free system predicts 3D hip and knee contact forces directly from uncalibrated monocular video, reaching musculoskeletal-simulation-level accuracy and showing strong generalization, which could enable scalable clinical and at-home joint loading assessment. / 一个基于Transformer、无需物理建模的系统可直接从未经标定的单目视频预测三维髋/膝关节接触力，精度达到肌骨仿真水平且泛化性强，有望推动可扩展的临床与居家关节载荷评估。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct measurement of joint contact forces is invasive and limited to a very small number of patients with instrumented implants, which constrains research and clinical use. A noninvasive, scalable alternative from ordinary video would enable broader assessment of implant loading, cartilage health, rehabilitation outcomes, and intervention effects. / 研究动机在于：关节接触力的直接测量具有侵入性，且仅限于少数装有测量型植入物的患者，严重限制了研究和临床应用。如果能从普通视频中实现非侵入、可扩展的估计，就能更广泛地评估植入物受力、软骨健康、康复效果和干预收益。

Method: The method is a physics-free pipeline: recover per-frame parametric body meshes from raw monocular video, encode them as kinematic features, and decode them into hip/knee contact forces using a transformer. The pose stream is adaptively modulated at every layer by body shape, joint, side, activity text, and self-supervised video tokens (V-JEPA 2). The model jointly handles hip and knee prediction and can also operate without curated activity labels using video features alone. / 方法是一条无物理模型的流程：先从原始单目视频逐帧恢复参数化人体网格，将其编码为运动学特征，再通过Transformer解码为髋/膝关节接触力。其姿态流在每一层都由体型、关节类型、左右侧、活动文本以及自监督视频token（V-JEPA 2）进行自适应调制。模型统一处理髋关节和膝关节预测，并且在没有精细活动标签时也可仅依赖视频特征运行。

Result: Under leave-one-subject-out cross-validation on 26 patients and 25 activity categories from OrthoLoad, the method achieves 0.32 ± 0.08 BW RMSE for hip and 0.23 ± 0.03 BW RMSE for knee, matching subject-specific musculoskeletal simulations. It also generalizes zero-shot to an independent instrumented cohort, where it rivals or exceeds prior published methods. Video-only features preserve accuracy without curated activity labels, and the learned predictor can drive a generative motion prior to produce plausible lower-load movement variants. / 在OrthoLoad数据库的26名患者、25类活动上进行留一受试者交叉验证时，该方法取得了髋关节0.32 ± 0.08 BW RMSE、膝关节0.23 ± 0.03 BW RMSE的结果，与受试者特异性肌骨仿真精度相当。它还能零样本泛化到独立的植入式测量队列，并达到或超过既有公开方法。即使没有人工活动标签，仅用视频特征也能保持精度；此外，该预测器还能驱动生成式动作先验，产生生物力学合理的低峰值载荷动作变体。

Conclusion: The paper concludes that uncalibrated monocular video can be used as a practical modality for estimating instantaneous 3D hip and knee joint contact forces with accuracy comparable to subject-specific musculoskeletal simulations, while also supporting zero-shot generalization and biomechanically guided motion generation. / 论文结论是：未经标定的单目视频可以作为一种可行的关节载荷估计模态，用于预测瞬时三维髋关节与膝关节接触力，其精度可与受试者特异性的肌骨仿真相当，同时具备零样本泛化和生物力学引导动作生成能力。

Abstract: Joint contact forces govern implant longevity, cartilage health, and rehabilitation outcomes, shaping who develops osteoarthritis, who recovers well from joint replacement, and who benefits from biomechanical interventions. Yet they remain measurable only invasively, in a few dozen patients with instrumented implants. I present a physics-free pipeline to predict instantaneous 3D hip and knee contact forces from an uncalibrated monocular video: no markers, force plates, electromyography, subject-specific imaging, or musculoskeletal model. Parametric body meshes are recovered per frame, encoded as kinematic features, and decoded into forces by a transformer whose pose stream is adaptively modulated at every layer by body shape, joint, side, activity text, and self-supervised video tokens (V-JEPA 2), unifying hip and knee in a single model. Under leave-one-subject-out cross-validation across 26 patients and 25 activity categories from the in vivo OrthoLoad database, the pipeline matches the accuracy of subject-specific musculoskeletal simulations ($0.32 \pm 0.08$ BW RMSE for hip; $0.23 \pm 0.03$ BW for knee) and resolves peak force changes smaller than those reported for gait retraining and osteoarthritis progression. Applied zero-shot to an independent instrumented cohort, it rivals or outperforms prior published methods. Even without curated activity labels, video features alone preserve accuracy and enable end-to-end inference on raw footage. Driven by the predictor, a generative motion prior produces biomechanically plausible variants with reduced peak loading, rediscovering strategies from the predictive simulation literature. This pipeline establishes uncalibrated monocular video as a viable modality for estimating joint loading, opening a path toward retrospective analysis of archived clinical recordings, primary-care screening, and at-home rehabilitation tracking.

</details>


### [10] [3DMorph: Single-Image-Guided Local 3D Shape Editing and Morphing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07115)
*Tobias Preintner,Yunfei Deng,Phillip Müller,Sebastian Illing,Adrian König,Thomas Bäck,Elena Raponi,Niki van Stein*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 3DMorph is a training-free system for editing local 3D geometry from a single edited image; it finds the target region automatically, applies the shape change while keeping the rest intact, supports morphing, and beats prior methods on the new Delta3D benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work is motivated by the lack of simple, intuitive, and effective local geometry editing tools for general 3D objects. Existing methods are often global, domain-specific, interaction-heavy, or focused on appearance rather than shape geometry.

Method: The paper proposes 3DMorph, a training-free framework that takes an edited 2D image as guidance, automatically localizes the corresponding editable region on a 3D object, transfers the desired 2D shape change into 3D geometry, preserves untouched parts, and supports interpolation between the original and edited shapes.

Result: Experiments show that 3DMorph successfully converts intuitive 2D edits into local 3D shape changes and outperforms state-of-the-art generative and editing baselines. The paper also introduces Delta3D, a benchmark with paired ground-truth local edits for evaluating image-guided 3D editing quality.

Conclusion: 3DMorph demonstrates that training-free, single-image-guided local 3D shape editing is feasible and effective, enabling accurate geometry-aware local modifications and smooth intermediate morphing while preserving unchanged regions.

Abstract: Despite recent progress in 3D generation, intuitive editing of existing shapes remains limited. Unlike images, which benefit from well-established inpainting tools, general 3D objects such as meshes still lack simple and effective methods for local shape editing. Existing approaches are often global, domain-specific, require complex user interaction, or focus on appearance (color and texture) rather than geometry. We introduce 3DMorph, a training-free framework for single-image-guided local 3D shape editing and morphing. Given an edited image showing a desired shape modification, our method automatically localizes the relevant 3D region and transfers 2D modifications to 3D while preserving unmodified areas. 3DMorph also enables intermediate shape generation between the original and edited objects, facilitating design exploration. To benchmark editing quality, we introduce Delta3D, an image-guided local 3D editing benchmark with paired ground-truth edits. Experimental results show that 3DMorph translates intuitive 2D edits into 3D, outperforming state-of-the-art generative and editing methods.

</details>


### [11] [Inside the Visual Mind: Neuroscience-Motivated Concept Circuits for Interpreting and Steering Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06664)
*Tang Li,Yanlin Chen,Mengmeng Ma,Xi Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ViSAE用更强的概念库和概念电路分析，把ViT内部表示变得更可解释，并能实际修正由伪相关特征导致的错误。 / ViSAE makes ViT representations more interpretable through a stronger concept vocabulary and concept-circuit analysis, and can practically correct errors driven by spurious features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：虽然ViT准确率很高，但其预测可能依赖伪相关线索，部署前需要理解其内部机制；而现有基于稀疏自编码器的解释方法在ViT上面临概念覆盖不足、特征解释主观且难扩展的问题。 / The motivation is that although ViTs are highly accurate, their predictions can rely on spurious cues, so understanding their internal mechanisms is necessary before safe deployment; existing SAE-based interpretation for ViTs suffers from limited concept coverage and subjective, non-scalable feature interpretation.

Method: 方法上，论文构建了ViSAE框架，包含三部分：一个含64K图像和16K视觉概念词汇的探测数据与概念库；自顶向下的概念读取算法；自底向上的电路追踪算法，用于从SAE分解后的表征中恢复ViT的概念电路，并进一步执行概念编辑。 / Methodologically, the paper builds the ViSAE framework with three parts: a probing suite containing 64K images and a 16K visually grounded concept vocabulary, a top-down concept reading algorithm, and a bottom-up circuit tracing algorithm to recover ViT concept circuits from SAE-based representations, followed by concept editing for intervention.

Result: 结果显示，ViSAE的概念库相比ImageNet将概念覆盖效率提升20倍，相比现有概念集合将解释准确率提升28.7%；在WaterBirds上，通过概念编辑将最差组准确率提升48.2%，并比现有方法高23.8%。 / Results show that ViSAE improves concept coverage efficiency by 20x over ImageNet and interpretation accuracy by 28.7% over existing concept sets; on WaterBirds, concept editing improves worst-group accuracy by 48.2%, outperforming prior methods by 23.8%.

Conclusion: ViSAE提出了一套面向Vision Transformer的机制可解释性工具箱，通过概念电路自动恢复模型内部工作机制，并能用于审计与干预模型行为；实验显示其在概念覆盖、解释准确性和偏置纠正上均显著优于现有方法。 / ViSAE introduces a mechanistic interpretability toolbox for Vision Transformers that automatically recovers internal concept circuits and supports auditing and steering model behavior; experiments show clear gains in concept coverage, interpretation accuracy, and bias mitigation over prior methods.

Abstract: Despite high accuracy, Vision Transformer (ViT) predictions can be driven by spurious cues, raising the need to understand their inner workings before safe deployment. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) provide a promising lens for decomposing model representations into human-interpretable concepts, yet adapting SAE-based interpretation to ViTs remains challenging due to limited control over concept coverage and subjective, non-scalable feature interpretation. To fill the gaps, motivated by neuroscience-inspired principles, we propose ViSAE, a mechanistic interpretability toolbox for understanding ViT inner workings through concept circuits. ViSAE consists of three components: (1) A probing suite with 64K images and a 16K visually grounded concept vocabulary, improving concept coverage efficiency by 20x over ImageNet and interpretation accuracy by 28.7% over existing concept sets. (2) Top-down concept reading and Bottom-up circuit tracing algorithms that automatically recover ViT inner workings via concept circuits. (3) Applications for auditing and steering ViT behavior. Through concept editing, ViSAE improves the worst-group accuracy on WaterBirds by 48.2%, outperforming existing methods by 23.8%. Our data and code: https://github.com/deep-real/ViSAE.

</details>


### [12] [ExMesh: EXplicit Mesh Reconstruction with Topology Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07288)
*Chuanjin Fan,Lifan Wu,Wenjie Chang,Hanzhi Chang,Wenfei Yang,Tianzhu Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ExMesh directly reconstructs and optimizes explicit meshes from multi-view images using adaptive split/merge topology updates plus real-time UV maintenance, avoiding common post-processing artifacts and producing accurate, efficient, and concise meshes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-view surface reconstruction methods usually depend on intermediate representations and post-processing, which often introduce artifacts and fragmented geometry. Although direct explicit mesh optimization is promising, it faces two main challenges: adaptive topology refinement without degeneracies, and consistent UV maintenance for high-quality texturing during structural changes.

Method: The paper proposes ExMesh, a framework for direct explicit mesh optimization. It introduces adaptive vertex splitting and merging for topology refinement and simplification, and maintains UV coordinates in real time during topology changes, enabling coarse-to-fine differentiable optimization without relying on intermediate representations such as implicit fields, Marching Cubes, or TSDF fusion.

Result: Extensive experiments show that ExMesh achieves a strong tradeoff among reconstruction accuracy, computational efficiency, and mesh compactness. The authors also claim it is the first method to seamlessly integrate discrete topology operations into a continuous differentiable optimization pipeline for explicit mesh reconstruction.

Conclusion: ExMesh demonstrates that explicit surface meshes can be directly optimized from multi-view images by combining differentiable optimization with discrete topology updates, yielding accurate, efficient, and compact reconstructions while preserving mesh and UV consistency.

Abstract: Reconstructing surface meshes from multi-view images has remained a core challenge in recent years. Most existing methods, whether implicit or explicit, depend on intermediate representations and post-processing steps like Marching Cubes or TSDF fusion, often resulting in artifacts and fragmented geometry. Directly optimizing explicit meshes is a promising approach. However, it presents two critical challenges. The first is how to adaptively refine mesh topology to capture detail without introducing degenerate faces. The second is how to maintain consistent UV coordinates for high-fidelity texturing as the mesh structure evolves. To overcome these, we propose ExMesh, a novel framework that directly optimizes explicit meshes by integrating differentiable optimization with discrete topology updates. Specifically, we introduce an adaptive vertex splitting and merging strategy, along with real-time UV maintenance, to enable coarse-to-fine optimization while preserving geometric integrity. To our knowledge, ExMesh is the first framework to seamlessly integrate discrete topology operations into a continuous differentiable optimization pipeline. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ExMesh achieves a balance among accuracy, computational efficiency, and mesh conciseness.

</details>


### [13] [Architecture-Adaptive Uncertainty Fusion for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06666)
*Ritesh Sharma,Mohammad Ghasemigol,Yuichi Motai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces COF, a fast, model-agnostic way to fuse multiple uncertainty signals for deepfake detection. It is competitive in-domain, more robust than stronger non-linear baselines under distribution shift, and exposes that cross-dataset uncertainty generalization remains largely unsolved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although deepfake detectors report near-perfect benchmark accuracy, deployment requires trustworthy uncertainty estimates. Existing uncertainty quantification methods usually rely on a single source of uncertainty and do not account for the fact that the best way to combine uncertainty signals may depend on the model architecture.

Method: The paper proposes Correlation-Optimized Fusion (COF), which combines five complementary uncertainty sources—epistemic, aleatoric, calibration, conformal, and distributional—by solving a constrained optimization problem on the probability simplex to maximize the Pearson correlation between fused uncertainty scores and prediction errors. It requires no modification to the base detector and only optimizes fusion weights.

Result: Across eleven architectures on FaceForensics++, non-linear methods achieve about 5–6% higher in-domain error-uncertainty correlation than COF on average, but COF becomes stronger under distribution shift. On CelebDF, COF outperforms Random Forest in 9 of 11 architectures, with gains up to 7.3x in correlation. Cross-dataset tests on CelebDF and DFDC show that all methods collapse from in-domain correlations around 0.41–0.47 to near zero externally, with a mean degradation of 90.7% and uncertainty inversion in seven architectures.

Conclusion: COF is a practical and interpretable architecture-adaptive uncertainty fusion framework for deepfake detection under controlled distributions, but all evaluated methods suffer severe cross-dataset generalization failure, making domain-adaptive uncertainty quantification the key unresolved problem for real-world forensic deployment.

Abstract: Deepfake detection systems achieve near-perfect accuracy on benchmarks, yet forensic deployment demands reliable prediction uncertainty. Existing uncertainty quantification (UQ) methods rely on single sources and ignore that optimal uncertainty composition varies across architectures. We propose Correlation-Optimized Fusion (COF), an architecture-adaptive framework that fuses five complementary uncertainty sources -- epistemic, aleatoric, calibration, conformal, and distributional -- by maximizing Pearson correlation between fused uncertainty scores and prediction errors via constrained optimization on the probability simplex. COF requires no model modifications and only 42 s of weight optimization, compared to 20--45 h for a 5-model Deep Ensemble. Evaluation across eleven architectures on FaceForensics++ reveals a fundamental trade-off: under matched train/evaluation protocol, non-linear methods achieve approximately 5--6% higher in-domain correlation than COF (mean r = 0.438), but this reverses under distribution shift. On CelebDF, COF outperforms Random Forest in 9/11 architectures with up to 7.3x higher correlation (MaxViT-B: r = 0.249 vs. 0.034); RF degrades 85% cross-domain to r = 0.071, whereas COF retains substantially more signal (74% drop to r = 0.116). Cross-dataset evaluation on CelebDF and DFDC reveals catastrophic generalization failure across all methods: in-domain correlations of 0.41--0.47 collapse to near-zero externally (mean degradation 90.7%), with seven of eleven architectures exhibiting uncertainty inversion. These results establish COF as a practical, interpretable framework for controlled-distribution deployment and identify domain-adaptive UQ as the central open challenge for forensic deployment.

</details>


### [14] [JA-SIREN: Deterministic Initialization for Sinusoidal Networks via Spectral Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06671)
*Mohammed Alsakabi,Kejia Hu,John M. Dolan,Ozan K. Tonguz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: JA-SIREN uses DST plus Jacobi-Anger expansion to deterministically initialize sinusoidal INRs, yielding much better PSNR and completely eliminating randomness across runs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing INR methods rely on stochastic initialization, which causes large performance variability across runs and undermines reproducibility. This is especially problematic in scientific computing and simulation, where stable and repeatable results are required.

Method: The paper proposes JA-SIREN, a deterministic initialization scheme for a two-layer sinusoidal MLP. It computes the Discrete Sine Transform (DST) of the target signal and uses the Jacobi-Anger expansion to derive closed-form network weights so that the initial spectral response analytically matches the target signal, without random seeds or extra hyperparameter tuning.

Result: On the Kodak dataset, JA-SIREN reaches a mean PSNR of 67.18 dB, improving by 21.30 dB over the best baseline, while achieving zero run-to-run variance.

Conclusion: JA-SIREN demonstrates that deterministic, spectrally-informed initialization can replace stochastic initialization in sinusoidal implicit neural representations, delivering both higher reconstruction quality and perfect run-to-run reproducibility.

Abstract: Existing implicit neural representation (INR) approaches suffer from stochastic initialization that does not guarantee consistent or high-quality performance across runs, with variations reaching more than 2.5 dB (78%) in image regression. This variation is problematic for scientific computing and simulation, where result reproducibility is crucial. To address this problem, we present Jacobi-Anger Sinusoidal Representation Network (JA-SIREN), a deterministic initialization scheme for sinusoidal networks grounded in classical spectral analysis. By computing the Discrete Sine Transform (DST) of the target signal and leveraging the Jacobi-Anger expansion, we derive closed-form weights for a two-layer sinusoidal MLP that analytically match the network's initial spectral response to the target signal, requiring no random seed or additional hyperparameter tuning. On the Kodak dataset, JA-SIREN achieves a mean PSNR of 67.18 dB, a 21.30 dB improvement over the best baseline. This is achieved with zero run-to-run variance, confirming that spectrally-informed initialization is a more effective and reproducible alternative to stochastic initialization for sinusoidal INRs.

</details>


### [15] [Adaptive Band Selection for Hyperspectral Classification with Spatially Disjoint Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06684)
*Ikram El-Hajri,Ouassim Karrakchou,Alejandro Mousist*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SGBR-HC first ranks hyperspectral bands, then uses that ranking to initialize trainable Hard-Concrete sparse gates. This avoids fragile initialization and fixed band-count constraints, and it delivers top accuracy with ~20 bands under stricter spatially disjoint testing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing differentiable band selection methods are sensitive to initialization, struggle when converting soft selections into a final discrete subset, and often require a pre-specified number of bands, reducing flexibility. The work aims to stabilize training and make band-count selection data-driven while enforcing more reliable evaluation.

Method: The paper proposes SGBR-HC. In Stage 1, candidate spectral bands are ranked using training pixels based on class discriminability and spectral diversity. This ranking initializes the logits of Hard-Concrete sparse gates. In Stage 2, the sparse gates are trained jointly with a spatial classifier, allowing training to determine how many bands are retained. The selected bands are then validated by retraining a fresh classifier using only those bands.

Result: Under spatially disjoint evaluation on Pavia University and Houston 2013, SGBR-HC achieves the best mean overall accuracy and Cohen's kappa with about 20 selected bands. Removing Stage 1 reduces OA by 8.84 percentage points on Pavia University and 22.15 points on Houston 2013. Using random pixel splits inflates OA on Pavia University by 30.56 points, revealing substantial spatial leakage.

Conclusion: SGBR-HC is an effective two-stage hyperspectral band selection method that uses supervised spectral ranking to initialize trainable sparse gates, yielding better and more flexible discrete band selection than differentiable selector baselines. Proper spatially disjoint evaluation is essential because random pixel splits can severely overestimate performance.

Abstract: Hyperspectral band selection methods based on differentiable selectors can be sensitive to initialization and to extracting a final discrete subset, while prescribed band counts limit flexibility. We propose SGBR-HC (Spectral-Group Band Ranking with Hard-Concrete initialization), a two-stage method that uses a supervised spectral ranking to initialize trainable sparse gates rather than treating ranking as a fixed selection rule, letting the number of selected bands be determined by training. Stage-1 scores candidate bands from training pixels by class discriminability and spectral diversity; this ranking seeds the gate logits for Stage-2, which trains the sparse gates jointly with a spatial classifier. Under spatially disjoint evaluation on Pavia University and Houston 2013, verified by retraining a fresh classifier on the selected bands, SGBR-HC achieves the highest mean overall accuracy and Cohen's kappa with approximately twenty bands. Bypassing Stage-1 degrades OA by 8.84 pp on Pavia University and 22.15 pp on Houston 2013, confirming the ranking prior's role. Random pixel splits inflate OA on Pavia University by 30.56 pp, underscoring spatial leakage as a critical evaluation confound.

</details>


### [16] [RPC-GS: Gaussian Splatting with native RPC Rendering for Satellite Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06690)
*Valentin Wagner,Sebastian Bullinger,Christoph Bodensteiner,Michael Arens*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RPC-GS is the first satellite Gaussian Splatting method to use RPC cameras directly instead of approximating them, yielding more accurate 3D reconstruction and lower altitude error on benchmark datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing satellite Gaussian Splatting methods simplify the imaging model by approximating RPC cameras with perspective or affine cameras, which introduces geometric inaccuracies in reconstruction. Since RPC is the standard model for modern pushbroom satellite sensors, a native treatment is needed to better preserve real sensor geometry.

Method: The paper proposes RPC-GS, a Gaussian Splatting framework that directly projects Gaussian means and covariances through Rational Polynomial Camera models. It embeds RPCs into a geo-coordinate transformation chain, derives a numerically robust Jacobian-based covariance projection for partially nonlinear transforms, and introduces a metric ray-based depth formulation to compensate for the lack of explicit camera depth in RPCs.

Result: In a unified benchmark comparing RPC, perspective, and affine camera models, the native RPC renderer achieves the lowest reconstruction error on major satellite datasets. It improves mean altitude error by 29.6% over perspective and 63.8% over affine on DFC2019, and by 9.9% over perspective and 37.9% over affine on IARPA2016.

Conclusion: A native RPC-based Gaussian Splatting pipeline is more faithful to satellite pushbroom imaging geometry than perspective or affine approximations, and it improves reconstruction accuracy consistently across benchmarks.

Abstract: We present RPC-GS, the first Gaussian Splatting framework for satellite imagery that operates natively with Rational Polynomial Camera (RPC) models. The RPC model is the de facto standard for representing the complex imaging geometry of modern pushbroom satellite sensors. To simplify rendering, prior satellite Gaussian Splatting methods replace the RPC model with perspective or affine camera approximations, leading to geometric errors during reconstruction. RPC-GS avoids these approximations by projecting Gaussian means and covariances directly through the RPC model during the splatting process. We embed the RPC model in a chain of carefully selected geo-coordinate transformations representing a mapping from splatting-suitable scene coordinates to image coordinates. To map the Gaussian covariance matrices, we derive a numerically robust Jacobian-based covariance projection for the (partially nonlinear) coordinate transformations. Since RPCs lack an explicit notion of camera depth, we integrate a metric ray-based depth formulation. We benchmark RPC, perspective, and affine camera models in a unified framework, with our native RPC renderer consistently achieving the lowest reconstruction error on leading satellite benchmark datasets, improving mean altitude error over perspective and affine approximations by 29.6% and 63.8% on DFC2019, and by 9.9% and 37.9% on IARPA2016. We release our code to support future research of Gaussian Splatting in the satellite imaging domain.

</details>


### [17] [S23DR 2026 Winning Solution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06695)
*Jan Skvrna,Miroslav Purkrabek,Lukas Neumann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A two-stage flow-matching DiT generates and refines 3D wireframe vertex sets from sparse SfM, depth, and semantic inputs, with consensus sampling improving stability; it won the S23DR 2026 challenge with HSS = 0.654.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to reconstruct structured 3D wireframes accurately from sparse and heterogeneous scene cues, where direct prediction is difficult due to incomplete geometry and ambiguity. The challenge setting demands a model that can fuse sparse SfM, fitted depth, and semantic signals into coherent global and refined structural predictions.

Method: The method models 3D wireframe vertices as a conditional set of 64 vertex tokens and denoises them using a flow-matching Diffusion Transformer conditioned on Perceiver-style scene tokens derived from sparse SfM, fitted depth, and semantic segmentations. It uses a global first pass for coarse reconstruction, a hull-cropped second pass for refinement, and a small multi-sample consensus step to stabilize stochastic sampling.

Result: The final system ranked first on the private leaderboard of the S23DR 2026 challenge and achieved an HSS score of 0.654.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a two-pass flow-matching DiT with conditional vertex-set generation and a consensus sampling step is highly effective for structured 3D wireframe reconstruction from sparse geometric and semantic inputs, achieving the top rank in the S23DR 2026 challenge.

Abstract: This text presents the winning solution to the S23DR 2026 challenge for structured 3D wireframe reconstruction from sparse SfM, fitted depth, and semantic segmentations. The method treats vertices as a conditional set and denoises 64 vertex tokens with a flow-matching DiT conditioned on Perceiver-style scene tokens. A global pass predicts the coarse structure, a hull-cropped second pass refines it, and a small multi-sample consensus step keeps the stochastic sampler well behaved. The final system ranked first on the private leaderboard, achievingHSS = 0.654.

</details>


### [18] [MMBU: A Massive Multi-modal Biomedical Understanding Benchmark to Probe the Perception Capabilities of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06696)
*Ryan D'Cunha,Alejandro Lozano,Xiaoxiao Sun,Daniel Vela Jarquin,Min Woo Sun,Josiah Aklilu,James Burgess,Yuhui Zhang,Ryan Nayebi,Paola Avila,Robayo,Jin Ye,Ming Hu,Zhongying Deng,Junjun He,Xin Chen,Yue Yao,Robert Tibshirani,Jeffrey J. Nirschl,Serena Yeung-Levy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MMBU是目前规模最大的生物医学视觉语言基准之一，用更广泛、更细粒度的任务检验VLM；结果显示，很多模型在传统基准上的高分并不代表其真正具备稳健的生物医学视觉理解能力。 / MMBU is the largest biomedical VLM benchmark to date, designed to test broader and finer-grained capabilities; results show that strong scores on traditional benchmarks do not necessarily indicate robust biomedical visual understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前生物医学VLM虽潜力巨大，但实际落地依赖强健且细粒度的视觉理解能力；而现有基准在模态覆盖、尺度多样性和评测粒度方面不足，难以暴露模型真实短板。 / Although biomedical VLMs are promising, practical deployment requires robust fine-grained visual understanding; existing benchmarks are limited in modality coverage, scale diversity, and evaluation granularity, making model weaknesses hard to detect.

Method: 提出MMBU这一大规模生物医学多模态理解基准，覆盖35个子模态及结构化元数据，包含开放式与封闭式的非定位分类、定位分类和目标检测任务；并用15个开源权重模型和2个前沿VLM进行系统评测。 / The paper introduces MMBU, a large-scale multimodal biomedical understanding benchmark covering 35 submodalities with structured metadata, including open and closed versions of ungrounded classification, grounded classification, and object detection, and evaluates 15 open-weight plus 2 frontier VLMs on it.

Result: 评测表明，面向医疗领域的适配对部分模型有可测提升，但整体上多种模型在更全面的MMBU评测下仍暴露出视觉感知和领域泛化缺陷。 / The evaluation shows that medical-domain adaptation yields measurable gains for some models, but many models still exhibit deficiencies in visual perception and domain generalization under the broader MMBU benchmark.

Conclusion: 现有生物医学视觉语言模型在既有基准上表现出的高准确率，可能掩盖其在细粒度视觉感知与跨域泛化方面的不足；MMBU提供了更系统、更严格的评测基础。 / Reported high accuracy on existing biomedical benchmarks can obscure substantial weaknesses in fine-grained visual perception and domain generalization; MMBU provides a more rigorous basis for evaluation.

Abstract: Vision and language models (VLMs) hold immense promise to transform biomedical imaging workflows, from detecting lesions in chest X-rays to profiling cellular features in microscopy. Realizing this potential, however, requires robust and fine-grained visual perception. Models need to correctly interpret subtle features in images, and they must do so across diverse biomedical modalities, scales, and contexts. Nevertheless, current benchmarks remain limited. To address these gaps, we introduce the Massive Multimodal Biomedical Understanding (MMBU) benchmark. It is the largest biomedical vision and language benchmark to date, covering 35 submodalities with rich structured metadata. It includes both open and closed versions of ungrounded classification, grounded classification, and object detection, enabling systematic evaluation of model performance across biological scales, clinical settings, and imaging modalities. Evaluating 15 open-weight and 2 frontier VLMs, we find that while medical adaptation provides measurable gains for some models, the high accuracy often reported on established benchmarks can mask deficiencies in visual perception and domain generalization.

</details>


### [19] [USU-Corn-WeedDB: A UAV RGB Image Dataset for Multi-Species Weed Detection in Forage Corn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06709)
*Utsav Bhandari,Saroj Burlakoti,Rhonda Miller,Sierra Young,Eric Westra,Aaron Etienne*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper releases a realistic public UAV weed dataset for forage corn and shows that modern object detectors, including lightweight YOLO models, perform well on it, making the dataset a useful benchmark for supervised and semi-supervised weed detection research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing site-specific weed management systems based on UAV imagery and deep learning are limited by the lack of field-representative public datasets, which restricts development and evaluation of robust weed detection methods in real forage corn production settings.

Method: The study collected UAV RGB imagery over a commercial forage corn field, tiled 366 high-resolution images into 8,800 patches, manually annotated 800 patches for three weed classes with bounding boxes, retained 8,000 unlabeled patches for semi-supervised learning, and benchmarked 28 object detection models across YOLO and RT-DETR families under identical training conditions without hyperparameter tuning.

Result: The dataset contains 10,539 annotated weed instances across three species and preserves real-world class imbalance. Across 28 tested detectors, test mAP@0.5 ranged from 0.773 to 0.840, and lightweight models achieved competitive performance relevant for UAV edge deployment.

Conclusion: USU-Corn-WeedDB is a publicly available UAV RGB weed-detection dataset for forage corn that is useful for benchmarking supervised and semi-supervised object detection models, with baseline results showing strong detection performance and practical suitability for lightweight edge deployment.

Abstract: Weed pressure in forage corn production causes yield losses of up to 31.5%, yet site-specific weed management (SSWM) systems built on UAV imagery and deep learning remain constrained by the scarcity of field-representative training datasets. We present USU-Corn-WeedDB, a publicly available UAV RGB image dataset collected from a commercial forage corn field in Cache Valley, Utah, designed to support multi-class weed detection under both supervised and semi-supervised learning frameworks. RGB imagery was acquired on 27 June 2025 using an Autel EVO II Dual 640T V2 drone at ~10m above ground level, yielding a ground sampling distance of approximately 0.48 cm/pixel. A total of 366 full-resolution images were tiled into 8,800 patches at 640 x 640-pixel resolution. Of these, 800 images were manually annotated for three weed species; common lambsquarters (Chenopodium album), redroot pigweed (Amaranthus retroflexus), and green foxtail (Setaria viridis) comprising 10,539 bounding-box instances, with the remaining 8,000 tiles retained as an unlabeled pool for semi-supervised experiments. This dataset reflects a natural class imbalance where redroot pigweed constitutes 53.86% of annotated instances, which was preserved intentionally to mirror real field conditions. To validate dataset utility, we trained 28 object detection models spanning five architecture families including YOLOv8, YOLOv9, YOLOv10, YOLO11, YOLO26, and RT-DETR under identical conditions without hyperparameter tuning. Test set mAP@0.5 ranged from 0.773 to 0.840, with lightweight models achieving competitive performance relevant to edge-deployed UAV systems. USU-Corn-WeedDB is publicly available at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.20044178.

</details>


### [20] [Anchored, Not Graded: Vision-Language Models Fail at Slant-from-Texture Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06714)
*Qian Zhang,Michal Golovanevsky,Fulvio Domini,James Tompkin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VLMs are poor at expressing continuous texture-based slant perception: they collapse judgments to a few discrete angle anchors, and fine-tuning only partially fixes this.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work found that unsupervised CNNs capture several human-like perceptual biases in slant-from-texture while supervised CNNs do not. The paper asks whether modern VLMs, despite strong high-level benchmark performance, can express similar low-level geometric perceptual competence.

Method: The paper evaluates multiple VLM families and scales on texture-based surface slant perception tasks using zero-shot, in-context prompting, and supervised fine-tuning, and compares their prediction patterns across variations in field of view, optical slant, and surface curvature.

Result: Across models and scales, both zero-shot and in-context prompting fail in a distinctive way: VLMs predict slant at a few preferred anchors such as 0 degrees, plus/minus 25 degrees, and plus/minus 45 degrees, with weak sensitivity to the actual geometric stimulus factors. Supervised fine-tuning improves performance somewhat but does not remove the anchoring effect.

Conclusion: Vision-Language Models do not naturally reproduce human-like graded surface-slant perception from texture; instead they show discretized anchor-based predictions, and even after supervised fine-tuning some anchoring remains, suggesting a representation-to-language readout failure rather than a total absence of geometric information.

Abstract: Human perception of surface slant from texture exhibits systematic, graded biases that emerge reliably in psychophysical experiments. Prior work showed that unsupervised CNNs reproduce several human-like biases, while supervised CNNs do not. Do Vision-Language Models (VLMs) exhibit similar competences? Across multiple VLM families and model scales, zero-shot and in-context prompting both produce distinctive failures: slant is predicted at only a small set of anchors (e.g., 0\degree, $\pm$25\degree, $\pm$45\degree) with little dependence on stimulus field of view, optical slant, or surface curvature. Supervised fine-tuning partially remediates the failure, but residual anchoring persists. While success in high-level vision-language benchmarks might not require sensitivity to low-level geometric cues, we interpret anchoring as a failure at the representation-to-output language interface: Not necessarily an absence of geometric encoding, but a failure to express it in a graded form.

</details>


### [21] [MedSIGHT: Towards Grounded Visual Comprehension in Medical Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06760)
*Aofei Chang,Le Huang,Alex James Boyd,Parminder Bhatia,Taha Kass-Hout,Fenglong Ma,Cao Xiao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedSIGHT lets a medical LLM reason about images and also point to exact regions by generating learned region codes that decode into segmentation masks, reaching SOTA on comprehension and segmentation with 72K training pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing medical large vision-language models perform well either on visual-language understanding or on medical image segmentation, but they do not effectively unify both capabilities. The authors aim to bridge semantic reasoning and pixel-level grounding so that clinical reasoning can connect textual interpretation with precise anatomical and pathological regions.

Method: The paper proposes MedSIGHT, which integrates three main components: a Region Perceiver that produces region-centric tokens with spatial information, a medical region codebook inserted into the LLM vocabulary so the model can emit discrete region codes, and a decoder pathway through the Region Perceiver to reconstruct segmentation masks. A progressive training strategy is used to align the Region Perceiver, codebook, and LLM stably.

Result: With training on only 72K multimodal instruction pairs, MedSIGHT reports state-of-the-art results across multiple imaging modalities on both medical comprehension and segmentation benchmarks.

Conclusion: MedSIGHT unifies medical vision-language comprehension and image segmentation within a single end-to-end framework by introducing region-centric symbolic grounding, and achieves strong performance with relatively limited instruction data.

Abstract: Medical large vision-language models (Med-LVLMs) have recently achieved remarkable progress in vision-language comprehension and medical image segmentation. However, existing models still struggle to unify these two capabilities, which is essential for achieving clinically reasoning that connects visual findings with semantic interpretation. We present MedSIGHT, a unified framework that equips Med-LVLMs with structured, pixel-level understanding for grounded visual comprehension. MedSIGHT introduces a novel Region Perceiver module that produces region-centric tokens, encoding spatial information directly into representation space of the language model. We further propose a medical region codebook into the LLM vocabulary, allowing the model to generate discrete region codes as symbolic representations of anatomical and pathological regions. These codes are decoded through the Region Perceiver to reconstruct segmentation mask, achieving end-to-end spatial grounding. Lastly, MedSIGHT combines Region Perceiver, Codebook and LLM using our proposed progressive training strategy to gradually aligns these modules stably. Trained on only 72K multimodal instruction pairs, MedSIGHT achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse imaging modalities on both medical comprehension and segmentation tasks.

</details>


### [22] [Breaking the Lock-in: Diversifying Text-to-Image Generation via Representation Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06813)
*Dahee Kwon,Haeun Lee,Jaesik Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper finds that text-to-image models become too similar across random seeds because an early low-frequency feature component quickly collapses; it introduces DAVE, a lightweight training-free fix that suppresses that component early to get more diverse images at almost no extra cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although modern Transformer-based and flow-based text-to-image models achieve strong alignment and image quality, they often generate overly similar images for the same prompt. Existing methods to improve diversity usually add expensive sampling or optimization overhead, motivating a cheaper and more direct solution based on the underlying cause of homogeneity.

Method: The authors analyze intermediate Transformer representations during generation, identify rapid cross-seed convergence of the DC component, and propose DAVE, a training-free representation-level intervention that attenuates the DC component in the early generation stage without changing the main sampling pipeline.

Result: DAVE improves prompt-consistent diversity while maintaining competitive visual quality, and does so with negligible computational overhead because it is training-free and minimally modifies the existing sampling process.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that early convergence of the zero-frequency spatial average (DC) component in Transformer features is a key cause of low sample diversity in text-to-image generation, and that selectively attenuating this component early in sampling can improve diversity with negligible overhead while preserving image quality.

Abstract: Recent text-to-image models built on large-scale Transformer backbones and flow-based objectives deliver strong text-image alignment and high visual quality, yet often produce overly similar samples under a fixed prompt. Existing diversity-enhancement methods alleviate this issue, but typically require expensive sampling or auxiliary optimization, incurring non-trivial overhead. To investigate the root cause of this homogeneity, we examine intermediate Transformer features and observe that the zero-frequency spatial average (DC) component rapidly converges across seeds early in generation, causing early trajectory lock-in that limits downstream variation. Building on this observation, we propose DC Attenuation for diVersity Enhancement (DAVE), a training-free representation-level intervention that selectively attenuates this component in the early regime. DAVE preserves the sampling pipeline with negligible overhead, improving prompt-consistent diversity while maintaining competitive image quality.

</details>


### [23] [VideoSEG-O3: A Multi-turn Reinforcement Learning Framework for Reasoning Video Object Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06819)
*Ming Dai,Sen Yang,Boqiang Duan,Boyuan Tong,Jiedong Zhuang,Wankou Yang,Jingdong Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VideoSEG-O3 brings multi-turn reinforcement learning to reasoning video object segmentation: it progressively inspects video segments and keyframes like a human coarse-to-fine process, uses segmentation-aware rewards/logit calibration, and trains on a new reasoning-trajectory dataset (VTS-CoT) to better handle complex long-video references.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing RVOS methods reason only from fixed initial inputs and cannot actively seek additional visual evidence, which makes them weak on long videos and complex references that require progressive temporal, spatial, and linguistic disambiguation.

Method: The paper proposes VideoSEG-O3, the first multi-turn RL framework for RVOS. It uses a multi-turn temporal-spatial chain-of-thought to iteratively select important intervals and keyframes, introduces SEG-aware logit calibration to inject pixel-wise segmentation feedback into token logits during RL, and employs a decoupled thinking trace that separates temporal, spatial, and linguistic reasoning. It also builds VTS-CoT, a cold-start dataset with detailed reasoning trajectories.

Result: From the abstract, the claimed result is that VideoSEG-O3 enables finer-grained and more effective RVOS through active evidence acquisition, better segmentation-aware policy learning, and structured reasoning traces. Specific quantitative numbers are not provided in the abstract.

Conclusion: VideoSEG-O3 demonstrates that multi-turn reinforcement learning with coarse-to-fine temporal-spatial reasoning can improve reasoning video object segmentation by actively gathering visual evidence and aligning token prediction with pixel-level segmentation quality.

Abstract: Reasoning Video Object Segmentation (RVOS) demands a sophisticated integration of temporal dynamics, spatial details, and linguistic reasoning to achieve precise pixel-level localization. Existing methods are limited to reasoning over fixed initial inputs and lack the capacity to actively acquire further visual evidence, which is often essential for resolving complex references in long or intricate videos. To address this, we propose \textbf{VideoSEG-O3}, the first multi-turn reinforcement learning framework for RVOS that emulates the human \textit{``coarse-to-fine''} cognitive process. It employs a \textit{multi-turn temporal-spatial chain-of-thought} to capture fine-grained details by iteratively pinpointing critical intervals and keyframes. Additionally, to enable the policy to perceive segmentation quality beyond mere text probability of \texttt{[SEG]} during the RL stage, we introduce \textit{SEG-aware logit calibration}, which integrates pixel-wise segmentation feedback directly into the token-level logits. Furthermore, we design a \textit{decoupled thinking trace} to hierarchically decompose the reasoning process into temporal, spatial, and linguistic dimensions, and construct \textbf{VTS-CoT}, a specialized cold-start dataset featuring comprehensive reasoning trajectories. The code and models will be released at https://github.com/Dmmm1997/VideoSEG-O3.

</details>


### [24] [AdaGRPO: A Capability-Aware Adaptive Enhancement for Flow-based GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06828)
*Jiazi Bu,Pengyang Ling,Yujie Zhou,Yibin Wang,Yuhang Zang,Tianyi Wei,Xiaohang Zhan,Jiaqi Wang,Tong Wu,Xingang Pan,Dahua Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AdaGRPO makes flow-model GRPO capability-aware by adaptively choosing training prompts and blending local plus global advantage signals, leading to stronger and more stable text-to-image preference alignment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing flow-based GRPO methods are decoupled from the learner's current capability. They randomly sample prompts, ignoring the importance of data selection in RL, and estimate sample quality only from intra-group statistics, which misses a global view of policy improvement. These blind spots reduce training effectiveness and stability.

Method: The paper proposes AdaGRPO, which has two main components: an Online Curriculum Filtering Strategy that dynamically selects prompts according to the model's current proficiency, and a Cross-Level Advantage Fusion mechanism that combines intra-group local advantages with global advantages for more reliable policy evaluation. It is designed as a lightweight plug-and-play module for frameworks such as Flow-GRPO, DanceGRPO, and Flow-CPS.

Result: Extensive experiments show that AdaGRPO consistently improves performance and significantly stabilizes GRPO training for text-to-image flow models across existing frameworks.

Conclusion: AdaGRPO improves text-to-image flow-model alignment by making GRPO capability-aware through adaptive prompt selection and fused advantage estimation, yielding better performance and more stable training while remaining easy to integrate into existing GRPO frameworks.

Abstract: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has demonstrated remarkable success in aligning text-to-image (T2I) flow models with human preferences. However, we have identified that the learning loop of current flow-based GRPO is fundamentally decoupled from the learner's current capability, suffering from critical blind spots at both prompt selection and advantage estimation: (i) Existing methods sample prompts randomly, overlooking the substantial impact of data selection on reinforcement learning (RL) efficacy--a factor proven crucial in GRPO for large language models; (ii) They evaluate sample quality solely relying on intra-group statistics, lacking a global perspective to accurately measure true policy improvement. To address these issues, we propose Adaptive GRPO (AdaGRPO), a novel capability-aware RL algorithm tailored for flow models. Specifically, AdaGRPO consists of two principal components: (i) Online Curriculum Filtering Strategy: Dynamically tracks the model's proficiency and adaptively selects prompts that best match its current learning boundary; (ii) Cross-Level Advantage Fusion: Synergistically integrates fine-grained intra-group advantages with macro-level global advantages, providing a comprehensive and unbiased policy evaluation. As a lightweight, plug-and-play module, AdaGRPO can be seamlessly integrated with existing frameworks such as Flow-GRPO, DanceGRPO, and Flow-CPS. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AdaGRPO consistently drives performance gains while significantly stabilizes GRPO training for flow models.

</details>


### [25] [CFRNet: Cycle-Consistent Fixed-Point Training for Real-Time Blind Face Restoration on Consumer Embedded NPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06850)
*Fuchen Li,Xinyang Wang,Yahui Zhang,Yuhan Chen,Jiahong Guo,Zhuohan Qin,Wenbo Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight 2.0M on-device face restorer is trained as a fixed-point operator, so repeated runs improve restoration quality without extra training changes; it outperforms retrained baselines under NPU deployment constraints and runs in real time on consumer hardware.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing high-quality blind face restoration methods rely on large generative priors and operators that are difficult to compile or quantize on small consumer NPUs, while lightweight CNN restorers are deployable but often produce over-smoothed faces and local artifacts. The work aims to close this deployment-quality gap.

Method: The paper proposes CFRNet, a 2.0M-parameter ResNet-style blind face restorer for 256x256 inputs, trained with Cycle-Consistent Fixed-Point Training (CCFP). CCFP includes progressive multi-cycle supervision, an idempotence loss, and a re-degradation cycle loss so the model behaves like a fixed-point operator under repeated application.
