Result: Under equal 256x256 deployment constraints, CFRNet achieves the best perceptual quality on a 300-image test set with LPIPS 0.250 at three cycles, and the best PSNR/SSIM at two cycles. It runs at about 23 ms per cycle in INT8 on a HiSilicon Hi3402 NPU, while the compared baselines cannot be compiled to that chip. The cycle count serves as a no-retraining quality knob.

Conclusion: CFRNet shows that fixed-point style training can make a small, NPU-friendly face restoration network improve quality across repeated cycles without adding inference cost, making it practical for real-time on-device deployment.

Abstract: Blind face restoration on consumer devices has to balance image quality against speed and memory. Strong methods such as GFPGAN and CodeFormer give good perceptual quality, but they rely on large pretrained generative priors and on operators such as attention, codebook lookup, and style modulation that are hard to compile and quantize on the small neural processing units (NPUs) used in consumer hardware. Small convolutional restorers run fast enough, but they tend to over-smooth and to leave artifacts around the eyes, nose, and mouth. We present CFRNet, a 2.0,M-parameter ResNet-style restorer for on-device use at $256\times256$, the common face-crop size on consumer NPUs. The main idea is Cycle-Consistent Fixed-Point Training (CCFP). Instead of training the network for one pass and then running it several times by hand, we train it to act as a fixed-point operator, so that applying it again to a restored face does not change the face. CCFP uses three training losses, namely progressive multi-cycle supervision, an idempotence loss, and a re-degradation cycle loss, and it adds no cost at inference. To compare fairly under our deployment limits, we retrain all baselines from scratch at the same $256\times256$ resolution. On a 300-image test set, CFRNet reaches the best perceptual score (LPIPS 0.250 at three cycles, which is 31% lower than one cycle) and also the best PSNR and SSIM at two cycles. It runs in about 23,ms per cycle in INT8 on a HiSilicon Hi3402 NPU, while the same baselines cannot be compiled to that chip. The cycle count $k$ acts as a simple quality knob that needs no retraining: PSNR is best at $k\!=\!2$ and LPIPS keeps improving up to $k\!=\!3$. We further show that the same idea works with a plain CNN that is even easier to deploy, and we run the model in real time on an in-car driver-monitoring board.

</details>


### [26] [MotionEnhancer: Leveraging Video Diffusion for Motion-Enhanced Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06853)
*Yifan Xu,Chao Zhang,Ruifei Ma,Fei Gao,Zhifei Yang,Jiaxing Qi,Zhipeng Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TL;DR：作者把视频扩散模型擅长的‘运动感知’通过无参数注意力对齐迁移到视觉语言模型中，从而以低开销方式提升视频细粒度运动理解。 / TL;DR: The paper transfers motion-awareness from a video diffusion model into a VLM through parameter-free attention alignment, improving fine-grained video motion understanding with minimal overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：现有 VLM 更擅长高层事件或故事理解，但对细粒度运动细节建模不足；而 VDM 在动态运动模式建模上更强，因此作者希望将 VDM 的运动先验迁移给 VLM。 / The motivation is that existing VLMs are strong at high-level event or story understanding but weak at capturing fine-grained motion details, while VDMs are naturally better at modeling dynamic motion patterns, motivating transfer of motion priors from VDMs to VLMs.

Method: 方法上，MotionEnhancer利用视频扩散模型中的运动相关注意力作为辅助监督，包含两个无参数模块：Motion-sensitive Head Selection (MHS) 用于选择对运动敏感的注意力头，Motion-salient Text Token Identification (MTTI) 用于识别与运动最相关的文本 token，并通过注意力对齐增强 VLM 的运动理解。 / Methodologically, MotionEnhancer uses motion-related attentions from a video diffusion model as auxiliary supervision. It includes two parameter-free modules: Motion-sensitive Head Selection (MHS) to select motion-sensitive attention heads, and Motion-salient Text Token Identification (MTTI) to identify motion-relevant text tokens, then enhances VLM motion understanding through attention alignment.

Result: 实验结果表明，MotionEnhancer 在两个运动级视频理解基准上持续优于当前先进 VLM，尤其在与运动相关的评测指标上提升更明显。 / Experimental results show that MotionEnhancer consistently improves state-of-the-art VLMs on two motion-level video understanding benchmarks, with especially strong gains on motion-related metrics.

Conclusion: 该论文提出 MotionEnhancer，通过从视频扩散模型蒸馏运动先验，并以注意力对齐方式监督视觉语言模型，在不增加训练参数、不修改架构的前提下，显著提升了细粒度运动理解能力。 / The paper concludes that MotionEnhancer improves fine-grained motion understanding in VLMs by distilling motion priors from a video diffusion model and aligning attention, without adding training parameters or changing model architectures.

Abstract: The new era has witnessed a remarkable capability to extend Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for tackling tasks of video understanding. While current VLMs excel at event- or story-level understanding, their ability to capture fine-grained motion details remains limited, primarily due to their focus on high-level static semantic structures and macro-event logic. In contrast, Video Diffusion Models (VDMs) are adept at modeling dynamic motion patterns, benefiting from large-scale video data and the intrinsic requirement of temporal generation. In this paper, we introduce MotionEnhancer, a novel approach that leverages motion priors distilled from a powerful video diffusion model as auxiliary supervision to enhance the motion understanding capability of a VLM via attention alignment. MotionEnhancer comprises two simple parameter-free modules, Motion-sensitive Head Selection (MHS) and Motion-salient Text Token Identification (MTTI), to directly extract and optimize motion-related attentions from the VDM in a computation-only manner. MotionEnhancer provides a scalable solution for motion understanding without additional training parameters, modifications to existing architectures, or tool calling. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MotionEnhancer can achieve consistent improvements over state-of-the-art VLMs on two motion-level video understanding benchmarks, especially on motion-related metrics.

</details>


### [27] [FS-DVS: A Frequency-Selective Dynamic Visual Sensing Paradigm for Enhancing Information Completeness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06856)
*Feiyu Ji,Xiaokang Yang,Xiaoyun Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FS-DVS adds a learnable retina-like spatial filter before DVS event generation, making the sensor selectively sensitive to mid spatial frequencies like human vision and improving task performance with strong noise robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional DVS trigger events independently at each pixel and therefore miss the spatial integration behavior of retinal ganglion cells. This causes them to lack a contrast sensitivity function and lose sub-threshold but informative signals, especially around mid-spatial frequencies.

Method: The paper proposes FS-DVS, which places a learnable spatial filter before the event triggering stage of a dynamic vision sensor. Using a differentiable event simulation framework, the filter is optimized end-to-end for downstream tasks such as object detection and action recognition.

Result: Learned filters initialized from a delta function consistently evolve into center-surround patterns that emphasize mid-frequency components, matching human CSF tendencies. The method yields clear gains on object detection and action recognition and shows better selective enhancement and noise robustness than simply increasing sensitivity or relying on post-processing.

Conclusion: FS-DVS demonstrates that inserting a learnable spatial filter before event triggering enables DVS to recover biologically inspired mid-spatial-frequency selectivity, improving downstream vision performance while remaining robust to noise.

Abstract: Dynamic vision sensors (DVS) offer exceptional temporal resolution and dynamic range by asynchronously reporting pixel-level intensity changes. However, conventional DVS rely on a per-pixel independent triggering mechanism, ignoring the spatial integration performed by biological retinal ganglion cells (RGCs). Consequently, they lack the contrast sensitivity function (CSF) and its inherent sensitivity to mid-spatial frequencies, which inevitably leads to information incompleteness due to sub-threshold signal loss. To bridge this gap, we propose FS-DVS (Frequency-Selective Dynamic Vision Sensor), a novel paradigm that integrates a learnable spatial filter strictly preceding the event triggering process to mimic the RGC aggregation mechanism. By developing a differentiable event simulation framework, the spatial filter can be optimized end-to-end with downstream tasks. Our study reveals that starting from a delta function, the learned spatial filters spontaneously evolve into center-surround patterns that emphasize mid-frequency components, consistently aligning with human CSF. Beyond achieving substantial performance gains in object detection and action recognition, the consistent convergence to human-like CSF characteristics across different tasks underscores the universality of this mid-frequency selective mechanism. Compared to naively increasing sensor sensitivity or relying on post-processing, our paradigm achieves selective information enhancement with high noise resilience, providing a robust, biologically plausible blueprint for next-generation neuromorphic sensors.

</details>


### [28] [LRMIL: Efficient Low-Resolution Multiple Instance Learning via High-Resolution Knowledge Distillation for Whole Slide Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06864)
*Yonghan Shin,Won-Ki Jeong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 提出LRMIL：把高分辨率WSI知识蒸馏到低分辨率MIL模型中，训练时借助高分辨率教师，测试时只看低分辨率patch，结果更准且更省算力。 / LRMIL distills high-resolution WSI knowledge into a low-resolution MIL model: train with a high-resolution teacher, test using only low-resolution patches, and get better accuracy with lower compute.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于现有WSI-MIL方法通常依赖大量高分辨率patch，现实临床中存在两类问题：难以建模低倍率下的全局视觉线索，以及高分辨率patch数量巨大导致预处理和推理计算开销过高。 / The motivation is that existing WSI MIL methods depend on exhaustive high-resolution patch extraction, which in real clinical settings both misses global low-magnification visual cues and incurs heavy preprocessing and computational cost due to the huge number of high-resolution patches.

Method: 方法提出了一个低分辨率多实例学习框架LRMIL，包含两阶段蒸馏：1）patch级跨分辨率蒸馏，使低分辨率patch嵌入对齐高分辨率表示；2）slide级知识蒸馏，在slide标签监督和教师模型引导下训练低分辨率学生MIL模型。推理阶段仅使用低分辨率patch。 / The paper proposes LRMIL, a low-resolution MIL framework with two-stage distillation: (1) patch-level cross-resolution distillation to align low-resolution patch embeddings with high-resolution representations, and (2) slide-level knowledge distillation to train a low-resolution student MIL model under slide-level supervision and teacher guidance. Inference uses only low-resolution patches.

Result: 在多个WSI基准上的广泛实验表明，LRMIL持续优于现有最先进MIL方法，并实现更高效的推理。 / Extensive experiments on multiple WSI benchmarks show that LRMIL consistently outperforms state-of-the-art MIL methods while delivering more efficient inference.

Conclusion: LRMIL通过两阶段蒸馏将高分辨率知识迁移到低分辨率表示，在仅使用低分辨率patch推理的情况下，既提升了WSI分析性能，又显著降低了预处理与计算成本，是临床数字病理中可扩展且实用的方案。 / LRMIL transfers high-resolution knowledge to low-resolution representations via a two-stage distillation scheme, enabling inference with only low-resolution patches while improving WSI performance and substantially reducing preprocessing and computation, making it a practical and scalable clinical pathology solution.

Abstract: Multiple instance learning (MIL) has become a standard paradigm for whole slide image (WSI) analysis in digital pathology, as it enables slide-level prediction without dense annotations. Existing MIL methods typically rely on exhaustive extraction and encoding of high-resolution patches. However, this practice suffers from two critical limitations in real-world clinical settings: it struggles to capture global visual cues at lower magnifications, and incurs substantial computational overhead due to the massive number of high-resolution patches per slide. To address these limitations, we propose an efficient low-resolution multiple instance learning (LRMIL) framework that transfers high-resolution knowledge to low-resolution representations. LRMIL adopts a two-stage distillation strategy. First, patch-level cross-resolution distillation aligns low-resolution patch embeddings with high-resolution representations. Second, slide-level knowledge distillation trains a low-resolution student MIL model under both slide-level supervision and teacher guidance. At inference time, LRMIL operates exclusively on low-resolution patches, substantially reducing data preprocessing and computational cost. Extensive experiments on multiple WSI benchmarks demonstrate that LRMIL consistently outperforms state-of-the-art MIL methods while achieving more efficient inference. These results highlight LRMIL as a practical and scalable solution for WSI analysis in clinical pathology.

</details>


### [29] [Multi-FRuGaL: Multimodal Flexible Redundancy-aware Decomposed Gated Learning for Cancer Diagnosis and Prognosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06867)
*Sanket Kachole,Siddhesh Thakur,Shubham Innani,Sanyukta Adap,Suhang You,Carla Pitarch-Abaigar,Spyridon Bakas*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Multi-FRuGaL tackles missing-modality medical data by separating redundant from complementary signals and adaptively weighting available modalities, leading to strong gains on head and neck cancer prediction tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard multimodal fusion methods assume complete data, but real-world medical datasets are heterogeneous and frequently missing one or more modalities, which reduces the effectiveness of conventional approaches.

Method: The paper proposes Multi-FRuGaL, an adaptive gated intermediate-fusion framework with per-modality encoders, a signal decomposition layer, an input-conditioned gating network, and an information-aware fusion objective to disentangle redundant and complementary modality information and fuse available modalities under missing data.

Result: On HANCOCK and HECKTOR, Multi-FRuGaL improves AUC from 0.601 to 0.8496 for survival, from 0.672 to 0.8102 for recurrence, and reaches 0.975 AUC for HPV prediction. It also achieves concordance indices of 0.6814 for overall survival, 0.7421 for recurrence-free survival, 0.7143 for progression-free survival on HANCOCK, and 0.7203 for recurrence-free survival on HECKTOR.

Conclusion: Multi-FRuGaL is an effective and robust multimodal learning framework for incomplete clinical data, consistently outperforming baseline methods across prognosis and classification tasks while remaining reliable under severe missing-modality settings.

Abstract: Modern medicine relies on heterogeneous data sources spanning radiology, pathology, text reports, and structured clinical information. However, real-world patient data are frequently incomplete, with missing or sparsely acquired modalities, limiting the effectiveness of standard multimodal fusion approaches. To this end, we propose the Multimodal Flexible Redundancy-aware decomposed GAted Learning (Multi-FRuGaL) framework, a decomposition-aware, adaptive gated intermediate-fusion framework that performs modality-level representation learning under missing data. Multi-FRuGaL integrates per-modality encoders with a signal decomposition layer, an input-conditioned gating network, and an information-aware fusion objective to separate redundant from modality-specific complementary signals, selectively upweighting informative modalities and suppressing redundant or noisy inputs, and remaining well-defined even when multiple modalities are absent. We evaluate Multi-FRuGaL on two multimodal head and neck cancer cohorts: the HANCOCK challenge dataset (N = 763) comprising five modalities and two prognostic endpoints (5-year survival and 2-year recurrence), and the HECKTOR challenge dataset (N = 588) comprising three modalities for human papillomavirus (HPV) status classification. Multi-FRuGaL consistently achieves higher mean performance than the evaluated baselines across multiple tasks, improving AUC from 0.601 to 0.8496 for survival, from 0.672 to 0.8102 for recurrence, and achieving 0.975 AUC for HPV prediction on HECKTOR. For survival analysis, it further achieves a concordance index of 0.6814 for overall survival, 0.7421 for recurrence-free survival, and 0.7143 for progression-free survival on HANCOCK, and 0.7203 for recurrence-free survival on HECKTOR. Qualitative analyses further show that Multi-FRuGaL learns discriminative and robust multimodal representations, even under severe missing-modality conditions.

</details>


### [30] [EgoPressDiff: Multimodal Video Diffusion for Egocentric UV-Domain Hand-Pressure Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06872)
*Yuan Zeng,Zilue Gao,Yujia Shi,Zongqing Lu,Wenming Yang,QingMin Liao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A video diffusion model uses hand pose, 3D mesh vertices, and depth to generate temporally consistent UV pressure maps from egocentric video, clearly outperforming prior hand-pressure estimation methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing hand-pressure estimation methods typically discretize pressure signals and process frames independently, which causes quantization errors and temporal inconsistency. The work is motivated by the need for accurate, temporally coherent, and physically grounded pressure estimation for AR/VR, robotic imitation, and ergonomics.

Method: The paper proposes EgoPressDiff, a conditional video diffusion framework that generates UV-pressure maps from visual input. It uses multimodal conditioning with a PoseNet for hand pose features, a Vertex Encoder for 3D hand mesh vertex features, and depth cues. It also introduces a Distribution-Calibrated Spatial Layer to align the statistics of heterogeneous features before fusing them during generation.

Result: On the EgoPressure egocentric benchmark, EgoPressDiff achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving Volumetric IoU by more than 34% relative to the previous baseline, while also reducing MAE and preserving strong temporal accuracy.

Conclusion: EgoPressDiff demonstrates that a conditional video diffusion model with multimodal physical conditioning can estimate hand-surface contact pressure from egocentric video more accurately and more consistently over time than prior methods.

Abstract: Estimating hand-surface contact pressure from an egocentric view is crucial for AR/VR devices, robotic imitation, and ergonomic analysis. Existing methods often discretize pressure signal and process frames independently, leading to quantization errors and temporal inconsistencies. We present \emph{EgoPressDiff}, a conditional video diffusion framework that generates UV-pressure maps from visual input. The core of our approach is a multi-modal conditioning strategy, introducing a PoseNet and a Vertex Encoder to efficiently extract features from hand pose and 3D mesh vertices. These signals, along with depth information, guide the generative process to ensure the pressure fields are physically grounded. To effectively fuse these heterogeneous features, we further propose a Distribution-Calibrated Spatial Layer, which aligns their statistical properties before combination. Evaluated on the EgoPressure ego-view setting, EgoPressDiff achieves state-of-the-art results, improving Volumetric IoU by over 34\% relative to prior baseline, while reducing MAE and maintaining high temporal accuracy. Our project page is at https://egopressdiff.github.io/.

</details>


### [31] [Unified Safe In-context Image Generation in Multimodal Diffusion Transformers via Restricting Unsafe Information Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06875)
*Xiang Yang,Feifei Li,Mi Zhang,Geng Hong,Xiaoyu You,Mi Wen,Min Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UVR is a training-free safety layer for DiT image generators that finds where unsafe content first appears in attention maps and suppresses its spread, delivering strong safety in both generation and editing with little quality drop.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing safety methods mainly target text-to-image generation or U-Net-based models, making them insufficient for unified safety control in DiT-based frameworks, especially for image-to-image editing. The paper aims to provide an architecture-compatible and task-unified safety mitigation method for modern DiT generators.

Method: The paper analyzes multimodal attention as information flow and identifies a task-independent start-up stage where unsafe semantics first emerge in output patches, followed by task-specific amplification and interference stages. Based on this, UVR performs targeted attention modulation and explicitly restricts harmful information flow over localized unsafe output patches during generation.

Result: Across multiple unsafe concepts, UVR achieves state-of-the-art safety performance, reporting 91% erase rate for image synthesis and 77% erase rate for image editing, with only minimal loss in visual quality and fidelity.

Conclusion: UVR is a training-free, unified safety regulation framework for DiT-based multimodal attention image generation that effectively suppresses unsafe semantics in both text-to-image synthesis and image-to-image editing while largely preserving image quality and fidelity.

Abstract: Diffusion transformers (DiTs) equipped with multimodal attention (MM-Attn) have become a dominant paradigm for image generation. However, preventing the generation of harmful content remains a critical challenge, particularly in image-to-image (I2I) editing tasks. Existing safety mechanisms are primarily designed for text-to-image (T2I) synthesis or U-Net-based architectures, which limits their effectiveness for unified safety mitigation in DiT-based frameworks. To bridge this gap, we propose Unified Visual Safety Regulator (UVR), a training-free safe generation framework that regulates unsafe semantics in generated images. UVR is grounded in an analysis of attention dynamics from the perspective of information flow in MM-Attn. We identify a task-independent start-up stage, during which unsafe semantics in output patches rapidly emerge and can be accurately localized, followed by task-specific semantic amplification and interference stages, where harmful signals are further propagated and entangled with benign content. Based on these observations, UVR mitigates unsafe generation through unified, targeted attention modulation and explicit restriction of harmful information flow over the identified unsafe output patches. Experiments across various concepts show that UVR achieves state-of-the-art safety performance by achieving 91% and 77% erase rate in image synthesis and editing tasks, while preserving visual quality and fidelity with minimal degradation. Code is available at https://github.com/deng12yx/UVR.

</details>


### [32] [FreeAnimate: Training-Free Human Image Animation with Preview-Guided Denoising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06885)
*Yuan Zeng,Yujia Shi,Zongqing Lu,QingMin Liao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FreeAnimate uses pretrained diffusion models for human image animation without extra training, adding preview-based guidance and attention mechanisms to keep motion, identity, and background consistent, and it performs competitively with top trained methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing human image animation methods, especially diffusion-based ones, usually require large-scale training data and heavy computational resources, which restrict accessibility, efficiency, and cross-dataset generalization. The paper aims to remove the training requirement while retaining high generation quality.

Method: The method introduces FreeAnimate, which uses a preview generation strategy to produce temporal and structural priors from preview frames for pose alignment and background consistency, and adds Inversion-Boosted Attention plus Reference-Anchored Self-Attention to improve temporal coherence and identity preservation without any additional training.

Result: Experiments show that FreeAnimate surpasses existing training-free competitors and some training-based baselines, produces quality comparable to state-of-the-art methods, and demonstrates robust generalization on diverse datasets.

Conclusion: FreeAnimate is a training-free human image animation framework that can achieve strong temporal consistency, identity preservation, and background stability by exploiting pretrained image diffusion models, reaching quality comparable to state-of-the-art methods while generalizing robustly across datasets.

Abstract: Human Image Animation has seen significant advancements, primarily driven by diffusion models. However, existing methods typically demand substantial training data and resources to achieve high-quality results, limiting generalization and accessibility. In this work, we introduce \emph{FreeAnimate}, a training-free framework that leverages the inherent capabilities of image diffusion models to enable temporal consistency, identity preservation, and background stability. Our approach incorporates a novel preview generation strategy that provides temporal and structural priors from generated preview frames, effectively guiding pose alignment and background consistency without training. Additionally, FreeAnimate introduces Inversion-Boosted Attention and Reference-Anchored Self-Attention modules to guarantee temporal consistency and identity preservation. Experimental results demonstrate that FreeAnimate outperforms existing training-free competitors and training-based baseline methods, achieving generation quality comparable to state-of-the-art methods and offering robust generalization across diverse datasets. Our project page is at https://freeani.github.io/.

</details>


### [33] [ARAPDiffusion: ARAP Regularization for Diffusion-Based Deformable Shape Space Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06887)
*Haibo Liu,Jinghan Ke,Haitao Yang,Xiangru Huang,Georgios Pavlakos,Qixing Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ARAPDiffusion adds ARAP deformation regularization to latent diffusion so 3D shape generators can learn better continuous shape spaces from less data, and experiments show it beats baselines on unconditional and conditional generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard latent diffusion models for 3D shape generation typically require abundant training data and may not effectively exploit geometric deformation structure. The paper is motivated by the need to learn continuous deformation-aware shape spaces from limited 3D data by incorporating a strong geometric prior.

Method: The paper proposes a latent diffusion framework regularized by ARAP losses. ARAP priors are used to improve both the encoder/decoder and the diffusion model through an alternating training scheme: the diffusion-generated synthetic distribution regularizes the autoencoder, and the decoder-induced shape prior regularizes the diffusion model. It also combines a representation-free latent diffusion process with an implicit shape decoder for unorganized point clouds.

Result: Experiments on unconditional and conditional shape generation show that ARAPDiffusion outperforms baseline methods, indicating better generative performance and applicability to point clouds.

Conclusion: ARAPDiffusion demonstrates that injecting as-rigid-as-possible deformation priors into latent diffusion can improve 3D shape generation quality and data efficiency, including for point-cloud settings with limited training data.

Abstract: This paper introduces ARAPDiffusion, a latent diffusion model to learn the underlying continuous shape space of a deformation shape collection. The key innovation is in injecting the as-rigid-as-possible (ARAP) deformation model as regularization losses into latent diffusion (LD), releasing the requirement of having abundant 3D training data for learning generative models. In contrast to the standard LD, we show how the ARAP model can be used to improve both the encoder/decoder and the LD model. The training procedure alternates between using the synthetic distribution defined by the LD model to develop a regularization loss that enhances the shape encoder/decoder and using the shape decoder to develop a regularization loss to improve the LD model. We also show the benefit of the LD paradigm in combining a representation-free LD process and an implicit shape decoder that is applicable to unorganized point clouds. The experimental results of unconditional and conditional shape generation demonstrate the advantages of ARAPDiffusion over baseline approaches.

</details>


### [34] [Diagnosing Visual Ignorance in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06890)
*Runyu Zhou,Qi Zhang,Qixun Wang,Yisen Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper shows that many VLM errors are not just dataset artifacts but come from an internal routing failure where language biases override visual evidence. It also shows that popular VQA benchmarks often fail to test real image grounding, because models can still answer many questions even when the image is almost or completely destroyed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although VLMs are known to rely on language priors and sometimes answer confidently without strong visual grounding, the internal cause of this behavior and its effect on benchmark validity are not well understood. The paper aims to explain where this failure happens inside the model and to quantify how much current benchmarks reward answers that do not truly depend on visual evidence.

Method: The paper studies the problem from two angles. Mechanistically, it uses counterfactual layer replacement and supervised layer-wise MLP probing to track the competition between ground-truth visual semantics and language-prior semantics across decoder layers. Behaviorally, it proposes a progressive visual decay metric using multi-step Gaussian blurring to test whether model answers remain unchanged as visual information is gradually destroyed, and evaluates this across 12 VQA benchmarks and 3 representative VLMs.

Result: The analysis finds a multi-stage bottleneck in the language decoder: intermediate layers often fail to retrieve visual semantics effectively, and later layers may suppress the remaining visual signals in favor of textual bias. On the evaluation side, a substantial fraction of benchmark examples remain answerable under severe or even total visual degradation across multiple VLMs, showing that current benchmarks can unintentionally reward visual ignorance.

Conclusion: Language-prior reliance in VLMs is a systematic routing failure: visual evidence is weakly retrieved in intermediate decoder layers and can be further suppressed by later layers, while existing VQA benchmarks often overestimate true visual grounding because many examples remain answerable even when images are heavily blurred or fully obfuscated.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) frequently rely on language priors, producing confident answers that are weakly grounded in visual evidence. While this behavior is widely observed, its internal mechanisms and its impact on benchmark evaluation remain insufficiently understood. In this work, we study language-prior reliance from both mechanistic and behavioral perspectives. Internally, we combine counterfactual layer replacement with supervised layer-wise MLP probing to trace how ground-truth visual semantics and language-prior semantics compete across the language decoder. Our analysis reveals a multi-stage bottleneck: intermediate layers often fail to effectively retrieve visual information, while later layers can further suppress surviving visual signals in favor of text-space biases. Externally, we introduce a progressive visual decay metric based on multi-step Gaussian blurring, which identifies instances whose answers remain invariant even as visual content is increasingly destroyed. Across twelve visual question-answering benchmarks and three representative VLMs, we find that a substantial fraction of examples remain answerable under severe or total visual obfuscation, indicating that current benchmarks can inadvertently reward visual ignorance. These findings demonstrate that language-prior reliance is a systematic routing failure affecting both model internals and benchmark validity. Finally, we outline critical pathways for future research, highlighting the necessity of designing training distributions and evaluation protocols built on structurally isolated or counterfactual data to enforce genuine cross-modal grounding.

</details>


### [35] [Stream3D-VLM: Online 3D Spatial Understanding with Incremental Geometry Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06891)
*Hanxun Yu,Xuan Qu,Lei Ke,Boqiang Zhang,Yuxin Wang,Jianke Zhu,Dong Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: An online 3D VLM for streaming video combines response-timing control, incremental geometry integration, and adaptive token compression, plus large-scale generated training data, to achieve state-of-the-art real-time 3D spatial understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D large multimodal models mainly work in offline settings and require complete scene observations or predefined video clips, which limits real-time spatial understanding from streaming video. The paper is motivated by the need for online 3D vision-language understanding and by the scarcity of streaming 3D-language training data.

Method: The method introduces an online 3D VLM with three main components: autoregressive streaming control modeling based on next-token prediction to decide when to respond, a lightweight Visual-Spatial Feature Integration (VSFI) module to inject temporally aligned geometry priors into the visual stream incrementally, and a plug-and-play Geometry-Adaptive Voxel Compression (GAVC) module to compress visual tokens efficiently for long-context decoding.

Result: The authors build a scalable data generation pipeline with over 1M online spatio-temporal 3D QA pairs and a benchmark covering 29 tasks. Experiments show substantial gains over proprietary and open-source models across online and offline 3D spatial understanding, reasoning, and grounding tasks.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that an online 3D vision-language model can achieve real-time spatial understanding from streaming video and significantly outperform both proprietary and open-source baselines on online and offline 3D spatial understanding, reasoning, and grounding tasks.

Abstract: Despite advances in 3D scene understanding, existing 3D Large Multimodal Models operate in offline settings, requiring complete scene observations or predefined video clips. In this paper, we present an online 3D vision-language model that enables real-time spatial understanding from streaming video. Our approach adopts an autoregressive streaming control modeling based on the LLM's next-token prediction objective to learn when to respond, and employs a lightweight Visual-Spatial Feature Integration (VSFI) module to incrementally inject temporally aligned geometry priors into the visual stream. To alleviate long-context decoding overhead, we propose a plug-and-play Geometry-Adaptive Voxel Compression (GAVC) module for efficient visual token compression. To address the scarcity of streaming 3D-language data, we further develop a scalable data generation pipeline that curates over 1M online spatio-temporal 3D QA pairs and establishes a comprehensive benchmark spanning 29 tasks. Extensive experiments show that our approach significantly outperforms both proprietary and open-source models across online and offline 3D spatial understanding, reasoning, and grounding tasks. The project page is available at https://stream3d-vlm.github.io/

</details>


### [36] [Lighting-Aware Representation Learning under Controllable Lighting Variation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06899)
*Lizhen Zhu,Charantej Reddy Pochimireddy,James Z Wang,Brad Wyble*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Treat lighting as a learning signal, not just noise: adding a lighting-aware auxiliary objective to contrastive learning yields better and more robust visual representations across multiple benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that illumination variation causes major appearance shifts and remains a core obstacle in visual representation learning. Existing augmentation-based approaches mainly suppress lighting effects as nuisance factors, but they do not explicitly model lighting information during learning.

Method: The method proposes a lighting-aware representation learning framework that extends contrastive learning with an auxiliary objective. This auxiliary task captures illumination-dependent variation in rendered scenes, so the model jointly learns semantically consistent features while remaining sensitive to lighting-related visual structure.

Result: On ImageNet, ExDark, and PASCAL VOC for image classification and object detection, the proposed training consistently outperforms standard contrastive learning baselines under the same architecture and training budget. It also shows promising results in supervised learning and simpler lighting-variation settings.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that explicitly modeling illumination during representation learning improves robustness and downstream performance over standard contrastive baselines without changing model architecture or training budget, and the approach generalizes beyond complex lighting scenarios to supervised settings and simpler illumination variations.

Abstract: Variations in illumination remain a major challenge for visual representation learning, as they induce substantial appearance changes both across and within environments. While existing approaches typically address this issue through data augmentations that encourage models to become invariant to lighting changes, such strategies do not explicitly model lighting information during learning. Inspired by theories of human vision, we propose a lighting-aware representation learning framework that incorporates illumination variation as an explicit training signal rather than a nuisance factor to be suppressed. Our method extends contrastive learning by introducing an auxiliary objective that captures illumination-dependent variation in rendered scenes, enabling the model to jointly learn representations that preserve semantic consistency while remaining sensitive to lighting-dependent visual structure. We evaluate the proposed model on image classification and object detection tasks across the ImageNet, ExDark, and PASCAL VOC benchmarks. Results demonstrate that the proposed lighting-aware training consistently improves downstream performance over standard contrastive learning baselines, while maintaining the same architecture and training budget. Furthermore, our approach shows promising performance in supervised learning frameworks and under settings involving simpler lighting variation, suggesting broad applicability beyond complex illumination scenarios. These results indicate its potential to enhance model robustness and adaptability in complex visual environments as well as in more conventional image processing tasks.

</details>


### [37] [LUCID: Learning Unified Control for Image Deflaring and Exposure Mastery in Nighttime Photography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06901)
*Tingyu Yang,Yuan Cheng,Xiaoyun Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LUCID is a controllable diffusion-based framework for nighttime image restoration that jointly removes flare, reconstructs structure, and adjusts exposure, achieving better results than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing nighttime restoration methods typically treat degradations such as flare and low-light noise separately, even though these effects are strongly entangled in real nighttime scenes. This limits restoration quality and user control.

Method: The paper proposes LUCID, a unified nighttime restoration framework with two cooperative parts: a flare disentanglement module for recovering scene structure and a diffusion-based reconstruction module that uses generative priors to restore clean, well-exposed images. It also introduces a four-mode training strategy and classifier-free guidance for controllable restoration, including selective light-source/flare handling and continuous exposure control for HDR reconstruction.

Result: Extensive experiments on diverse real-world nighttime scenes show that LUCID consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods and provides flexible user control over restoration behavior.

Conclusion: LUCID shows that nighttime image restoration is more effective when flare removal, denoising, exposure recovery, and controllability are handled within a single unified framework rather than as isolated tasks.

Abstract: Photography is the art of painting with light, yet nighttime scenes are shaped by competing degradations: intense flares obscure scene structure, while photon-limited regions collapse into noise. Conventional approaches address these factors in isolation, overlooking the fact that these degradations are fundamentally entangled. To bridge this gap, we introduce LUCID, a unified framework that reframes nighttime restoration as a continuous and controllable process rather than a fixed correction. We decompose nighttime restoration into two cooperative components: a flare disentanglement module that lifts the 'curtain' of optical artifacts to provide reliable structural guidance, and a diffusion-driven module that leverages generative priors to reconstruct clean and well-exposed imagery. Crucially, LUCID introduces explicit controllability through a novel four-mode training strategy, enabling users to steer the restoration process via classifier-free guidance (CFG) and allowing selective control over light sources and their associated flare and ghosting artifacts, while also supporting high dynamic range (HDR) reconstruction through continuous exposure control. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LUCID consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods across diverse real-world nighttime scenarios.

</details>


### [38] [Beyond Skeletons: Learning Animation Directly from Driving Videos with Same2X Training Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06903)
*Yuan Zeng,Yujia Shi,Yuhao Yang,Dongxia Liu,Zongqing Lu,Wenming Yang,Qingmin Liao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DirectAnimator removes the dependency on noisy pose estimators by learning directly from raw driving videos, using fused pose/face/location cues and a Same2X training scheme to deliver better animation quality, identity consistency, robustness, and efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing human image animation methods depend on pose-estimation-based intermediate representations, which are often unreliable under occlusion, complex articulation, or estimation errors. The paper is motivated by the need for a more robust and efficient control mechanism that can directly use raw driving videos without inheriting the weaknesses of explicit pose extraction.

Method: The paper proposes DirectAnimator, a human image animation framework that avoids external pose estimators. It constructs a Driving Cue Triplet of pose, face, and location cues from raw driving videos, then fuses these signals with a CueFusion DiT block during the denoising process. It also introduces a Same2X training strategy to transfer stable cross-identity learning from same-identity supervision, improving optimization stability and convergence speed.

Result: Extensive experiments indicate that DirectAnimator reaches state-of-the-art performance in visual quality and identity preservation. It is also more robust to occlusions and challenging poses, while requiring fewer computational resources than prior methods.

Conclusion: DirectAnimator shows that bypassing explicit pose extraction and learning directly from raw driving videos can improve human image animation. With the Driving Cue Triplet, CueFusion DiT, and Same2X training, it achieves stronger visual fidelity, better identity preservation, higher robustness to occlusion and complex poses, and lower computational cost.

Abstract: Human image animation aims to generate a video from a static reference image, guided by pose information extracted from a driving video. Existing approaches often rely on pose estimators to extract intermediate representations, but such signals are prone to errors under occlusion or complex poses. Building on these observations, we present DirectAnimator, a framework that bypasses pose extraction and directly learns from raw driving videos. We introduce a Driving Cue Triplet consisting of pose, face, and location cues that captures motion, expression, and alignment in a semantically rich yet stable form, and we fuse them through a CueFusion DiT block for reliable control during denoising. To make learning dependable when the driving and reference identities differ, we devise a Same2X training strategy that aligns cross-ID features with those learned from same-ID data, regularizing optimization and accelerating convergence. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DirectAnimator attains state-of-the-art visual quality and identity preservation while remaining robust to occlusions and complex articulation, and it does so with fewer computational resources. Our project page is at https://directanimator.github.io/.

</details>


### [39] [polyDAG: Polynomial Acyclicity Constraints for Efficient Continuous Causal Discovery in Visual Semantic Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06908)
*Wenhao Zhang,Ramin Ramezani,Tao Han,Kai Hwang,Minyi Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: polyDAG is a faster acyclicity constraint for continuous causal discovery that keeps exact DAG validity while improving graph recovery and runtime on synthetic and visual-semantic datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuous DAG learning for visual semantic graphs is attractive because it yields interpretable dependencies among semantic image variables, but existing acyclicity enforcement is computationally expensive, limiting scalability and efficiency.

Method: The paper replaces the standard matrix-exponential acyclicity constraint with a finite polynomial trace constraint, proves it is exactly zero iff the graph is acyclic, and further derives a geometric-series implementation to avoid explicit summation while preserving the same acyclicity criterion.

Result: On synthetic Erdos-Renyi graphs and CelebA facial attribute graphs, polyDAG improves efficiency and accuracy: under the revised synthetic protocol with d in {100, 200, 500}, mean SHD decreases from 318.4 to 285.4 and mean F1 increases from 0.725 to 0.756; at 100 nodes, the geometric variant runs in 3.44s versus 5.16s for the exponential baseline, a 33.4% speedup.

Conclusion: polyDAG provides an efficient and theoretically sound polynomial acyclicity constraint for continuous DAG learning in visual semantic graphs, improving both runtime and structure recovery over matrix-exponential baselines.

Abstract: Modern image-analysis pipelines often convert images into structured semantic variables, such as facial attributes, object concepts, and scene descriptors. Learning directed dependencies among these variables can produce interpretable visual semantic graphs, but continuous directed acyclic graph learning is limited by the cost of enforcing acyclicity. We present polyDAG, a polynomial acyclicity framework for efficient continuous causal discovery in visual semantic graphs. polyDAG replaces the matrix-exponential acyclicity constraint with a finite polynomial trace constraint and proves that the new constraint is zero exactly for acyclic graphs. We further derive a geometric-series implementation that avoids the explicit summation loop while preserving the same acyclicity condition. Experiments on synthetic Erdos-Renyi graphs and CelebA facial visual attributes show that polyDAG improves efficiency and structure recovery. Averaged over the revised synthetic protocol with d in {100, 200, 500}, polyDAG reduces mean structural Hamming distance from 318.4 to 285.4 and improves mean F1 score from 0.725 to 0.756. At 100 nodes, the geometric variant runs in 3.44 seconds compared with 5.16 seconds for the exponential baseline, corresponding to a 33.4 percent speedup. Code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/wenhaoz-fengcai/polyDAG.

</details>


### [40] [DRIFT: From Robustness Gaps to Invariance Manifolds for AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06918)
*Abhishek Ameta,Sayan Banerjee,Shreyas Pandith,Harshit,Ankita Chatterjee,Akshay Janardan Bankar,Amit Satish Unde*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a one-class detector for AI-generated images that learns the invariance manifold of real images using a frozen vision model plus small projection heads. It separates physically robust changes from edit-sensitive changes, then detects fakes by identifying violations of this learned structure. This yields better open-world detection and interpretable localization than prior training-free methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing AI-generated image detectors struggle in open-world settings because new unseen generators evolve rapidly. Recent training-free robustness-based methods use frozen foundation models and perturbation-induced embedding drift, but they inherit fixed invariance geometry from pretraining and cannot adapt principledly to the detection objective. The paper is motivated by the need to learn a real-image-specific invariance structure that better distinguishes authentic images from generated ones under distribution shift.

Method: The method uses a frozen vision foundation model with lightweight projection heads to split representation space into two complementary subspaces: a robust subspace and a fragile subspace. Under one-class supervision using only real images, the robust subspace is trained to suppress changes caused by physically plausible imaging transformations, while the fragile subspace preserves sensitivity to edit-like perturbations. A structured ordering margin is imposed to hierarchically separate physical invariance from edit-induced variability. During inference, the model measures multi-scale patch-wise embedding drift under both transformation families to form a dual-channel invariance signature, and detection is performed by checking margin violations relative to the learned real-image manifold.

Result: Extensive experiments show strong open-world generalization across unseen generators and image resolutions. The proposed approach consistently outperforms training-free robustness-based baselines and additionally provides interpretable invariance-violation localization maps through its patch-wise dual-channel drift analysis.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that learning a task-specific structured invariance manifold of real images on top of a frozen vision foundation model improves open-world AI-generated image detection. By separating robust and fragile subspaces and testing margin violations, the method generalizes better to unseen generators and resolutions while also producing interpretable localization maps.

Abstract: The rapid evolution of generative image models challenges existing AI-generated image detectors, particularly in open-world settings with unseen generators. Recent training-free approaches measure robustness gaps in frozen vision foundation models (VFMs), detecting fakes via perturbation-induced embedding drift. However, these methods rely on fixed invariance geometry inherited from pretraining and lack principled adaptation to the detection task. We instead formulate AI-generated image detection as learning a structured invariance manifold of real images under one-class supervision. Building upon a frozen VFM, we introduce lightweight projection heads that decompose representation space into complementary robust and fragile subspaces. The robust subspace is explicitly trained to suppress variations induced by physically plausible imaging transformations, approximating tangent directions of a real-image manifold, while the fragile subspace retains sensitivity to edit-like perturbations. A structured ordering margin enforces hierarchical separation between physical invariance and edit-induced variability, enabling detection as a margin-violation test relative to the learned manifold. At inference, multi-scale patch-wise drift under both transformation families yields a dual-channel invariance signature and interpretable localization. Extensive experiments demonstrate strong open-world generalization across unseen generators and resolutions, consistently outperforming training-free robustness-based baselines while providing interpretable invariance-violation maps.

</details>


### [41] [SVHighlights: Towards Extremely Long Sport Video Highlight Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06926)
*Donggyu Lee,Youngbin Ki,Jeonghun Kang,Taehwan Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces the first large-scale benchmark for highlight detection in hour-long sports videos and proposes a training-free multimodal segment-based baseline that performs better than adapted existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing highlight detection methods mainly target short-form videos because there is no suitable benchmark for extremely long videos. They also struggle to generalize to hour-long content and clip-level scoring lacks sufficient global context for reliable highlight identification.

Method: The paper builds SVHighlights by pairing full-length sports videos with official highlight videos through a scalable data generation pipeline. As a baseline, it proposes TF-SELECTOR, a training-free method that merges adjacent semantically similar shots into context-aware segments and uses a multimodal large language model with visual captions, transcripts, and audio volume to score segment saliency.

Result: SVHighlights contains 320 sports videos with an average duration of 2.00 hours and a total of 640.18 hours, making it much larger than prior datasets. TF-SELECTOR outperforms VTG-tuned baselines on most metrics, improving HIT@1 by 3.12, HIT@K by 4.06, and IoU by 2.95.

Conclusion: SVHighlights establishes the first benchmark for highlight detection in extremely long sports videos and shows that a simple training-free segment-based strategy, TF-SELECTOR, can scale effectively to hour-long videos and outperform VTG-tuned baselines.

Abstract: While highlight detection for long-form videos is of great practical importance, most existing methods remain limited to short-form content, largely due to the absence of a suitable benchmark. To bridge this gap, we introduce SVHighlights, to the best of our knowledge, the first benchmark for highlight detection in extremely long sports videos, each exceeding one hour in duration, across multiple sports categories. SVHighlights is constructed from pairs of full-length sports videos and their corresponding official highlight videos using a dataset generation pipeline, enabling scalable label generation without conventional per-clip saliency annotation. The benchmark comprises 320 videos with an average duration of 2.00 hours and a total of 640.18 hours, substantially exceeding previous datasets. Existing methods also face fundamental challenges on long videos: models trained on short clips fail to generalize to hour-long content, and their clip-level scoring lacks the broader context needed to identify highlights. To address this and provide a strong baseline, we present TF-SELECTOR, a training-free segment-based approach that divides each video into context-aware segments by merging adjacent shots sharing the same semantic content, and predicts segment-level saliency scores using a large language model with multimodal inputs including visual captions, transcripts, and audio volume. Experiments demonstrate that TF-SELECTOR achieves superior performance across most metrics compared to Video Temporal Grounding (VTG)-tuned baselines, with improvements of +3.12 in HIT@1, +4.06 in HIT@K, and +2.95 in IoU. These results establish SVHighlights as a challenging testbed for long-form highlight detection and demonstrate that a simple segment-based strategy can effectively scale to hour-long videos.

</details>


### [42] [When CLIP Sees More, It Fights Back Harder: Multi-View Guided Adaptive Counterattacks for Test-Time Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06938)
*Sunoh Kim,Daeho Um*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MAC是一种面向CLIP的测试时鲁棒增强方法：它利用多视角信息并按污染程度自适应地执行counterattack，从而比TTC在强攻击下更稳健。 / MAC is a test-time robustness enhancement for CLIP that uses multi-view guidance and corruption-aware adaptive counterattacks, making it more robust than TTC under strong attacks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有TTC虽然能在测试时通过扰动输入来提升CLIP鲁棒性，但在强攻击下仍较脆弱，因为它依赖直接受损的原始视图，且基于噪声的硬门控机制无法适应不同程度的污染。 / Existing TTC improves robustness at test time, but remains fragile under strong attacks because it relies on a directly corrupted original view and uses noise-driven hard gating that cannot adapt to varying corruption severity.

Method: 该方法首先为输入图像构建多个增强视角以获得多样化嵌入；随后对各视角的受污染嵌入执行counterattack进行修正；接着依据每个视角估计的污染程度自适应调节counterattack强度；最后对多视角结果进行加权聚合得到最终预测。 / The method constructs multiple augmented views, refines corrupted embeddings via test-time counterattacks, adaptively scales attack intensity according to estimated corruption severity for each view, and aggregates the resulting views for final prediction.

Result: 在20个数据集和多种攻击场景上的大量实验表明，MAC相较于已有方法显著增强鲁棒性，同时保持较快推理速度、较低内存开销，并且无需调参。 / Extensive experiments on 20 datasets and diverse attack scenarios show that MAC substantially outperforms prior methods in robustness while remaining fast, memory-efficient, and tuning-free.

Conclusion: MAC通过多视角引导与基于污染程度的自适应反制，在保持较高推理速度和内存效率的同时，显著提升了CLIP在多数据集、多攻击场景下的鲁棒性。 / MAC significantly improves CLIP robustness across datasets and attack settings while retaining high inference speed and memory efficiency.

Abstract: Vision-language models such as CLIP have achieved remarkable zero-shot recognition capabilities, yet their robustness against adversarial perturbations remains limited. Test-time counterattack (TTC) was recently proposed to improve CLIP's robustness by perturbing an input image to steer it away from a corrupted state during inference. However, TTC remains fragile under strong attacks because its counterattack relies on a directly corrupted original view and employs a noise-driven hard-gating scheme that cannot adapt to varying corruption severity. To address these limitations, we introduce Multi-view guided Adaptive Counterattack (MAC), which performs counterattacks for multi-view with corruption-aware soft weighting. Specifically, MAC first constructs augmented views of an input image to obtain diverse embeddings. It then performs counterattacks to refine corrupted embeddings of views. Next, MAC adaptively scales the counterattack intensity for each view based on its estimated corruption degree. Finally, the adaptively counterattacked views are aggregated to yield a robust final prediction. Extensive experiments across 20 datasets and diverse attack scenarios demonstrate that MAC substantially improves robustness while preserving high inference speed and memory efficiency with its tuning-free design. Our code is available at https://github.com/sunoh-kim/MAC.

</details>


### [43] [SS-TPT: Stability and Suitability-Guided Test-Time Prompt Tuning for Adversarially Robust Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06943)
*Sunoh Kim,Daeho Um*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SS-TPT 在测试时为增强视图打分，只信任稳定且适配的视图来做 prompt tuning 和预测，因此比以往方法更鲁棒、也更快。 / SS-TPT scores augmented views at test time and trusts only stable, suitable ones for prompt tuning and prediction, making it both more robust and faster than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有基于测试时自适应的防御方法通常依赖大量增强视图来提升 VLM 的对抗鲁棒性，但会造成明显的推理减速，形成不实用的鲁棒性-吞吐量折中。因此需要一种能够识别高质量视图、减少低质量或受扰动视图影响的方法。 / Existing test-time adaptation defenses for VLMs often rely on many augmented views to improve adversarial robustness, causing substantial slowdown and an impractical robustness-throughput trade-off. This motivates a method that can identify high-quality views and reduce the influence of low-quality or corrupted ones.

Method: 论文提出 SS-TPT（Stability and Suitability-guided Test-time Prompt Tuning）。方法对每个增强视图计算两类分数：稳定性分数用于衡量预测在弱增强下的一致性，适配性分数用于衡量该视图在特征空间中的密度。随后，这两个分数被用于 SS 引导的一致性损失和 SS 加权预测，在测试时进行 prompt tuning 与推理。 / The paper proposes SS-TPT (Stability and Suitability-guided Test-time Prompt Tuning). It computes two scores for each augmented view: a stability score measuring prediction consistency under weak augmentations, and a suitability score measuring feature-space density. These scores are then used in an SS-guided consistency loss and SS-weighted prediction for test-time prompt tuning and inference.

Result: 大量实验表明，SS-TPT 在多种数据集和不同视图数量设置下，相比先前最先进方法取得了更强的鲁棒性和更优的吞吐效率，体现出良好的实用性与泛化性。 / Extensive experiments show that SS-TPT achieves stronger robustness and better throughput efficiency than prior state-of-the-art methods across diverse datasets and varying numbers of views, demonstrating practicality and generality.

Conclusion: SS-TPT 通过稳定性与适配性双重视角筛选和加权增强视图，在提升对抗鲁棒性的同时显著改善了鲁棒性与吞吐量之间的权衡，并在多数据集和不同视图数设置下优于现有方法。 / SS-TPT improves adversarial robustness while significantly improving the robustness-throughput trade-off by selecting and weighting augmented views using both stability and suitability, outperforming prior methods across datasets and view counts.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP achieve strong zero-shot recognition but remain highly fragile under adversarial perturbations. Recent test-time adaptation defenses improve robustness by leveraging many augmented views, but this leads to impractical slowdown and a clear robustness-throughput trade-off. To address this challenge, we present Stability and Suitability-guided Test-time Prompt Tuning (SS-TPT), evaluating the quality of each augmented view via two complementary scores: (1) stability, measuring prediction invariance to weak augmentations, and (2) suitability, measuring feature-space density among views. These stability and suitability (SS) scores guide both adaptation and inference through an SS-guided consistency loss and an SS-weighted prediction, amplifying trustworthy views while suppressing corrupted ones. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SS-TPT significantly outperforms prior state-of-the-art methods, achieving superior robustness-throughput trade-offs across diverse datasets and varying numbers of views, thereby demonstrating both strong practicality and generality. Our code is available at https://github.com/sunoh-kim/SS-TPT.

</details>


### [44] [When is 3D Worth It? A Resource-Performance Frontier for CNNs and Transformers in Lung CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06950)
*Md Enamul Hoq,Sharafat Hossain,Imraul Emmaka,Linda Larson-Prior,Lawrence Tarbox,Jonathan Bona,Donald Johann Jr. and Fred Prior*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper does not propose a new model; it benchmarks 2D, 2.5D, and 3D inputs for lung cancer screening classification. Under the same training setup, 2.5D CNNs were the most practical choice, while 3D CNNs and transformers showed stability or prediction failure issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although 3D models are commonly assumed to be better for volumetric medical imaging, their added compute and complexity are only justified if they deliver meaningful practical gains. The study investigates whether that assumption holds under fair comparison.

Method: The paper performs a controlled comparative study of input dimensionality (2D, 2.5D, 3D) across CNNs and Vision Transformers using a fixed training protocol on a leakage-free NLST cohort with supporting LIDC-IDRI data, evaluating performance, stability, and failure modes.

Result: The 2.5D CNN achieved the best discrimination-stability balance in the comparison, with ROC-AUC 0.682 (95% CI [0.546, 0.799]) and a stable operating point. 3D CNNs showed threshold instability, while transformers produced degenerate predictions such as all-positive outputs. Confidence intervals were wide and overlapping.

Conclusion: For class-imbalanced lung cancer screening classification, 2D and especially 2.5D inputs offer a more reliable trade-off between discrimination, stability, and computational efficiency than full 3D models under a controlled training setup.

Abstract: Three-dimensional models are widely assumed preferable for volumetric medical imaging, yet their practical value depends on whether performance gains justify added computational cost and complexity. Rather than proposing a new architecture, we study how input dimensionality (2D, 2.5D, 3D) affects model behavior across convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and Vision Transformers (ViTs) under a fixed training protocol. Using a leakage-free NLST cohort (n = 1,977) with supporting LIDC-IDRI data, we find that the 2.5D CNN offers the most favorable discrimination-stability trade-off in our comparison (ROC-AUC 0.682, 95% CI [0.546, 0.799]) with a stable operating point. In contrast, 3D CNNs show threshold instability, and transformers exhibit degenerate predictions, such as all-positive predictions. Confidence intervals are wide and overlapping, so we present these results as a controlled resource-performance frontier and a failure-mode taxonomy rather than as definitive superiority claims. For class-imbalanced lung cancer screening classification, 2D and 2.5D inputs provide a more reliable trade-off between performance, stability, and computational efficiency than full 3D representations.

</details>


### [45] [MVSegNet: A Lightweight Boundary-Aware Network for Fetal Lateral Ventricle Segmentation and Atrial Width Estimation in Prenatal Ultrasound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06958)
*Arafat Hossain Sayem*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a compact and fast ultrasound segmentation network, MVSegNet, for fetal lateral ventricle analysis. By combining multi-scale features and boundary-aware refinement, it improves segmentation boundaries and atrial-width measurement accuracy over six baselines while remaining computationally efficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate measurement of fetal lateral ventricle atrial width in prenatal ultrasound depends on precise segmentation, but this is challenging because ultrasound images suffer from acoustic shadowing, speckle noise, and poor contrast.

Method: The paper proposes MVSegNet, a lightweight encoder-decoder network that integrates multi-scale feature extraction with boundary-aware refinement. It was trained and evaluated on 584 expert-annotated transventricular prenatal ultrasound images using a 70/15/15 train-validation-test split and compared with six baseline segmentation methods.

Result: MVSegNet achieved a Dice score of 80.79%, IoU of 68.47%, Hausdorff distance of 4.07 mm, and atrial width mean absolute error of 3.40 mm. It used only 2.31 million parameters, ran at 165.6 FPS on an NVIDIA T4 GPU, and outperformed all evaluated baselines on boundary and measurement metrics.

Conclusion: MVSegNet is an efficient and lightweight fetal ultrasound segmentation model that improves boundary delineation and atrial-width measurement accuracy, making it suitable for automated analysis of fetal ventriculomegaly.

Abstract: Fetal ventriculomegaly is assessed by measuring the atrial width of the lateral ventricle in prenatal ultrasound. Accurate segmentation is essential for this measurement, but acoustic shadowing, speckle noise, and poor contrast make it difficult. We developed MVSegNet, a lightweight encoder-decoder network combining multi-scale feature extraction and boundary-aware refinement. The model was trained and evaluated on 584 expert-annotated transventricular ultrasound frames using a 70/15/15 split. Performance was compared against six segmentation baselines using overlap, boundary, and measurement metrics. MVSegNet achieved a Dice score of 80.79%, IoU of 68.47%, Hausdorff distance of 4.07 mm, and atrial width mean absolute error of 3.40 mm. The model contains 2.31 million parameters and runs at 165.6 frames per second on an NVIDIA T4 GPU. MVSegNet outperformed all evaluated baselines on boundary and measurement metrics while maintaining low computational cost, supporting its use in automated fetal ultrasound analysis.

</details>


### [46] [From Vision to Text: A Compact Multimodal Approach for Robust, Cross-Domain Presentation Attack Detection on ID Cards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06966)
*Qingwen Zeng,Juan E. Tapia,Sneha Das,Christoph Busch*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 论文提出一个紧凑型视觉+文本多模态身份证PAD模型。它在监督微调后跨域表现不错，但零样本不行。核心结论是：真实数据和足够模型容量比依赖合成数据更重要，当前合成数据基准需要重新审视。 / The paper introduces a compact vision-text multimodal model for ID-card PAD. It generalizes fairly well after supervised fine-tuning but not in zero-shot settings. Main takeaway: real data and adequate model capacity matter more than relying on synthetic datasets, whose benchmark value should be reconsidered.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 由于隐私限制，身份证PAD可用数据有限，而跨域分布偏移使模型部署困难，因此需要探索能更好泛化的多模态方法，并检验合成数据是否足以支撑评测与训练。 / Privacy constraints limit available ID-card PAD data, and cross-domain shifts make deployment difficult, motivating a multimodal approach with better generalization and an examination of whether synthetic data is sufficient for training and benchmarking.

Method: 提出一个紧凑型多模态模型，引入新的生成式与判别式模块，联合利用身份证图像中的视觉信息与文本信息，对真实与合成身份证图像进行展示攻击检测。 / The paper proposes a compact multimodal model with new generative and discriminative blocks that jointly use visual and textual cues from ID-card images for presentation attack detection on genuine and synthetic IDs.

Result: 实验表明，多模态模型在有监督微调后表现出良好的泛化，但零样本能力不足；同时，结果显示真实世界数据和模型规模对性能至关重要，且现有合成数据集不能充分反映真实挑战。 / Experiments show that multimodal models generalize well after supervised fine-tuning but have weak zero-shot performance; performance also depends strongly on real-world data and model capacity, while current synthetic datasets fail to capture real deployment challenges.

Conclusion: 多模态PAD在监督微调后具有较强跨域泛化能力，但在零样本场景下仍然失效；可靠的身份证PAD依赖足够的模型容量与真实世界数据，现有合成数据集不足以作为稳健基准。 / Multimodal PAD generalizes reasonably well after supervised fine-tuning, but still fails in zero-shot settings; reliable ID-card PAD requires sufficient model capacity and real-world data, and existing synthetic datasets are not robust benchmarks.

Abstract: Cross-domain shifts challenge Presentation Attack Detection (PAD) on ID Cards, given the restricted data available due to privacy concerns. This work proposes a compact multimodal model, based on new generative and discriminative blocks, which combines visual and textual data for PAD on genuine and synthetic ID images. While multimodal models exhibit strong generalisation after supervised fine-tuning, they fail in zero-shot settings. Our findings underscore that model capacity and real-world data are essential for reliable PAD, while existing synthetic datasets may not reflect real-world challenges. We argue for a re-evaluation of synthetic data as a benchmark and emphasise the need for more realistic, diverse datasets to advance PAD research.

</details>


### [47] [CL-CLIP: CLIP-Based Continual Learning Framework with Cost-Volume Category Decoupling for Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06978)
*Zihan Liu,Yuguang Yang,Shengjie Su,Jianing Pang,Linlin Yang,Chunyu Xie,Nikolai Yu. Zolotykh,Baochang Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TL;DR：这篇论文把CLIP开放词汇检测器改造成更适合持续学习的检测框架，核心是用图文相似度cost volume引导类别解耦，从而减少灾难性遗忘并提升新类别学习效果。 / TL;DR: The paper turns a CLIP-based open-vocabulary detector into a stronger continual detector by using image-text similarity cost volumes to decouple category-specific features, reducing forgetting and improving new-class learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：持续目标检测需要不断学习新类别，但现有CLIP式开放词汇检测器虽有强零样本泛化能力，一旦持续微调就会严重遗忘已学检测能力，因此需要把开放词汇能力与持续学习能力结合起来。 / Motivation: COD requires learning new categories over time, but existing CLIP-based open-vocabulary detectors, despite strong zero-shot generalization, suffer severe forgetting during continual fine-tuning. The work aims to combine open-vocabulary capability with robust continual learning.

Method: 方法上，论文基于CLIP与F-ViT/CAT-Seg思路，先计算图像token与类别文本嵌入之间的稠密相似度cost volume，作为zero-shot空间先验；再用该先验将共享区域特征解耦为类别特定通路，并交给Multi-Expert RoI head处理。 / Method: Building on CLIP and ideas from F-ViT/CAT-Seg, the method computes a dense image-text similarity cost volume as a zero-shot spatial prior, then decouples shared region features into class-specific pathways processed by a Multi-Expert RoI head.

Result: 结果表明，在PASCAL VOC和MS-COCO上的大量实验中，CL-CLIP相比持续微调下的F-ViT基线有明显提升，并且与现有持续目标检测方法相比具有竞争力，尤其在新类别适应方面更强，同时保持有竞争力的基类表现。 / Results: Extensive experiments on PASCAL VOC and MS-COCO show clear gains over the continually fine-tuned F-ViT baseline and competitive performance against existing continual object detectors, especially for newly introduced classes while maintaining competitive base-class performance.

Conclusion: 该论文得出结论：CL-CLIP通过cost-volume-guided category decoupling，能够显著缓解CLIP式开放词汇检测器在持续学习中的灾难性遗忘，并在学习新类别的同时较好保持旧类别性能。 / Conclusion: CL-CLIP significantly mitigates catastrophic forgetting in CLIP-based open-vocabulary detectors under continual learning, while adapting to new categories and retaining strong performance on previously learned classes.

Abstract: Continual Object Detection (COD) requires a detector to acquire new categories over time while preserving previously learned ones. This goal is closely related to open-vocabulary detection, since both settings require reasoning over categories that are not fully covered by the annotations available at the current training stage. Recent CLIP-based open-vocabulary detectors have shown strong zero-shot generalization, and frameworks such as F-ViT demonstrate that vision-language pretraining can provide powerful zero-shot detection ability for unseen categories. However, real-world deployments cannot remain purely zero-shot: once these detectors are continually updated on newly introduced categories, they suffer severe catastrophic forgetting and quickly lose their previously calibrated detection ability. We therefore propose CL-CLIP, a CLIP-based COD framework that equips open-vocabulary detectors with better continual learning ability through cost-volume-guided category decoupling. Specifically, following CAT-Seg, we compute a CLIP image-text similarity cost volume, defined as dense category-wise response maps between visual tokens and class text embeddings. This zero-shot spatial prior decomposes shared region features into class-specific pathways, which are then processed by a Multi-Expert RoI head. Extensive experiments on PASCAL VOC and MS-COCO show that CL-CLIP substantially improves the F-ViT baseline under continual fine-tuning and achieves competitive performance with existing continual object detectors, especially in adapting to newly introduced categories while preserving competitive base-class performance.

</details>


### [48] [Don't Pause: Streaming Video-Language Synchrony for Online Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06991)
*Zhenyu Yang,Kairui Zhang,Shengsheng Qian,Weiming Dong,Changsheng Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LyraV 通过帧级控制与动态 token 节奏调度，让视频大模型边看边说、不再因生成而停看，从而在流式视频场景中实现高同步、低卡顿的实时交互。 / LyraV lets a Video-LLM watch and speak at the same time by combining frame-level control with dynamic token pacing, enabling highly synchronized, low-stutter real-time interaction in streaming video.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于解决在线视频大模型在流式场景中的关键缺陷：现有方法通常在生成回复时暂停视频感知，导致视频理解与语言输出失去实时同步，产生卡顿和交互不连贯的问题。 / The motivation is to address a core limitation of existing online Video-LLMs in streaming settings: they typically pause video perception while generating responses, which breaks real-time synchrony between visual understanding and language output and causes stuttering, discontinuous interaction.

Method: 方法上，作者构建了一个分层控制框架 LyraV，核心包括两个模块：FDTC（Frame-Driven Transition Controller），用于基于帧驱动的高层语义状态切换；以及 SToP（Streaming Token Pacer），用于动态调节文本生成速率以匹配视觉内容节奏。同时采用 per-frame incremental, sub-budget decoding，使模型在每个视频帧间隔内只生成少量 token，从而避免生成期间阻塞视觉感知。 / Methodologically, the paper introduces a hierarchical control framework called LyraV with two key components: FDTC (Frame-Driven Transition Controller) for frame-driven high-level semantic state transitions, and SToP (Streaming Token Pacer) for dynamically adjusting generation speed to match the pace of visual input. It also uses per-frame incremental, sub-budget decoding so that only a small chunk of tokens is emitted within each frame interval, preventing perception from being blocked during generation.

Result: 实验在五个在线基准和三个离线基准上表明，LyraV 在保持主干模型整体理解能力的同时，显著提升了流式同步性和叙事流畅度，达到了 98.29% 的视频播放同步率和 3.89 FPS 的实时处理速度；此外，作者还观察到其具备随流式 token 进行动态推理的经验性能力。 / Experiments on five online and three offline benchmarks show that LyraV significantly improves streaming synchrony and narrative fluency while preserving the backbone’s general understanding capability, achieving 98.29% synchrony with video playback and 3.89 FPS real-time processing speed. The authors also observe an empirical capability for dynamic reasoning over streaming tokens.

Conclusion: 论文提出的 LyraV 通过 Streaming Video-Language Synchrony 范式实现了视频感知与语言生成的细粒度同步，在不损害主干模型通用理解能力的前提下，显著提升了流式场景中的同步性与叙事流畅度。 / The paper concludes that LyraV enables fine-grained synchronization between video perception and language generation under the SVLS paradigm, substantially improving streaming synchrony and narrative fluency without sacrificing the backbone model’s general understanding ability.

Abstract: Online Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) have advanced toward seamless human-AI interaction through frame-by-frame processing and proactive responding. However, a critical challenge remains in streaming scenarios: existing models typically pause video perception while generating responses, breaking real-time video-language synchrony and causing stutters. To address this, we introduce a novel paradigm for online video understanding: Streaming Video-Language Synchrony (SVLS), and present LyraV, a live streaming assistant built upon a hierarchical control framework with two core innovations. First, the Frame-Driven Transition Controller (FDTC), a training-free verification-based finite-state machine, makes high-level semantic decisions on when to continue speaking, start a new response, or stay silent. Second, the Streaming Token Pacer (SToP), a plug-and-play lightweight predictive module, dynamically adapts the language generation rate to match the pace of the visual content. Concretely, LyraV performs \emph{per-frame incremental, sub-budget decoding}: within each frame interval it emits only a small chunk of tokens that fits the real-time budget, so perception is never blocked for a full sentence. Together, these components enable LyraV to seamlessly interleave incoming video frames with generated word tokens, achieving a fine-grained synchrony.
