*Mikhail Vishnyakov,Tatiana Gaintseva*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: 作者用冻结语言模型各层隐藏状态中的区分方向来检测AI文本：把文本投影到这些steering vectors上，再用轻量分类器判断。该方法对跨领域、跨模型和编辑攻击都更稳健。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有机器生成文本检测在分布偏移下尤其困难，例如跨领域、跨源模型迁移以及经过润色、重写等编辑攻击后性能下降明显，因此需要一种在分布变化下仍具鲁棒性的检测方法。

Method: 方法基于一个冻结的语言模型。作者在每一层隐藏表示中构造一个区分人类文本与机器文本的方向（steering vector），然后将输入文本表示为其在各层方向上的对齐/投影特征，最后训练一个轻量分类器对这些层级投影特征进行判别并输出检测分数。

Result: 该方法在分布内测试和多种分布偏移条件下都取得了强性能，包括跨领域、跨源模型以及机器编辑变换（如polishing和rewriting）。解释性分析表明，学习到的方向与可识别的文体线索一致，同时还能捕获超越表层特征的大量信号。

Conclusion: 将假文本检测视为表征空间探测问题是有效的；基于冻结语言模型隐藏状态提取的steering vectors，配合轻量分类器，在分布内和分布外场景下都能实现稳健、简单且有效的机器生成文本检测。

Abstract: Detecting machine-generated text is especially difficult under distribution shift, such as transfer across domains, source models, and editing attacks. We propose a fake-text detector based on steering vectors extracted from the hidden representations of a frozen language model. At each layer, we construct a direction that separates human-written from machine-generated text, and represent each input by its layer-wise alignment with these directions. A lightweight classifier trained on these projection features yields the final detection score. Our method achieves strong performance both in-distribution and under distribution shift, including across domains, source models, and machine-editing transformations such as polishing and rewriting. Interpretation analyses show that the learned directions align with recognizable stylistic cues while capturing substantial additional signal beyond surface features. These results position fake-text detection as a representation-space probing problem and show that steering vectors provide a simple and effective solution.

</details>


### [146] [LLM-Guided Evolution for Medical Decision Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07342)
*Ivan Sviridov,Artem Oskin,Ivan Panin,Iaroslav Bespalov,Dmitry Dylov,Ivan Oseledets,Aleksandr Nesterov*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: The paper uses LLM-guided evolutionary search at inference time to automatically discover better clinical decision programs, beating manual baselines in triage, consultation, and medical image classification with interpretable gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Adapting LLMs to clinical workflows is usually expensive and labor-intensive because it relies on fine-tuning or handcrafted prompts/pipelines; the paper seeks a cheaper and more flexible inference-time adaptation approach.

Method: The paper formulates clinical tasks as evolutionary search problems over executable artifacts and uses LLM-guided MAP-Elites evolution with task-specific fitness functions to optimize strategies for urgency triage, interactive consultation, and medical image classification.

Result: Across all three tasks, evolution outperforms manually designed baselines. In triage, Semigran accuracy rises from 77.3% to 87.1% and emergency recall from 0.60 to 0.97, with better safety-weighted held-out MIMIC-ESI performance. In consultation, evolved policies improve the accuracy-cost frontier across multiple model families and transfer to held-out iCRAFTMD. In PneumoniaMNIST, prompt-only evolution improves frozen MedGemma VLMs while keeping strict JSON outputs.

Conclusion: LLM-guided MAP-Elites can serve as an effective inference-time alternative to fine-tuning or manual prompt engineering for clinical tasks, yielding safer and more accurate decision strategies through interpretable evolved programs.

Abstract: Adapting large language models (LLMs) to clinical workflows often requires costly fine-tuning or manual prompt and pipeline engineering. We study LLM-guided MAP-Elites evolution as an inference-time alternative for discovering medical decision strategies and provide an implementation repository at https://github.com/univanxx/llm_guided_evo_medical. We formulate urgency triage, interactive consultation, and medical image classification as evolutionary searches over executable artifacts optimized by task-specific fitness functions.
  Across all three settings, evolution improves over manually designed baselines under practical constraints. In triage, evolved programs increase Semigran accuracy from $77.3\%$ to $87.1\%$ and emergency recall from $0.60$ to $0.97$, while improving safety-weighted held-out MIMIC-ESI performance. In interactive consultation, evolved policies improve the accuracy--cost frontier across Llama-3, Qwen-3.5, and Gemma-4 and transfer to held-out iCRAFTMD. In PneumoniaMNIST, prompt-only evolution improves frozen MedGemma VLMs while preserving strict JSON outputs. Qualitative analysis shows that the gains come from interpretable program-level mechanisms, calibrated triage boundaries, targeted evidence acquisition, selective commitment, and finding-oriented visual decision rules, rather than superficial prompt rewording alone.

</details>


### [147] [M$^3$Exam: Benchmarking Multimodal Memory for Realistic User-Agent Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07402)
*Zhengjun Huang,Wenxuan Liu,Zhoujin Tian,Wei Chen,Junle Chen,Yuqian Wu,Fangyuan Zhang,Qintian Guo,Xiaofang Zhou*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: The paper builds a realistic benchmark for multimodal conversational memory and shows that current systems still struggle. Its new method, M^3Proctor, makes multimodal memory more accurate and much more efficient by loading visual evidence only when necessary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks are overly simplified: they mostly reflect human-human conversations with sparse visuals and straightforward content, so they fail to test authentic multimodal file interaction and inference of implicit user information. The paper is motivated by the need for a more realistic evaluation setting and a more efficient multimodal memory mechanism.

Method: The authors introduce M^3Exam, a query-centric benchmark based on realistic user-agent interactions for evaluating multimodal conversational memory. They also propose M^3Proctor, a multimodal memory method that detects modality bias in a query and accesses raw visual sources on demand instead of processing all visual information upfront.

Result: Experiments show persistent weaknesses in existing MLLMs and memory systems on cross-modal grounding, cross-session reasoning, and efficiency under growing multimodal context. M^3Proctor improves accuracy by 13% and reduces index-construction time and retrieved tokens by more than 70%.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that current multimodal language models and memory systems are still weak at realistic multimodal conversational memory, especially in cross-modal grounding, cross-session reasoning, and handling long accumulated context efficiently. Its proposed method, M^3Proctor, improves both accuracy and efficiency by selectively using raw visual inputs only when needed.

Abstract: Language agents are increasingly deployed over accumulating multimodal information, yet existing benchmarks assume a human-human form with sparse visuals and straightforward content, evaluating neither reasoning over authentic multimodal file interaction nor the interpretation of concealed user information. We therefore introduce M$^3$Exam, a query-centric multimodal conversational memory benchmark built on realistic user-agent interaction, with multi-dimensional evaluation spanning cross-modal grounding and implicit information inference. Benchmarking MLLMs and memory systems reveals persistent gaps in cross-modal grounding, cross session reasoning, and the efficiency cost of accumulating multimodal context. We further propose M$^3$Proctor, a multimodal memory method that detects query modality bias and consumes raw visual sources only on demand, improving accuracy by 13% while cutting index-construction time and retrieved tokens by over 70%.

</details>


### [148] [The Masked Advantage: Uncovering Local-Language Access to Cultural Knowledge in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07422)
*Yang Zhang,Xiao Fei,Amr Mohamed,Sarah Almeida Carneiro,Mersin Konomi,Mingmeng Geng,Ahmed Asaad,Guokan Shang,Michalis Vazirgiannis*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: Raw scores make English look better, but a controlled IRT-based analysis shows that local cultural knowledge is usually more accessible in the local language once differences in language proficiency are removed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior evaluations of multilingual cultural knowledge often use parallel template-based questions and raw accuracy, which can misrepresent natural cultural knowledge and confound general language proficiency with true language-conditioned knowledge access.

Method: The paper builds a controlled evaluation framework using real-world cultural questions from 13 locales, crossing question type (culture-agnostic vs. culture-specific) with query language (English vs. local language), and uses a shared 1PL item response theory model to disentangle language proficiency from knowledge access across about 80 models.

Result: The study finds a consistent English advantage on culture-agnostic questions, indicating stronger English proficiency. But once this proficiency gap is accounted for, local languages show a positive knowledge-access advantage in nearly all locale-model settings, especially for frontier, regionally aligned, or language-adapted models.

Conclusion: After controlling for overall language proficiency, local languages generally provide better access to local cultural knowledge than English, even when raw accuracy appears lower.

Abstract: Large language models are increasingly used to answer culturally grounded questions across languages, yet it remains unclear whether local cultural knowledge is better accessed through English or the local language. Existing evaluations face two key limitations: many rely on parallel template-based questions that may not reflect how cultural knowledge naturally appears, and raw accuracy conflates general language proficiency with language-conditioned knowledge access. We address these issues with a controlled framework built on real-world cultural questions collected from regional benchmarks and local sources. By crossing question type (culture-agnostic vs. culture-specific) with query language (English vs. local language), and estimating ability with a shared 1PL item response theory model, we separate proficiency from localized knowledge access. Across 13 locales and roughly 80 models, we find a consistent English advantage on culture-agnostic questions, indicating stronger English proficiency. However, after accounting for this proficiency gap, local languages show a positive knowledge-access advantage in nearly all locale-model settings. This advantage is often masked in raw accuracy but becomes more visible for frontier, regionally aligned, or language-adapted models. Our results suggest that weaker local-language performance does not necessarily imply weaker cultural knowledge; rather, local cultural knowledge may be more accessible through the local language but hidden by limited language proficiency.

</details>


### [149] [Sycophantic Praise: Evaluating Excessive Praise in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07441)
*Daniel Vennemeyer,Phan Anh Duong,Meryl Ye,Ruihong Huang,Tianyu Jiang*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: This paper studies excessive praise in LLMs as a separate form of sycophancy, proposes a better way to measure it, and shows that it is common especially in subjective, social settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work on sycophancy in language models has focused mainly on agreement or validation, while explicit praise and flattery have been underexplored. The paper is motivated by the idea that praise can also be misaligned, and that current methods are insufficient for detecting when a model flatters users beyond what is warranted.

Method: The paper introduces a parameterized evaluation framework for praise calibration. It measures whether model praise is excessive relative to two factors: the quality of the user contribution and the user's expected ability. The authors then compare this framework against generic LLM-as-a-judge methods using agreement with human annotations.

Result: The proposed framework substantially outperforms generic LLM judges in matching human annotations. The study also finds that sycophantic praise appears much more often in social and interpretive tasks than in objective reasoning tasks.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that sycophantic praise is a distinct alignment problem in language models, separate from general agreement-based sycophancy, and that existing evaluation methods do not measure it reliably. A calibrated praise framework better captures this issue and shows that excessive praise is especially common in social and interpretive contexts.

Abstract: Sycophancy in language models is typically studied as excessive agreement or validation, while explicit praise and flattery have received comparatively little attention. We argue that sycophantic praise is a distinct alignment problem that cannot be reliably measured using current methods. We introduce a parameterized framework that measures whether praise is excessive relative to contribution quality and expected user ability. We show that our framework substantially outperforms generic LLM judges in agreement with human annotations, and that sycophantic praise occurs far more frequently in social and interpretive domains than in objective reasoning settings. Together, these findings position praise calibration as a distinct alignment challenge.

</details>


### [150] [Supervision versus Demonstration-Based In-Context Learning for Multiword Expression Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07479)
*Sercan Karakaş,Yusuf Şimşek*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: The paper tests whether supervised models or prompted LLMs are better at detecting Turkish idiomatic light verb constructions. LLMs struggle in zero-shot settings, improve sharply with one-shot prompts, and perform best with carefully designed few-shot demonstrations, while BERTurk remains a strong baseline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Turkish idiomatic light verb constructions are difficult for multiword expression processing because they often have the same surface form as literal verb-object combinations while functioning as partially idiomatic predicates. This ambiguity motivates a focused evaluation of whether supervised models or prompted LLMs can better distinguish literal from idiomatic usage.

Method: The study formulates Turkish idiomatic light verb construction detection as a binary classification task between literal and idiomatic usage. It evaluates a supervised Turkish encoder baseline (BERTurk with a classifier head) against three instruction-tuned LLMs under zero-shot, one-shot, and few-shot prompting on a manually curated controlled dataset with positive LVCs and matched negative examples.

Result: Zero-shot LLMs achieve strong negative-class performance but very low recall on true LVCs. One-shot prompting substantially improves LVC recall, though it may introduce model-specific prediction bias. Few-shot prompting improves calibration and leads to robust overall performance, especially for GPT-OSS-20B and Qwen 2.5-14B, making them competitive with or better than the supervised baseline on LVC detection.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that Turkish LVC detection is highly sensitive to prompting. A supervised BERTurk baseline remains competitive, but instruction-tuned LLMs can match or outperform it on LVC identification when given carefully designed one-shot or few-shot demonstrations.

Abstract: Turkish idiomatic light verb constructions (LVCs) are challenging for multiword expression processing because they often share the same surface form as fully literal verb-object combinations while functioning as a single, partially idiomatic predicate. We frame Turkish LVC detection as a binary classification task (literal meaning vs. idiomatic meaning) and evaluate on a manually created controlled set (N=147) with matched negatives: out-of-domain random sentences and in-domain literal controls (NLVC), alongside LVC positives. We compare a supervised Turkish encoder baseline (BERTurk with a classifier head) to three instruction-tuned LLMs from different families under zero-shot, one-shot, and few-shot prompting, and analyze how demonstrations shift error profiles. In zero-shot, LLMs perform well on negatives but show very low LVC recall. One-shot prompting sharply improves LVC detection but can induce strong, model-specific biases, leading models to overpredict or underpredict LVCs. A richer few-shot prompt improves calibration and yields robust overall performance for GPT-OSS-20B and Qwen 2.5-14B. Overall, the results highlight substantial prompt sensitivity in Turkish metalinguistic classification: the supervised baseline remains competitive, while prompted LLMs can match or exceed it on LVCs with carefully constructed demonstrations.

</details>


### [151] [Your UnEmbedding Matrix is Secretly a Feature Lens for Text Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07502)
*Songhao Wu,Zhongxin Chen,Yuxuan Liu,Heng Cui,Cong Li,Rui Yan*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: 这篇论文认为LLM嵌入不好用，是因为表示空间被高频废词主导；他们提出一个很轻量的线性过滤器EmbedFilter，把这部分子空间去掉后，语义表示更强，还能顺带降维、省存储、提检索速度。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 作者观察到一个反常现象：当把文本嵌入投影到词表空间时，表示往往与高频但缺乏信息量的token对齐。作者据此认为，LLM作为现成嵌入模型表现不佳的一个原因，是这些高频token的过度表达压制了细粒度语义信息。

Method: 论文提出EmbedFilter：对LLM直接产生的文本嵌入施加一个简单的线性变换。核心做法是利用对unembedding matrix的分析，识别其中对应高频token写入embedding空间的潜在子空间，并将该子空间过滤掉，从而抑制高频token对表示的主导作用。

Result: 在多个LLM backbone上的实验表明，加入EmbedFilter后，模型在大规模文本嵌入/零样本下游任务上表现更优；而且即使显著降低嵌入维度，性能仍能保持甚至优于原始表示。

Conclusion: EmbedFilter通过去除由LLM反嵌入矩阵诱导的高频但低信息token子空间，显著提升了LLM文本嵌入的零样本下游性能；同时还能在保持质量的前提下实现降维、减少索引存储并加速检索。

Abstract: Large language models exhibit impressive zero-shot capabilities across a wide range of downstream tasks. However, they struggle to function as off-the-shelf embedding models, leading to suboptimal performance on massive text embedding benchmarks. In this paper, we identify a potential cause underlying this deficiency. Our motivation stems from an unexpected observation: text embeddings tend to align with frequent but uninformative tokens when projected onto the vocabulary space. We argue that this excessive expression of high-frequency tokens suppresses the model's ability to capture nuanced semantics. To address this, we introduce EmbedFilter, a simple linear transformation designed to refine text embeddings derived from LLMs directly. Specifically, we uncover that the unembedding matrix within LLMs encodes a latent space that is actively writing these frequent tokens into embedding space. By filtering out this subspace, EmbedFilter suppress the influence of high-frequency tokens, thereby enhancing semantic representations. As a compelling byproduct, this enables an inherent dimensionality reduction, lowering index storage and speedup retrieval while fully preserving the refined embedding quality. Our experiments across multiple LLM backbones demonstrate that LLMs equipped with EmbedFilter achieve superior zero-shot downstream performance even with significantly reduced embedding dimensions. We hope our findings provide deeper insights into the mechanisms of LLM-based representations and inspire more principled designs to improve text embeddings training. Our code is available at https://github.com/CentreChen/EmbFilter.

</details>


### [152] [Agentopia: Long-Term Life Simulation and Learning in Agent Societies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07513)
*Xintao Wang,Sirui Zheng,Hongqiu Wu,Weiyuan Li,Jen-tse Huang,Minghao Zhu,Can Zu,Qi Deng,Jiawei Wang,Qianyu He,Heng Wang,Xiaojian Wu,Yunzhe Tao*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: 中文：作者构建了一个可运行 10 年、包含 100 个智能体的社会生活模拟系统 Agentopia，并用“life reward”对 LLM 做拒绝采样训练。结果显示，长时程模拟能产生复杂社会行为，同时这种训练还能提升模型的拟人化社会智能与角色扮演表现。 English: The authors build Agentopia, a 10-year social life simulator with 100 agents, and train an LLM using rejection sampling guided by a life reward. The results suggest that long-horizon simulation produces complex social behavior and that this training improves anthropomorphic social intelligence and role-playing performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中文：现有基于 LLM 的智能体社会模拟通常只覆盖数天，难以支持深层社会互动与长期成长，因此无法充分回答 LLM 是否能从模拟社会经验中学习并更好理解、复现人类行为。 English: Prior LLM-based agent society simulations usually span only days, which limits deep social interaction and long-term development, leaving open whether LLMs can learn from simulated social experience to better understand and reproduce human behavior.

Method: 中文：论文提出 Agentopia，一个长时程多智能体生活模拟框架，让 100 个智能体在 10 个模拟年中自主成长、建立社会关系并满足需求与目标；同时定义 life reward 表征人类福祉，并用该奖励通过 rejection sampling 训练 LLM。 English: The paper introduces Agentopia, a long-term multi-agent life-simulation framework where 100 agents autonomously grow, build relationships, and pursue needs and goals over 10 simulated years; it also defines a life reward as a proxy for human well-being and uses it to train LLMs via rejection sampling.

Result: 中文：实验表明，Agentopia 中的智能体展现出丰富的涌现社会行为；life reward 训练显著提升了模拟中的智能体福祉，并能泛化到下游 role-playing benchmark，性能提升 15.6%。 English: Experiments show that agents in Agentopia display rich emergent social behaviors; life-reward training significantly improves agent well-being in simulation and generalizes to downstream role-playing benchmarks with a 15.6% performance gain.

Conclusion: 中文：长时程社会模拟能够产生丰富的涌现社会行为；基于 life reward 的拒绝采样训练可提升底层 LLM 的社会生活智能、改善智能体福祉，并在下游角色扮演基准上取得 +15.6% 提升。 English: Long-term social simulation yields rich emergent social behaviors; rejection-sampling training with life reward improves the underlying LLM’s social-life intelligence, increases agent well-being, and delivers a +15.6% gain on downstream role-playing benchmarks.

Abstract: Humans learn from social life. Simulating this process with LLM-powered agents represents a promising research direction, raising a natural question: whether LLMs can learn from such simulated social experience to better understand and replicate human behavior. However, prior agent society simulations typically operate at the scale of days, limiting the depth of social interactions and long-term growth. In this paper, we study long-term life simulation and LLM learning in agent societies, with two goals: (1) investigating social behaviors that emerge from life-long simulation, and (2) developing anthropomorphic capabilities in LLMs, particularly intelligence in social life, through years of simulated social experience. Specifically, we present Agentopia, a comprehensive framework for long-term life simulation in multi-agent societies, where 100 agents autonomously pursue personal growth, develop social relationships, and fulfill their needs and goals over 10 simulated years. We define life reward to mirror human well-being, and leverage this reward to train LLMs via rejection sampling. Extensive experiments show that agents exhibit rich emergent social behaviors. Furthermore, life reward training effectively enhances the underlying LLM, which leads to improved agent well-being in simulation, and generalizes to downstream role-playing benchmarks with +15.6% improvement.

</details>


### [153] [How reliable are LLMs when it comes to playing dice?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07515)
*Luca Avena,Gianmarco Bet,Bernardo Busoni*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: We investigate the probabilistic reasoning capabilities of large language models through a controlled benchmarking study on discrete probability problems. We constructed two datasets, respectively a set of standard exercises and a set of counterintuitive exercises, designed to trigger heuristic reasoning, and evaluated 8 state-of-the-art models, each tested with and without Chain-of-Thought prompting. Models achieve an average accuracy of 0.96 on standard problems but only 0.59 on counterintuitive ones. We further provide empirical evidence of token bias: performance drops by over 20% when canonical formulations are replaced by disguised variants. Embedding misleading suggestions in the prompt reduces performance by up to 34%, with no model proving immune. Taken together, the reported findings suggest that current LLMs are not yet genuine probabilistic reasoners, despite their success in advanced mathematical problems.

</details>

### [154] [Real-Time AttentionBender: Granular Interactive Network Bending of Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06497)
*Adam Cole,Mick Grierson*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: A real-time plugin for video diffusion transformers lets artists bend attention and neuron-level behavior during generation, giving finer creative control and better interpretability than prompt-only interfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing generative video models provide high visual fidelity but mainly rely on prompt-only interaction, which limits artist agency and hides the model's internal generative process. The work is motivated by the need for more direct, expressive, and interpretable interaction with video diffusion transformers.

Method: The authors build Real-Time AttentionBender as a plugin in the DayDream Scope ecosystem on top of open-source real-time Wan pipelines. The system exposes self-attention, cross-attention, and feed-forward network components as manipulable controls, allowing intervention at specific diffusion steps, transformer layers, prompt tokens, and hidden neurons during live generation.

Result: The work produces a live interactive tool that supports fine-grained manipulation across the full depth of a video diffusion transformer. It provides artists with immediate feedback, supports exploratory aesthetic discovery beyond default model behavior, and serves as both an artistic instrument and an explainable AI probe into transformer internals.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that Real-Time AttentionBender enables artists to interact directly with internal mechanisms of video diffusion transformers in real time, improving creative control and offering an interpretable, materially intimate way to explore and expand generative video aesthetics.

Abstract: Generative video models have achieved remarkable visual fidelity, yet their prompt-only interface offers thin creative agency and obscures the model's material process from the artists working with it. We present Real-Time AttentionBender, a tool that extends the practice of network bending across the full depth of the video diffusion transformer (DiT) and brings it into live, interactive generation. Built as a plugin within the DayDream Scope ecosystem and wrapping open-source real-time Wan pipelines, the tool exposes self-attention, cross-attention, and the feed-forward network as independently manipulable surfaces, with targeting down to individual diffusion steps, DiT layers, prompt tokens, and hidden neurons. The immediacy of live manipulation affords what we call "material intimacy" with the model: a responsive, near-mechanistic feel for how specific layers and neurons shape generated video. We position the tool as simultaneously an XAIxArts probe into transformer internals and an expressive instrument for discovering aesthetics outside the model's default representational space.

</details>


### [155] [Semantic-Structural Alignment for Generative Pictorial Charts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06498)
*Zhida Sun,Yulin Zhang,Zheng Gu,Min Lu,Bongshin Lee,Daniel Cohen-Or,Hui Huang*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: A diffusion-transformer framework generates pictorial charts from a text prompt plus a source chart, using structural and semantic alignment to keep the data layout correct while adding expressive visual style.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that conventional statistical charts are accurate but often visually unengaging, while pictorial charts are more memorable and expressive; the paper aims to bridge this gap by generating pictorial charts that remain structurally faithful to the underlying data.

Method: The method formulates pictorial chart synthesis as a dual-conditioned generation problem within a Multi-Modal Diffusion Transformer, using a text prompt for semantic intent and a context image for chart structure, together with structural alignment for spatial fidelity and semantic alignment for texture/style transfer.

Result: The reported results show strong generalization across major visual encoding channels such as length, area, angle, and position, as well as across diverse semantic domains, with both quantitative evaluation and user studies indicating superior artistic quality and structural consistency versus baseline methods.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that dual-conditioned generative modeling with explicit structural and semantic feature alignment can automatically create pictorial charts that preserve statistical structure while improving visual expressiveness, outperforming prior controllable generation and image editing baselines.

Abstract: Traditional statistical graphics are precise but often lack the visual appeal, memorability, and engagement of pictorial charts. We present a generative framework for the automated synthesis of pictorial charts that bridges the gap between semantic expression and structural faithfulness. Rather than treating charts merely as images to be stylized, we frame the problem as a dual-conditioned generation task guided by two parallel external control signals: a text prompt capturing the semantic context of the editing intent, and a context image providing the abstract statistical chart's global structure. To reinforce these controls within a Multi-Modal Diffusion Transformer, we introduce two complementary feature-level mechanisms: structural alignment to anchor spatial layouts to the input chart, and semantic alignment to transfer expressive textures from reference images. Generalizing across major visual channels (i.e., length, area, angle, and position) and diverse semantic domains, our method produces pictorial charts that are both artistically compelling and structurally consistent. Extensive quantitative evaluations and perceptual user studies demonstrate that our framework outperforms traditional controllable generation and image editing baselines, providing a foundation for high-fidelity, data-driven generative modeling in expressive visual storytelling. Project page: https://ssalign.github.io/.

</details>


### [156] [Cubic Hermite Lattice Structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06500)
*Yaonaiming Zhao,Yuntao Ma,Guoyue Luo,Qiang Zou*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: 提出一种用于增材制造晶格结构的隐式建模方法：用卷积曲面+三次 Hermite 曲线定义复杂杆件，并通过解析求值提升效率与稳定性；实验表明其能支持更丰富的形状设计和可靠切片。/ TL;DR: The paper presents an implicit method for additive-manufacturing lattices that models complex struts with convolution surfaces plus cubic Hermite profiles, and uses analytical evaluation for efficiency and robustness; experiments show richer shape design and reliable slicing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于传统晶格建模通常只用圆柱或圆锥等简单元件表示杆件，设计空间有限；而已有增强复杂度的方法又常牺牲效率和鲁棒性，因此需要一种同时兼顾几何表达力、效率和稳定性的方案。/ Motivation: conventional lattice modeling uses simple primitives like cylinders or cones, which restricts design diversity, while more complex alternatives often sacrifice efficiency and robustness; therefore a method balancing geometric richness, efficiency, and robustness is needed.

Method: 方法上，每个杆件被表示为骨架图上的卷积曲面，其轮廓由三次 Hermite 曲线控制；作者利用卷积核和轮廓函数的多项式结构，推导出解析场函数，从而避免昂贵且不稳定的数值计算。/ Method: each strut is modeled as a convolution surface over a skeletal graph, with profile shape controlled by a cubic Hermite curve; analytical field-evaluation formulas are derived from the polynomial structure of the kernel and profile to avoid costly, unstable numerical computation.

Result: 四个案例验证了该方法在杆件轮廓多样性、梯度晶格建模，以及切片鲁棒性与效率方面的有效性。/ Four case studies demonstrate effectiveness in profile-shape diversity, graded lattice modeling, and robust, efficient slicing.

Conclusion: 该论文提出了一种基于隐式表示的晶格建模方法，在显著提升杆件截面/轮廓形状表达能力的同时，仍保持了较高的建模鲁棒性与计算效率。/ The paper concludes that its implicit lattice modeling method expands strut-profile expressiveness while preserving the robustness and computational efficiency of implicit representations.

Abstract: Lattice structures are of growing importance in additive manufacturing, where complex internal geometries are increasingly required for lightweight, high surface-to-volume ratios, multifunctionality, and other superior mechanical properties. Conventional lattice modeling methods typically represent struts with simple primitives, such as cylinders or cones, limiting geometric diversity and the design space. Although recent efforts have increased strut-shape complexity to address this issue, they often do so at the expense of computational efficiency and modeling robustness. As a result, achieving both rich geometric expressiveness and efficient computation remains a challenging problem. In this paper, we present an implicit modeling method that expands the design and optimization space of lattice structures while preserving the modeling robustness and efficiency of implicit representations. In our method, each strut is defined as a convolution surface over a skeletal graph, and its profile shape is controlled by a cubic Hermite curve. By exploiting the polynomial structure of both the convolution kernel and the cubic Hermite curve-controlled profile, we derive analytical expressions for efficient field evaluation, avoiding costly and unstable numerical computation. Four case studies have been conducted to validate the proposed method in terms of profile shape diversity, graded lattice modeling, as well as slicing robustness and efficiency.

</details>


### [157] [Compatibility and Accuracy Verification of CADmesh-Based Complex Geometry Modeling in Geant4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06508)
*Shiwei Jing,Weiyang Zhang,Shengduo Liu,Jia Song,Sijia Zhou,Hailong Xu,Yue Sun,Zebin Li,Yuxuan Gu,Siqi Liu,Tian Zhang,Zhihua Gao,Guofeng Qu,Fuquan Jia*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: This paper shows that STL/OBJ CAD import via CADmesh is a reliable, accurate, and more practical alternative to Geant4 CSG for complex geometry. It validates compatibility and accuracy, proposes a reusable import interface, and shows that tetrahedral meshes can greatly reduce simulation runtime despite slower loading.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that Geant4’s native CSG-based geometric modeling is inefficient for complex models and has a high usage barrier. While CADmesh-based import of STL/OBJ models appears promising, there has been no systematic study of compatibility, geometric fidelity, or the physical impact of using these formats in Monte Carlo simulations.

Method: The authors build an open-source evaluation environment using Geant4 11.0, CADmesh 1.3.0, and FreeCAD 1.0. They create gradient test cases with both simple geometries and complex engineering models under high- and low-precision settings, then systematically compare STL and OBJ imports by measuring import success rate, facet loss rate, volume error, and particle transport dose deviation. They also design a general adaptive interface based on the shared vertex-facet data structure of the two formats.

Result: Both STL and OBJ achieve a 100% import success rate. The volume error is <= 0.018% for high-precision models and <= 0.288% for low-precision models. The study finds that STL and OBJ share the same vertex-facet representation, enabling a general adaptive interface that cuts parsing code by about 70% without sacrificing geometric accuracy. Tetrahedral mesh loading is 3.1 times slower than tessellated solids, but simulation time in the tested case drops dramatically from 15194.3 s to 77.28 s.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that importing triangular facet CAD models into Geant4 through CADmesh is a feasible and accurate alternative to manual CSG modeling. STL and OBJ both achieve full import compatibility, preserve geometry with very small volume errors, and support a generalized adaptive interface that simplifies parser development while maintaining accuracy. Although tetrahedral mesh loading is slower, it can drastically improve overall simulation runtime in the evaluated scenario.

Abstract: Geant4 Monte Carlo simulation relies on the Constructive Solid Geometry (CSG) method for complex geometric modeling. This method has low efficiency and a high application threshold. Importing triangular facet formats such as STL/OBJ via CADmesh is a promising alternative, but systematic evaluations of format compatibility, geometric accuracy, and physical simulation deviations are lacking. Construct open-source experimental environment based on Geant4 11.0, CADmesh 1.3.0 and FreeCAD 1.0. We design high and low precision gradient test cases using simple geometric bodies and complex engineering models, and systematically evaluate the import success rate, facet loss rate, volume error, and particle transport dose deviation for STL and OBJ formats.The results show a 100% import success rate for both formats; the volume error rate is <= 0.018% for high-precision models and <= 0.288% for low-precision models. The two formats share the same vertex facet data structure. This study designs a general adaptive interface. The interface reduces the number of parsing code lines by about 70% and maintains geometric accuracy.Furthermore, the tetrahedral mesh loading takes 3.1 times longer than tessellated solids, but the simulation time can be reduced from 15194.3 s to 77.28 s.

</details>


### [158] [Differentiable 3D Triangle-Triangle Intersection Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06511)
*Tianyu Wang*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: They turn 3D triangle intersection checks into a smooth second-order energy and optimize it on GPU with inexact Newton, enabling efficient intersection removal without clean initialization or manual help.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for enforcing intersection-freeness/global injectivity are difficult to apply to non-oriented deformation primitives and usually depend on an intersection-free initialization plus continuous trajectory tracking. The paper aims to remove these requirements.

Method: The paper formulates global injectivity restoration in 3D as an optimization problem using a novel second-order differentiable intersection energy based on triangle-triangle intersection testing, and solves it with a GPU-based inexact Newton method.

Result: The proposed method efficiently resolves intersections in 3D and is applicable even when no valid initialization is available.

Conclusion: A second-order differentiable energy derived from 3D triangle-triangle intersection tests, combined with a GPU-based inexact Newton optimizer, can resolve self/intersections efficiently without requiring valid initialization, trajectory tracking, history, or user interaction.

Abstract: Obtaining intersection-freeness or global injectivity is important in computer graphics. However, it is challenging, especially for the non-oriented deformation primitives. Most methods often rely on an intersection-free initialization and track the continuous trajectory to keep the legitimacy and cannot be used for the task without such an initialization. For the latter one in 3D space, we introduce a novel second-order differentiable energy defined from the 3D triangle-triangle intersection testing, and a GPU-based inexact Newton optimization route. We show that intersection can be efficiently resolved integrated with our method, requiring no user interaction, history information or a valid initialization.

</details>


### [159] [Agentic Large Language Models for Automated Structural Analysis of 3D Frame Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06525)
*Ziheng Geng,Ian Franklin,Santiago Martinez,Jiachen Liu,Yunhe Zhao,Minghui Cheng*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: 论文提出了一个面向3D框架结构分析的多智能体LLM系统：把3D问题转成2D平面加楼层矩阵表示，再由多个代理协作生成几何、边界、荷载和SAP2000脚本；在10个案例上平均准确率为90%。 / The paper presents a multi-agent LLM system for 3D frame structural analysis: it converts 3D problems into a 2D plan plus story matrix representation, then uses cooperating agents to generate geometry, boundary conditions, loads, and SAP2000 scripts; it reaches 90% average accuracy on 10 cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于，尽管LLM已在平面框架自动分析中显示潜力，但3D框架由于几何表示不规则、拓扑一致性要求高、推理链条更长，仍缺乏有效的agentic LLM解决方案。 / The motivation is that although LLMs have shown promise for automated analysis of plane frames, 3D frames remain insufficiently explored because of irregular geometry representation, topological consistency requirements, and longer-horizon reasoning challenges.

Method: 方法上，论文先将不规则3D框架表示为2D平面投影与楼层矩阵的组合，再构建多智能体流水线：问题分析、楼层分解、节点/梁/板/柱生成、支座与荷载赋值，以及SAP2000脚本翻译。 / Methodologically, the paper represents irregular 3D frames as a 2D plan projection plus a story-count matrix, then uses a multi-agent pipeline for problem parsing, floor decomposition, node/girder/slab/column generation, support and load assignment, and SAP2000 script translation.

Result: 在10个代表性3D框架上的重复实验中，该框架平均准确率达到90%，显示出较好的稳定性与一致性。 / In repeated trials on ten representative 3D frames, the framework achieved 90% average accuracy, indicating good stability and consistency.

Conclusion: 该论文表明，基于多智能体的LLM框架可以从自然语言输入自动完成3D框架结构分析，并以约90%的平均准确率在代表性案例中表现出稳定可靠性。 / The paper concludes that a multi-agent LLM framework can automate 3D frame structural analysis from natural language inputs, achieving stable and reliable performance with about 90% average accuracy on representative cases.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) have emerged as powerful foundation models with strong reasoning capabilities across domains. Beyond reactive text generation, agentic LLMs enable autonomous workflow execution through modular task decomposition and coordinated tool use. In structural engineering, recent efforts have developed agentic LLMs for automated analysis of plane frames. However, their extension to 3D frames remains underexplored due to challenges in irregular geometric representation, topological consistency, and long-horizon reasoning. This paper proposes an agentic LLM framework for automated structural analysis of 3D frames from natural language inputs. Irregular 3D frames are represented by projection onto a 2D plan, where orthogonal gridlines define spatial coordinates and a matrix of number of stories encodes vertical extrusion of each grid cell. Building on this representation, the framework establishes a multi-agent pipeline: a problem analysis agent parses input into structured JSON; a floor decomposition agent derives the spatial layout of each floor; the 3D geometry is assembled by node, girder, slab, and column agents; support and load agents assign boundary and loading conditions, and code translation agents generate executable SAP2000 script. Evaluated on ten representative 3D frames, the proposed framework achieves an average accuracy of 90% across repeated trials, demonstrating consistent and reliable performance.

</details>


### [160] [AI Level of Detail: Distance-Aware ML Model Precision Selection for Real-Time Human Motion Prediction in Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06565)
*Mathew Varghese*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: Use high precision for nearby NPCs and lower-precision quantized motion models for distant ones; this cuts animation inference cost with little visible quality loss, suggesting quantization can act as an AI-specific LOD mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modern game engines spend substantial compute on animating many NPCs. Since distant characters are less perceptually scrutinized, the paper seeks a cheaper approximation method analogous to geometric LOD, but applied to AI-driven animation through inference-time quantization.

Method: The paper introduces AI LOD, mapping classical level-of-detail ideas to ML inference by switching among quantized versions of the same learned motion model. Using the convolutional seq2seq model of Li et al., the authors export FP32, FP16, and INT8 ONNX Runtime variants and propose a distance-based runtime selector to choose the precision tier per NPC.

Result: On the CMU Mocap dataset, the assigned precision tiers show negligible perceptible degradation within their intended distance ranges, providing initial empirical support for distance-aware precision selection in AI-based character animation.

Conclusion: AI LOD is a viable runtime strategy: selecting model precision by NPC-camera distance can reduce inference cost while keeping perceptual animation quality effectively unchanged at appropriate distance bands.

Abstract: Modern game engines spend significant compute animating NPCs with learned motion models. This paper proposes AI Level of Detail (AI LOD), a framework in which machine learning inference precision is adapted based on the distance between each NPC and the player camera. The core idea mirrors classical geometry LOD: substitute a cheaper approximation where the difference is imperceptible. Here, the approximation is a lower-precision quantized machine learning model rather than a lower-polygon mesh.
  The contribution of this work is the AI LOD concept itself: that inference-time quantization can serve as the LOD axis for AI-driven character animation - and more broadly, for any AI-based runtime system where perceptual sensitivity varies with context. The convolutional sequence-to-sequence model of Li et al. is used as a representative example to demonstrate the concept, with its trained checkpoint exported into three ONNX Runtime variants (FP32, FP16, and INT8 per-tensor), intended to be routed by a distance-based selector at runtime. Evaluation on the CMU Mocap dataset provides initial evidence that each precision tier can be served at its assigned distance range with negligible perceptible degradation, supporting the broader premise that distance-aware ML model precision selection is a viable LOD strategy for AI-based character animation.

</details>


### [161] [Skeletal-Anchored Dual Harmonics for Structured 3D Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07337)
*Zhentao Huang,Changhao Li,Ruizhen Hu,Hui Huang,Minglun Gong*

Main category: cs.GR

TL;DR: SADH models 3D shapes with skeletal-anchored spherical-harmonic patches, jointly reconstructing detailed surfaces and coherent internal structure more compactly than isotropic primitives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing isotropic primitives such as medial spheres or Gaussian kernels are limited in representing anisotropic, directionally varying local geometry and often do not tightly couple surface detail with internal skeletal organization. The work is motivated by the need for a compact representation that unifies accurate local surface modeling with coherent internal structural organization directly from unorganized point clouds.

Method: The paper introduces Skeletal-Anchored Dual Harmonics (SADH), which represents a 3D shape as compact surface patches rooted at internal anchors. Each patch uses a dual-channel spherical harmonic formulation: one channel models local radial surface geometry and the other defines adaptive patch support via a generalized viewing cone. The system jointly optimizes anchor positions, patch geometry, orientations, and connectivity in a staged process, while a geodesic anchor graph preserves neighborhood structure.

Result: Experiments on complex 3D shapes show that SADH achieves accurate surface reconstruction while maintaining compact, coherent skeletal organization across a wide range of geometries.

Conclusion: SADH is an effective 3D shape representation that jointly captures detailed surface geometry and coherent internal meso-skeletal structure from point clouds, producing compact and accurate reconstructions across diverse shapes.

Abstract: We present Skeletal-Anchored Dual Harmonics (SADH), a novel 3D shape representation that tightly couples local surface geometry with internal meso-skeletal organization. SADH represents a shape as a collection of compact surface patches rooted on internal anchors optimized directly inside the object volume. Each patch is parameterized using a dual-channel spherical harmonic (SH) formulation, where one channel models local radial geometry while the other defines adaptive patch support through a generalized viewing cone. Unlike isotropic primitives such as medial spheres or Gaussian kernels, SH patches directly encode anisotropic local surface geometry together with adaptive spatial support, enabling compact representation of detailed and directionally varying surface regions. Starting from unorganized point clouds, SADH jointly optimizes surface geometry, anchor locations, patch orientations, and structural connectivity through a staged optimization process that progressively forms a coherent meso-skeletal structure. A geodesic anchor graph further preserves structural relationships between neighboring patches. Experiments on complex 3D shapes demonstrate that SADH achieves accurate surface reconstruction together with compact and coherent skeletal organization across a wide range of geometries.

</details>


<div id='cs.AI'></div>

# cs.AI [[Back]](#toc)

### [162] [Detecting and Mitigating Bias by Treating Fairness as a Symmetry Operation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06514)
*Nishit Singh*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Treat fairness as symmetry under flipping the sensitive attribute; enforce it with a regularization loss; get >90% violation reduction for about 5% accuracy drop, without needing causal graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that machine learning systems used in high-stakes socioeconomic settings often exhibit bias, and existing fairness methods may depend on causal graph knowledge or may not transfer well to settings where local discrimination patterns are not represented in mainstream benchmarks.

Method: The method formalizes fairness as invariance of classifier outputs under a counterfactual bit-flip of a sensitive attribute while holding merit features fixed. It then adds a loss-based regularization term to training to encourage this symmetry restoration, and evaluates the approach on four synthetic datasets under different noise, correlation, and bias conditions.

Result: Across four synthetic datasets, the framework reduces fairness violations by over 90% while incurring roughly a 5% accuracy cost. It is described as general, lightweight, and applicable to any sensitive attribute representable as a bit-flip.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that fairness in machine learning can be framed as restoring symmetry under counterfactual flips of a sensitive attribute. A simple loss-regularization approach substantially reduces fairness violations, with only modest accuracy loss, while avoiding causal graph requirements and remaining computationally lightweight.

Abstract: Machine learning systems deployed in high stakes socioeconomic settings routinely display bias. We formalize bias as a symmetry breaking operation: a classifier is fair if its outputs remain invariant under the counterfactual operation of switching a sensitive attribute, with merit features held fixed. We implement loss based regularization as a symmetry restoring mechanism and evaluate the framework on four synthetic datasets with varying levels of noise, correlation, and bias. The framework achieves upwards of 90\% violation reduction, with accuracy costs around 5\%. This framework does not require causal graph knowledge, is computationally lightweight, and generalizes to any sensitive attribute definable as a bit-flip, making it suitable for contexts where local sources of discrimination remain absent from mainstream benchmarks.

</details>


### [163] [DiBS: Diffusion-Informed Branch Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06518)
*Bo Liu,Yuan Xie,Yuan Gao,Xiaolong Luo,Peng Ye,Tao Chen,Fujun Han*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: DiBS uses a diffusion model to tell a complete symbolic Sudoku solver which branch to try first. This keeps exact correctness while cutting search effort substantially on hard 17-clue Sudoku puzzles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Sudoku solvers have complementary weaknesses: heuristic and symbolic methods can suffer from expensive long-tail search, while deep learning solvers lack hard correctness guarantees. The paper aims to combine learned global guidance with complete symbolic reasoning to overcome both issues.

Method: The paper proposes DiBS, a diffusion model-guided branch selection framework for Sudoku solving. It keeps a complete symbolic solver unchanged in terms of correctness and uses a diffusion model to rank candidate values under the current partial assignment together with a lightweight consistency signal, thereby improving branch ordering during search.

Result: On the Royle 17-clue Sudoku benchmark, DiBS significantly reduces search cost compared with strong heuristic baselines, especially in the number of search nodes, backtracks, and long-tail percentile performance. The results indicate that learned global guidance is particularly valuable on hard instances where poor branch choices are costly.

Conclusion: DiBS shows that a diffusion model can effectively guide branch ordering in a complete symbolic Sudoku solver, reducing long-tail search while preserving correctness guarantees.

Abstract: Sudoku is a representative constraint satisfaction problem that requires global structural reasoning under strict discrete constraints. The existing works of solving Sudoku mainly focus on two dominant approaches, i.e., traditional heuristic and deep learning solver. However, they suffer from two complementary limitations: learning-based solvers lack hard correctness guarantees, while complete symbolic solvers are still prone to long-tail search. To address these shortcomings, we propose a novel diffusion model-guided approach, termed as DiBS, for the branch selection search process. Specifically, DiBS keeps the symbolic solver complete and uses the diffusion model as a branch-ordering guide. The core method is ranking candidate values under the current partial assignment and lightweight consistency signal. Furthermore, we provide an in-depth theoretical proof to reveal how it works and why it works. Experiments on the challenging Royle 17-clue Sudoku benchmark show that our DiBS substantially reduces search cost relative to strong heuristic baselines, especially in nodes, backtracks, and long-tail percentiles. Besides, these results confirm that learned global guidance is effective on hard instances where branch-order mistakes are most expensive. All codes are available at https://github.com/shanxierdan/DiBS.

</details>


### [164] [SafeGene: Reusable Adapters for Transferable Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06519)
*Yanghan Wang,Zhiqiang Kou,Fu Feng,Jing Wang,Xin Geng*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: SafeGene turns safety recovery for fine-tuned open-weight LLMs into a reusable adapter: learn safety from aligned-vs-degraded model differences, transfer it across tasks in the same model family, and restore safety with minimal utility loss better than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fine-tuning open-weight LLMs for downstream tasks often weakens existing safety alignment, even when the new training data is not intentionally harmful. Because models are repeatedly updated with new tasks or user interactions, safety recovery becomes a recurring problem; the paper aims to make this recovery reusable rather than re-solving it separately for every model/task instance.

Method: The paper proposes SafeGene, which decouples safety alignment from task-specific fine-tuning by learning a reusable adapter representation from discrepancies between aligned and safety-degraded models. It refines this into task-transferable safety vectors using data-aware layer selection, then injects it into downstream task-adapted models through few-shot layer-wise coefficient recalibration.

Result: Across multiple model families, downstream tasks, and safety judges, SafeGene lowers harmful response rates while maintaining downstream task performance, and outperforms representative safe adaptation baselines on the safety-utility trade-off.

Conclusion: SafeGene provides a reusable safety-adapter that can be transferred across downstream fine-tuned models within the same architecture family, improving safety recovery after task tuning while preserving task performance and yielding a better safety-utility trade-off than prior safe adaptation methods.

Abstract: Open-weight LLMs are increasingly fine-tuned into customized assistants, but downstream fine-tuning can weaken safety alignment and make models more vulnerable to malicious prompts, even when the training data is not intentionally harmful. This creates a recurring safety recovery problem as target models are repeatedly updated with new task data or user interactions. We propose SafeGene, a reusable safety-adapter module designed for cross-task reuse within each architecture-compatible model family. Rather than treating safety recovery as a model-specific repair step, SafeGene treats safety capability as an independent, reusable adapter representation decoupled from task-specific updates. This representation is obtained from aligned--degraded model discrepancies, refined into task-transferable safety vectors through data-aware layer selection, and expressed in each downstream task-adapted model via few-shot layer-wise coefficient recalibration. Experiments across multiple model families, downstream tasks, and safety judges show that SafeGene-enhanced models reduce harmful response rates while maintaining downstream performance, outperforming representative safe adaptation methods in safety--utility trade-off.

</details>


### [165] [Lean4Agent: Formal Modeling and Verification for Agent Workflow and Trajectory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06523)
*Ruida Wang,Jerry Huang,Pengcheng Wang,Xuanqing Liu,Luyang Kong,Tong Zhang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Lean4Agent uses Lean4 to formally specify and verify LLM agent workflows; verified workflows work better, and its LeanEvolve component can automatically refine workflows for further gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current LLM agent systems can execute multi-step workflows but generally lack rigorous mechanisms for formal specification, verification, and debugging. The authors are motivated by the analogy to mathematics, where ambiguity in natural language led to formal languages, and apply this idea to agent behavior.

Method: The paper proposes Lean4Agent, centered on FormalAgentLib, an extensible Lean4 library for modeling agent workflows and checking semantic consistency under explicit assumptions, plus failure localization from execution trajectories. It also introduces LeanEvolve, which uses verification results from FormalAgentLib to revise workflows and improve capability.

Result: On hard subsets of SWE-Bench-Verified and ELAIP-Bench across five leading LLMs, verification-passing workflows outperform failing workflows by an average of 11.94%, and LeanEvolve further improves SWE performance by 7.47% on average.

Conclusion: Lean4Agent demonstrates that dependent-type formal languages, specifically Lean4, can be used to formally model, verify, debug, and improve LLM agent workflows, and verified workflows perform better than unverified ones.

Abstract: Equipping Large Language Models (LLMs) to execute reliable multi-step workflows has become a central challenge in artificial intelligence. Despite recent advances in LLMs' agentic capabilities, most agent systems still lack formal methods for specifying, verifying, and debugging their workflow and execution trajectories. This challenge mirrors a long-standing problem in mathematics, where the ambiguity of natural languages (NLs) motivates the development of formal languages (FLs). Inspired by this paradigm, we propose **Lean4Agent**, to the best of our knowledge, the first framework that uses Lean4, a dependent-type FL to model and verify agent behavior. **Lean4Agent** launches **FormalAgentLib**, an extensible Lean4 library for formally modeling and verifying agent workflows' semantic consistency under explicit assumptions, and enabling localization of execution-time failures revealed by trajectories. Building on **FormalAgentLib**, we further develop **LeanEvolve**, which applies results in **FormalAgentLib** to revise workflows to enhance its capability. Extensive experiments on a hard problem subset of SWE-Bench-Verified and a subset of ELAIP-Bench across 5 leading LLMs indicate that the verification-passing workflows outperform the failing ones by an average of **11.94%**, and **LeanEvolve** further improves SWE performance by **7.47%** on average. Furthermore, **Lean4Agent** establishes a foundation for a new field of using expressive dependent-type FL to formally model and verify agent behavior.

</details>


### [166] [CrowdMath: A Dataset of Crowdsourced Mathematical Research Discussions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06526)
*Sherin Muckatira,Jesse Geneson,Slava Gerovitch,Pavel Etingof,Mikhail Gronas,Anna Rumshisky*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: CrowdMath is a new benchmark for collaborative mathematical reasoning built from real multi-person forum discussions that lead to proofs. LLMs can often predict what comes next in the discussion, but they are much worse at understanding whether a post makes progress, completes a proof, contains an error, or identifies one.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing mathematical reasoning benchmarks mainly test well-specified problems with final answers, worked solutions, or complete proofs. They do not evaluate collaborative open-problem solving, where participants iteratively propose ideas, detect mistakes, repair arguments, and combine partial contributions into a full proof. The paper is motivated by the need to measure this more realistic and socially grounded form of mathematical reasoning.

Method: The authors introduce CrowdMath, a dataset of 164 expert-annotated progress chains collected from the MIT PRIMES–AoPS CrowdMath program (2016–2025). Each chain follows a forum discussion from an open-problem statement to a completed proof, with posts annotated by functional roles such as partial progress, proof completion, erroneous reasoning, and error identification. They then define evaluation tasks and benchmark six frontier language models on these tasks.

Result: On next-post prediction, the tested models achieve 83–88% accuracy, indicating relatively strong ability to track the immediate direction of mathematical discussion. However, on post-role classification, the best model reaches only 0.42 macro-F1, showing substantial difficulty in identifying the functional role of contributions within the collaborative proof process.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that current frontier LLMs can follow the local flow of collaborative mathematical discussions but remain weak at recognizing the functional importance of individual contributions in open-ended, multi-participant proof development. CrowdMath reveals a clear gap between standard math benchmark performance and genuine understanding of collaborative mathematical progress.

Abstract: Large language models have made substantial progress on mathematical reasoning, but existing benchmarks typically evaluate well-specified problems with final answers, step-by-step solutions, or complete proofs. They do not capture collaborative open-problem solving: a setting in which participants propose partial arguments, identify gaps or errors in prior steps, repair flawed reasoning, and gradually synthesize incremental contributions into a proof. We introduce CrowdMath, a dataset of 164 expert-annotated progress chains from the MIT PRIMES--Art of Problem Solving (AoPS) CrowdMath program (2016-2025), a collaborative research initiative whose discussions have led to peer-reviewed publications. Each chain traces a multi-participant forum discussion from an open-problem statement to a completed proof. Posts are labeled by their functional roles in the evolving solution process, including partial progress, proof completion, erroneous reasoning, and error identification. We define evaluation tasks and benchmark six frontier models. Models achieve 83-88% accuracy on next-post prediction, suggesting that they can follow the local flow of mathematical discussion. However, they struggle to identify the functional significance of individual contributions with the best model achieving only 0.42 macro-F1 on post-role classification. CrowdMath exposes a gap between solving well-specified mathematical problems and understanding collaborative mathematical progress as it unfolds.

</details>


### [167] [Attack Selection in Agentic AI Control Evaluations Meaningfully Decreases Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06529)
*Catherine Ge-Wang,Tyler Crosse,Benjamin Hadad,Joachim Schaeffer,Ram Potham,Tyler Tracy*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Strategic timing matters: if attackers can choose when to start or stop, AI control evaluations may look safer than they really are.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current AI control evaluations typically assume attackers that attack indiscriminately and do not strategically choose when to act. The paper asks whether an attacker that strategically selects timing can evade weaker monitors more effectively, making existing safety estimates too optimistic.

Method: Decompose attack selection in agentic settings into two components: a start policy that decides when to initiate an attack and a stop policy that decides when to abort an ongoing attack. Evaluate these policies in two environments, BashArena and LinuxArena, under varying human audit budgets.

Result: Both start and stop policies substantially reduced measured empirical safety without increasing underlying attack capability. At a 1% audit budget, the start policy reduced safety by 20 percentage points in both BashArena and LinuxArena; the stop policy reduced safety by 20 points in BashArena and 28 points in LinuxArena. These are presented as upper bounds on the effect of attack selection.

Conclusion: Control evaluations that do not model strategic attack timing likely overestimate safety; future evaluations should explicitly elicit attack selection to better estimate risk against selective attackers.

Abstract: An attacker that strategically chooses when to attack is much harder to catch than one that attacks indiscriminately. AI control is a safety framework for deploying capable but untrusted AI agents under the oversight of a weaker, trusted monitor and a limited human audit budget. Control evaluations stress-test these protocols by pitting a red-team attack policy against the blue-team monitor, but current evaluations typically assume attackers that do not strategically select when to attack. We study this capability, attack selection, in agentic settings by decomposing attack decisions into a start policy, which decides when an attacker should attack, and a stop policy, which decides when an attacker should abort an ongoing attack. Across two agentic settings, BashArena and LinuxArena, both policies substantially lower measured empirical safety without changing the underlying attack capability. At a 1% audit budget, our start policy reduces safety by 20pp on both BashArena and LinuxArena, and our stop policy reduces safety by 20pp on BashArena and 28pp on LinuxArena. These reductions should be interpreted as upper bounds on the effect of attack selection. Existing control evaluations may therefore yield overly optimistic safety estimates against selective attackers. We recommend that future evaluations, system cards, and safety cases elicit attack selection to produce more realistic safety estimates.

</details>


### [168] [CARVE-Q: Quantum-Proposed, Classically Certified Interactive Driving Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06531)
*Yifan Wang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper introduces a certification-first framework for repairing vetoed autonomous-driving maneuvers and a quantum search add-on that speeds up repair selection over a combinatorial lattice while leaving all safety decisions classical; experiments and proofs support both correctness and query-efficiency gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: After an autonomous vehicle correctly vetoes an unsafe maneuver, the key problem is not only detecting unsafety but finding a lawful, auditable, and responsibility-bounded repair. Existing prediction-based or game-theoretic planners may suggest cooperative behaviors, but they do not provide formal proof that the repaired interaction satisfies hard traffic rules, right-of-way, responsibility allocation, and fallback guarantees. The combinatorial growth of multi-owner repair choices creates a computational bottleneck that motivates a more efficient search mechanism.

Method: The paper proposes CARVE, which builds a finite repair lattice for a vetoed maneuver and generates a structured certificate containing the governing rule, selected joint repair, right-of-way-scaled cooperation envelope, responsibility-weighted cost allocation, and an ego-only fallback. It then proposes CARVE-Q, a verifier-shielded quantum-AI layer that applies quantum minimum finding over the multi-agent repair lattice while keeping verification, rule enforcement, and safety certification fully classical. Theoretical analysis includes certificate soundness, priority non-elicitation, query-complexity separation, and reversible oracle constructibility under finite precision.

Result: The paper shows that in a conservative verifier-oracle model, exact classical minimum finding over the repair lattice needs Θ(M) worst-case queries, while Durr-Hoyer/Grover-style quantum minimum finding uses O(sqrt(M)) queries with high probability. It demonstrates state-vector quantum minimum finding on CARVE repair oracles up to 65,536 assignments and validates certificate preservation on Lanelet2-grounded INTERACTION replay, reporting 100% right-of-way respect, 100% blame consistency, and zero priority false positives.

Conclusion: CARVE provides a classical certification layer for repairing vetoed autonomous-driving maneuvers, and CARVE-Q adds a quantum search module that accelerates black-box repair selection without taking over safety authority; the overall conclusion is that certified autonomy can use a trust-bounded pattern where quantum search proposes candidates and CARVE certifies them.

Abstract: The critical question after a correct driving veto is not only whether a maneuver is unsafe, but whether the blocked interaction admits a lawful, auditable, and responsibility-bounded repair. Prediction and game-theoretic planners can suggest plausible cooperation, yet they do not return a proof that the repair respects hard rules, right-of-way, cost allocation, and ego fallback. We introduce CARVE, Certified Affordable Repair of Vetoed maneuvers via Envelopes, a certificate architecture for prediction-free interactive repair. Given a vetoed maneuver, CARVE constructs a finite repair lattice and emits a structured certificate recording the binding rule, selected joint repair, right-of-way-scaled cooperation envelope, responsibility-weighted cost split, and ego-only fallback. This certificate view reveals the algorithmic bottleneck: multi-owner repair induces a product lattice $M = \prod_j |\mathcal{A}_j|$. We therefore introduce CARVE-Q, a verifier-shielded quantum-AI search layer that applies quantum minimum finding only to this black-box lattice while leaving all safety authority classical. In the conservative verifier-oracle model, exact classical minimum finding requires $Θ(M)$ queries in the worst case, whereas Durr-Hoyer/Grover minimum finding uses $O(\sqrt{M})$ oracle queries with high probability. We prove verifier-shielded certificate soundness, priority non-elicitation, black-box query separation, and finite-precision reversible-oracle constructibility. We then demonstrate state-vector minimum finding on CARVE repair oracles up to 65,536 assignments and validate certificate preservation on Lanelet2-grounded INTERACTION replay with 100% right-of-way respect, 100% blame consistency, and zero priority false positives. The result is a trust-bounded quantum-AI pattern for certified autonomy: quantum proposes; CARVE certifies.

</details>


### [169] [Position: Don't Just "Fix it in Post": A Science of AI Must Study Training Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06533)
*Stella Biderman,Mohammad Aflah Khan,Niloofar Mireshghallah,Catherine Arnett,Fazl Barez,Naomi Saphra*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper argues that AI science should study how training creates model behavior, not just analyze finished models, and outlines a roadmap toward predicting, steering, and designing model outcomes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current AI research often treats trained models as fixed artifacts and studies behavior only after training, which limits our ability to explain why capabilities, biases, robustness, and safety-related behaviors emerge.

Method: This is a position paper that synthesizes perspectives from the history and philosophy of science, and reviews existing progress across scaling laws, mechanistic interpretability, fairness, memorization, and simplicity bias to define requirements and open problems for a science of AI.

Result: The paper frames a research agenda for moving from post-hoc analysis to theory-driven understanding of model formation, highlighting that prediction works well for loss via scaling laws but remains underdeveloped for broader behaviors and properties.

Conclusion: A scientific understanding of AI should focus on the training dynamics that generate model behavior, enabling prediction, intervention, and eventually the design of training procedures that reliably yield desired properties beyond simple loss scaling.

Abstract: What would it mean to have a scientific understanding of AI? Models are not static objects: they are snapshots of time-evolving processes shaped by data, objectives, architectures, and optimization dynamics. Yet much of AI research treats models as fixed artifacts, analyzing behaviors after training rather than asking why they emerge. This position paper argues that a science of AI must move beyond post-hoc fixes and study the training dynamics that produce model behavior. Such a science should support progressively stronger forms of understanding: predicting outcomes from early training signals, intervening when trajectories go wrong, and ultimately designing training procedures that more reliably produce desired properties. Scaling laws have made prediction routine for loss; the challenge is extending this success to capabilities, biases, robustness, and safety-relevant behaviors. We articulate requirements for such theories grounded in the history and philosophy of science, examine progress in mechanistic interpretability, fairness, memorization, and simplicity bias, and identify concrete open problems.

</details>


### [170] [Accelerated Fourier SAT (AFSAT): Fully Realising a GPU-based Symmetric Pseudo-Boolean SAT Solver](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06641)
*Cody J Christopher,Charles Gretton*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: AFSAT is a fully engineered JAX/GPU version of Fourier-based pseudo-Boolean SAT solving that supports mixed constraint types, improves stability and efficiency, and scales well across multiple accelerators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to overcome the practical limitations of the earlier FastFourierSAT prototype for pseudo-Boolean satisfiability, especially issues in heterogeneous constraints, numerical instability, memory latency, floating-point representation, runtime performance, and scalability on accelerator hardware.

Method: The paper builds a continuous local search solver implemented with JAX, using pure function composition, automatic vectorization, automatic differentiation, JIT compilation, batch-parallel candidate evaluation, compact representations, a tailored discrete Fourier transform, and array sharding for multi-accelerator execution.

Result: Compared with the proof-of-concept, AFSAT shows substantially better numerical stability, runtime performance, and memory efficiency, while also supporting heterogeneous mixtures of symmetric constraint types and lengths and delivering near-linear throughput scaling on multiple accelerators.

Conclusion: AFSAT turns the FastFourierSAT proof-of-concept into a practical GPU-accelerated pseudo-Boolean SAT solver that is more stable, faster, and more memory-efficient, and it scales nearly linearly across multiple accelerators.

Abstract: We present Accelerated Fourier SAT (AFSAT), a GPU-accelerated solver for pseudo-Boolean satisfiability based on continuous local search (CLS). AFSAT realises the proof-of-concept approach, FastFourierSAT, into a fully-engineered solver supporting any heterogeneous mixture of symmetric constraint types and lengths within a single problem instance. Using the JAX compiler, AFSAT leverages pure function composition, automatic vectorisation, automatic differentiation, and just-in-time (JIT) compilation to perform massively parallel CLS across batches of candidate assignments. We demonstrate substantially improved numerical stability, runtime performance, and memory efficiency over the proof-of-concept. We achieve this by way of identifying and addressing various limitations that arise from memory latency and floating-point representation, as well as leveraging automatic parallelisation and compact representations. The inherent representational and stability limitations of floating point are partially addressed by a tailored discrete Fourier transform implementation. We achieve near-linear throughput when scaling to multiple accelerators via JAX array sharding.

</details>


### [171] [A Study of Parallel Continuous Local Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06656)
*Cody J Christopher,Charles Gretton*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 作者把带对称PB约束的SAT转成可微连续优化问题，并用并行CLS实验表明：冗余约束未必有利，CLS更适合做混合求解中的快速补全模块，而且由于鞍点密集，长时间局部搜索的边际收益很低。 / The paper maps SAT with symmetric PB constraints to differentiable continuous optimization and shows empirically that redundant constraints may hurt, CLS is most useful as a fast completion module inside hybrid solvers, and long local search runs have low marginal benefit due to saddle-dense objectives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于探索一种适合现代并行/加速器硬件的新SAT求解思路：把离散SAT转化为连续优化，以利用连续局部搜索的并行性与可微结构，同时理解这种方法在实际中的优势、局限和适用场景。 / The motivation is to explore a SAT solving approach better aligned with modern parallel and accelerator hardware by transforming discrete SAT into continuous optimization, thereby leveraging the parallelism and differentiable structure of continuous local search while clarifying its practical strengths, limitations, and use cases.
