Method: 论文将含有n个变量的伪布尔可满足性问题松弛为n维超立方体上的连续优化问题，并构造可微目标函数，使得在可满足实例上，其全局极小点对应原SAT问题的满足赋值。随后作者通过并行CLS进行经验实验，评估冗余约束、部分赋值补全能力以及局部搜索收敛行为。 / The paper relaxes an n-variable pseudo-Boolean satisfiability problem into a continuous optimization problem over an n-dimensional hypercube with a differentiable objective whose global minimizers correspond to satisfying assignments for satisfiable instances. It then uses parallel CLS in empirical experiments to study the effect of redundant constraints, the ability to complete partial assignments, and convergence behavior.

Result: 实验发现三点：1）冗余约束可能抑制而不是加速收敛；2）CLS在混合求解框架中作为子求解器很有前景，能快速补全部分赋值；3）由于目标函数鞍点密集，局部搜索会很快收敛到稳定的解质量分布，继续增加迭代步数收益递减。 / The experiments report three main findings: (1) redundant constraints can inhibit rather than accelerate convergence; (2) CLS is promising as a sub-solver in hybrid settings, where it quickly completes partial assignments; and (3) because the objectives are saddle-dense, local search rapidly reaches a stable distribution of solution quality, after which extra solver steps provide diminishing returns.

Conclusion: 并行连续局部搜索（CLS）在带对称伪布尔约束的SAT问题上具有实际潜力，但其有效使用依赖于问题结构与混合求解方式；尤其是在现代加速器硬件上，CLS更适合作为子求解器而非单独长时间迭代的全能求解器。 / Parallel CLS is practically promising for SAT with symmetric pseudo-Boolean constraints, but its utility depends strongly on problem structure and hybrid usage; on modern accelerator hardware it appears better suited as a sub-solver than as a standalone solver run for long iterations.

Abstract: We study parallel Continuous Local Search (CLS) as a solution approach for Boolean satisfiability problems with symmetric pseudo-Boolean (PB) constraints. Here, the $n$-variable PB-satisfiability problem is relaxed to a continuous optimisation problem with a differentiable objective function on an $n$-dimensional hypercube. For satisfiable instances, the global minimisers of this optimisation problem correspond to satisfying assignments of the SAT problem at hand. We present several novel findings via empirical experiments: (i) redundant constraints can inhibit rather than accelerate convergence; (ii) CLS shows promise as a sub-solver in hybridised settings, quickly completing partial assignments; and (iii) local search rapidly converges to a stable distribution of solution quality (i.e., degree of satisfaction), due to saddle-dense objectives where additional solver steps yield diminishing returns. Our findings inform practical uses of CLS for SAT on modern accelerator hardware.

</details>


### [172] [AEGIS: A Backup Reflex for Physical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06660)
*Josef Chen*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: AEGIS watches a weak robot policy’s internal activations, predicts failure early, and switches to a stronger policy only when needed. This improves recovery of failing long-horizon manipulation trajectories versus matched baselines, with gains coming from better timing rather than more compute.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long-horizon robot manipulation often fails progressively: early mistakes push the system into unrecoverable states. Since these failures are often detectable before they fully occur, the paper is motivated by the idea of intervening early and selectively instead of always paying the cost of a stronger policy.

Method: The paper proposes AEGIS, which trains a lightweight activation probe on frozen internal activations of a weak policy to predict imminent failure early in a trajectory. When the probe detects a high-risk step, execution is gated to a separate stronger policy only for those steps, enabling selective intervention rather than full-time escalation.

Result: On LIBERO-Spatial, AEGIS recovers 10.1% of trajectories lost by the weak policy, compared with 4.6% for budget-matched blind escalation and 5.1% for random triggering. It uses the stronger policy on only 38% of steps. The probe achieves an early-window AUROC of 0.764 (95% CI [0.70, 0.84]), and the performance gains are reported as statistically significant under the pre-registered analysis.

Conclusion: AEGIS can selectively escalate from a weak robot-manipulation policy to a stronger one at high-risk moments, recovering substantially more failing trajectories than compute-matched or random baselines while using the stronger policy on only a minority of steps.

Abstract: Long-horizon robot manipulation tends to fail gradually: one bad step degrades the state, and the policy spirals into a basin from which it cannot recover. The failure is often visible before it happens. We introduce AEGIS (Activation-probe Early-warning, Gated Inference Switching), a selective escalation method that uses a lightweight probe on a weak policy's frozen activations to detect high-risk steps while there is still time to act. When the probe flags a step, control switches to a stronger separate policy, but only for the steps that need it. On LIBERO-Spatial, AEGIS recovers 10.1% of the trajectories the weak policy alone loses, versus 4.6% for budget-matched blind escalation and 5.1% for a random-trigger placebo. These gains are significant under one-sided exact paired McNemar tests with Holm-Bonferroni adjustment over three pre-registered contrasts: +5.4pp over blind escalation, p=8.5e-6; +5.0pp over random triggering, p=1.0e-4; paired-trajectory bootstrap CIs exclude zero. AEGIS activates the stronger policy on only 38% of steps, so the lever is timing rather than compute. The probe clears its precondition with an early-window AUROC of 0.764, 95% CI [0.70, 0.84], read from the weak-policy path over the first 30% of trajectory steps before any handoff. We pre-register the full analysis plan, including a conditional recovered-task-rate estimand and explicit kill criteria, and confirm the result on 700 common-random-number episodes per arm, with nA-fail=646.

</details>


### [173] [A Geometric Account of Activation Steering through Angle-Norm Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06735)
*Georgii Aparin,Tatiana Gaintseva*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper shows that steering works through two coupled geometric factors: direction and norm. Concepts live mostly in direction, but norm still affects robustness, so steering should tune angular and radial parts separately instead of using one additive strength.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior spherical steering methods assume hidden-state norm contains little concept-relevant information and aim to fix limitations of additive steering. The paper revisits this assumption and asks how angular versus radial geometry actually contributes to steering behavior.

Method: The paper conducts a controlled empirical study across seven language models to disentangle angular and radial effects in steering interventions, comparing linear/additive and spherical steering through their impact on concept alignment and hidden-state norm.

Result: Across seven models, the authors find that concept information is represented mainly in angular structure, supporting spherical steering motivation, but hidden-state norm remains important for the stability and downstream effects of interventions. This explains why methods with similar concept-level changes can behave differently.

Conclusion: Activation steering should be parameterized separately by interpretable angular and radial components, because concepts are mostly encoded in angular structure while hidden-state norm still matters for stability and downstream behavior.

Abstract: Linear activation steering has gained popularity as a simple and empirically effective way to control language model behavior. More recently, spherical steering paradigms have been proposed to address limitations of additive interventions, often motivated by the assumption that hidden-state norm does not carry concept-relevant information. In this work, we revisit this assumption through a controlled empirical study designed to disentangle the roles of angular and radial components. We show that steering methods differ mainly in how they couple two geometric effects: changing a token's angular alignment with a concept direction and changing its hidden-state norm. Across seven language models, we find that concepts are represented primarily in angular structure, supporting the motivation for spherical methods, but that norm remains important for the stability and downstream effects of steering. Our results explain why interventions with similar concept-level effects can behave differently, and suggest that activation steering should be parameterized by interpretable angular and radial components of the intervention, rather than by a single additive coefficient that entangles these two effects.

</details>


### [174] [OpenSkill: Open-World Self-Evolution for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06741)
*Zhiling Yan,Dingjie Song,Hanrong Zhang,Wei Liang,Yuxuan Zhang,Yutong Dai,Lifang He,Philip S. Yu,Ran Xu,Xiang Li,Lichao Sun*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: OpenSkill lets deployed agents teach themselves in the open world by mining external resources, building their own practice tasks and verification signals, and learning transferable skills without target-task supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing self-evolving agent methods assume an available learning loop with curated skills, successful trajectories, or verifier signals, but real open-world deployment may provide only a task prompt and no supervision. The work aims to remove this assumption and study how agents can self-improve from scratch in the open world.

Method: The paper proposes OpenSkill, a framework that gathers grounded knowledge and verification anchors from documentation, code repositories, and the web; synthesizes them into reusable skills; and iteratively improves these skills using self-constructed virtual tasks grounded in the anchors instead of target-task answers.

Result: Across three benchmarks and two target agents, OpenSkill achieves the best automated pass rate under the no-supervision constraint. Its learned skills transfer across models without model-specific adaptation, and its self-built verifier correlates well with ground-truth outcomes despite never seeing them during training.

Conclusion: OpenSkill enables open-world self-evolution without target-task supervision by constructing both transferable skills and self-built verification signals from open-world resources, achieving the best automated pass rate across benchmarks while maintaining verifier alignment with true outcomes.

Abstract: Self-evolving agents requires adaptation after deployment, but existing approaches assume a usable learning loop, such as curated skills, successful trajectories, or verifier signals. Real open-world deployments may provide none of these, offering only a task prompt. In this work, we study open-world self-evolution, where an agent must build both its skills and its own verification signals from scratch, using open-world resources but no target-task supervision. We propose OpenSkill, a framework that bootstraps this loop: it acquires grounded knowledge and verification anchors from documentation, repositories, and the web, synthesizes them into transferable skills, and refines those skills against self-built virtual tasks grounded in the anchors rather than in target answers. The open world thus supplies both the knowledge to be learned and a supervision-independent practice environment, with target-task supervision reserved for final evaluation. Across three benchmarks and two target agents, OpenSkill attains the best automated pass rate while satisfying the no-supervision constraint. Analysis shows its skills transfer across models without model-specific adaptation, and its self-built verifier aligns with ground-truth outcomes despite never accessing them.

</details>


### [175] [AdMem: Advanced Memory for Task-solving Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06787)
*Runzhe Wang,Huilin Lu,Shengjie Liu,Li Dong,Jason Zhu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 论文提出一种统一的LLM智能体记忆框架，把语义、情景和程序记忆放进短期+长期的双层系统中，并通过actor/memory/critic多智能体机制自动生成、评估和检索记忆；实验显示它在长时程多轮任务中比现有方法更稳健、更容易成功。
The paper proposes a unified memory framework for LLM agents that combines semantic, episodic, and procedural memory in a short-term/long-term bi-level system, using actor/memory/critic agents to automatically generate, evaluate, and retrieve memories; experiments show stronger robustness and higher success rates on long-horizon multi-turn tasks than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：虽然LLM已展现出工具使用能力，但在需要长期记忆、知识组织与复用的长时程任务中仍表现不足。已有工作多聚焦事实性存储，或仅重放历史成功经验，难以处理失败案例，也缺乏在线扩展能力，因此需要一个更全面、自动化的记忆机制。
The motivation is that although LLMs show promise as tool-using agents, they still struggle with long-horizon tasks requiring memory, organization, and reuse of knowledge. Prior work focuses mainly on factual storage or simply replaying past successes, which fails to address failures and online scalability, motivating a more comprehensive and automated memory mechanism.

Method: 方法上，作者提出一个自动化统一记忆框架：采用双层设计，将短期记忆与长期记忆结合；在架构上使用actor、memory、critic三个agent协同完成记忆生成、奖励标注和自适应检索；长期记忆通过基于奖励的评估、合并与剪枝进行管理，以支持持续学习和可扩展性。
Methodologically, the authors propose an automatic unified memory framework with a bi-level design that combines short-term and long-term memory. A multi-agent architecture with actor, memory, and critic agents supports memory creation, reward annotation, and adaptive retrieval. Long-term memory is managed through reward-based evaluation, merging, and pruning for continual learning and scalability.

Result: 实验结果表明，在多个环境中的长多轮任务上，该方法相较现有基线提升了鲁棒性和成功率，说明综合且自适应的记忆设计对LLM智能体性能具有明显帮助。
Results show that across multiple environments and long multi-turn tasks, the approach improves robustness and success rates over existing baselines, demonstrating the value of comprehensive and adaptive memory for LLM agents.

Conclusion: 该论文认为，面向长时程任务的LLM智能体需要一种统一、可自适应且可持续扩展的综合记忆系统；结合语义、情景和程序记忆，并通过奖励驱动的长期管理，可以显著提升鲁棒性与任务成功率。
The paper concludes that LLM agents handling long-horizon tasks need a unified, adaptive, and scalable memory system; integrating semantic, episodic, and procedural memory with reward-driven long-term management significantly improves robustness and task success.

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) show promise as tool-using agents but remain limited in long-horizon tasks that require remembering, organizing, and reusing knowledge. Prior memory approaches aim to resolve the situation, but mainly focus on storing factual information. Recent work on procedural memory improves task reuse, yet often reduces to replaying past successes without addressing failure cases or online scalability. We introduce a unified and automatic memory framework that integrates semantic, episodic, and procedural memory in a bi-level design combining short-term and long-term stores. A multi-agent architecture with actor, memory, and critic agents enables automatic memory generation, reward annotation, and adaptive retrieval. Long-term memory is managed through reward-based evaluation, merging, and pruning, ensuring scalability and continual improvement. Experiments across various environments show that our approach improves robustness and success on long multi-turn tasks compared to existing baselines. This work highlights the importance of comprehensive, adaptive memory for advancing LLM-based agents.

</details>


### [176] [Evidence-Based Intelligent Diagnostic and Therapeutic Visualization System with Large Language Models: Multi-Turn Interaction and Multimodal Treatment Plan Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06869)
*Yunhan Wang,Yuda Wang,Zhiying Tu,Mingqiang Song,Li Song,Kun Li,Dianhui Chu,Bolin Zhang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: A knowledge-enhanced, visually interactive AI system for TCM diagnosis uses a Neo4j knowledge graph, proactive questioning, and multimodal treatment visualization to make diagnostic reasoning more transparent and trustworthy, with measurable gains in trust, output standardization, and cognitive usability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing AI-assisted TCM diagnostic tools have opaque reasoning, passive interaction, and weak treatment presentation, which limits transparency, interpretability, and user trust in syndrome differentiation and treatment recommendations.

Method: The study built a Neo4j knowledge graph with 241 syndromes, 1,263 symptoms, and 2,485 relations; implemented a four-stage symptom matching pipeline (exact, semantic, fuzzy, and LLM verification); used an information gain-based proactive questioning strategy optimized by genetic algorithms; and presented treatments through AI-generated illustrations, 3D meridian-acupoint models, and evidence-based literature.

Result: Knowledge graph constraints reduced non-standard outputs by 32%. Across case studies and 30 automated paired-comparison cases, the system improved diagnostic trust significantly (Cohen's d = 1.82, p < 0.001), reduced cognitive load in four of five dimensions, and increased the credibility rating of evidence-based references (4.21 vs. 2.95).

Conclusion: The proposed knowledge graph-driven visual diagnostic system improves the transparency of traditional Chinese medicine syndrome differentiation and the interpretability of treatment plans through multimodal interaction, providing a practical and trustworthy AI-assisted TCM solution.

Abstract: Aim: Existing AI-assisted traditional Chinese medicine diagnostic tools suffer from opaque reasoning processes, passive interaction, and limited treatment plan presentation. This study proposes a knowledge-enhanced visual diagnostic system to improve the transparency and interpretability of syndrome differentiation and treatment. Methods: The system is built upon a Neo4j knowledge graph comprising 241 syndromes, 1,263 symptoms, and 2,485 relations. It incorporates a four-stage symptom matching pipeline (exact, semantic, fuzzy, and large language model verification), an information gain-driven proactive questioning strategy optimized with genetic algorithms, and a multimodal treatment presentation integrating artificial intelligence-generated illustrations, three-dimensional meridian-acupoint models, and evidence-based literature. Results: Knowledge graph constraints reduced non-standard outputs by 32%. Case studies validated the effectiveness of the interactive workflow across patient self-assessment, clinician-assisted diagnosis, and traditional Chinese medicine education. Automated paired-comparison evaluation across 30 cases further demonstrated significant improvements in diagnostic trust (Cohen's d = 1.82, p < 0.001), reduced cognitive load (improvements in four of five dimensions), and higher credibility of evidence-based references (4.21 vs. 2.95). Conclusions: The proposed system enhances the transparency of traditional Chinese medicine diagnostic reasoning and the interpretability of treatment plans through knowledge graph-driven visualization and multimodal interaction, offering a practical solution for trustworthy artificial intelligence-assisted traditional Chinese medicine applications.

</details>


### [177] [Workflow-to-Skill: Skill Creation via Routing-Workflow-Semantics-Attachments Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06893)
*Yuyang Zhang,Xinyuan Han,Xudong Jiang,Run Wang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper proposes a structured trace-to-skill pipeline: represent Skills with RWSA and synthesize them with W2S from messy agent traces, achieving a 10.5% gain in replay consistency over simpler baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hand-writing high-quality Skills for LLM agents is expensive, while available interaction evidence is heterogeneous, fragmented, redundant, and may omit rare but safety-critical behaviors, making naive summarization insufficient for Skill construction.

Method: The paper introduces RWSA, an intermediate representation that separates Skills into Workflow structure, execution Semantics, and runtime Attachments, and builds W2S to segment heterogeneous traces, draft local Skills, align shared structures, reconcile branches, and compress redundancy while preserving evidence and confidence.

Result: On 70 Skills, W2S improves behavioral replay consistency by 10.5% over summarization- and prompting-based baselines.

Conclusion: Treating interaction traces as executable runtime specifications and converting them into structured Skills via RWSA/W2S yields better Skill quality than plain summarization or prompting baselines.

Abstract: Large language model agents increasingly rely on Skills to encode procedural knowledge, yet high-quality Skills remain costly to hand-write. This paper studies automatic Skill construction from heterogeneous interaction evidence, including demonstrations, agent trajectories, tool traces, and execution logs. We argue that trace-to-skill construction is not simple summarization tasks, because traces are fragmented, redundant, and may miss rare but safety-critical behaviors. To address this, we introduce RWSA, a workflow-oriented intermediate representation that decomposes Skills into Workflow structure, execution Semantics, and runtime Attachments, capturing task decomposition, control flow, verification, safety, rollback, and state management. Building on RWSA, we propose W2S, a framework that segments traces, induces local Skill drafts, aligns shared structures, reconciles branches, and compresses redundancy while preserving evidence and confidence annotations. Experiments on 70 Skills show that W2S improves behavioral replay consistency by 10.5% over summarization- and prompting-based baselines, highlighting the need to treat traces as executable runtime specifications rather than compressible text.

</details>


### [178] [Declarative Skills for AI Agents in Knowledge-Grounded Tool-Use Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06923)
*M. Danish Lim,I. Danial Bin Sharudin,Wen Han Chen,Cedric Lim,Laura Wynter*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper compares declarative, imperative, and unscaffolded tool-using agents for customer-service workflows. Retrieval quality matters most; with good retrieval, declarative skill files help more than brittle state-machine orchestration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work is motivated by the need to understand how to orchestrate tool-using AI agents in realistic customer-service settings over unstructured knowledge bases, and by the question of whether declarative orchestration via natural-language skills can outperform imperative, externally controlled workflows and unscaffolded agents.

Method: The paper formalizes three agent designs as policy classes in a decentralized partially-observable Markov decision process: a DeclarativeAgent using three natural-language skill files at inference time, an ImperativeAgent using an explicit programmatic state machine, and an unscaffolded baseline. It then analyzes their information-theoretic and structural properties and evaluates them empirically across five language models and two retrieval regimes over an unstructured knowledge base in customer-service workflows.

Result: Empirically, all agent types degrade significantly under incomplete or biased retrieval, and skill files cannot compensate for missing evidence. Under high-quality retrieval, declarative skills consistently improve procedural-task accuracy and reduce orchestration mistakes, while imperative state-machine control does not consistently improve task success or compliance.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that retrieval quality is the main bottleneck for tool-using customer-service agents. When retrieval is strong, declarative agents with natural-language skill files improve procedural accuracy and reduce orchestration errors more consistently than imperative state-machine agents, whose brittleness does not reliably improve success or compliance.

Abstract: We study orchestration mechanisms for tool-using AI agents in realistic customer-service workflows over an unstructured knowledge base. We argue that declarative agents -- AI agents equipped with natural-language skill files appended to the system prompt -- are an effective orchestration paradigm. Concretely, we compare (i) a DeclarativeAgent that reads three domain-specific skill files at inference time and decides its own control flow, (ii) an ImperativeAgent based on a programmatic state machine with explicit phases, and (iii) an unscaffolded baseline agent modeled after the $τ$-Knowledge benchmark agent. Our ImperativeAgent is motivated by externalised-control inference as in Recursive Language Models and graph-based orchestration frameworks. We formalise the three agents as policy classes within a decentralised partially-observable Markov decision process and analyse their information-theoretic and structural properties; we then test the predicted differences empirically on five language models and two retrieval regimes. Our results show that retrieval quality is a dominant bottleneck for AI agents: when evidence is incomplete or skewed, all agents degrade substantially, and skill files cannot recover lost performance. Under high-quality retrieval, however, declarative skills consistently improve accuracy on procedural tasks and reduce orchestration errors, while the imperative state machine's brittleness does not reliably improve task success or compliance.

</details>


### [179] [Quantum-Inspired Trace-Augmented Evidence Selection for Reasoning over Structured Hypothesis Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06941)
*Laura Wynter,Nirvik Sahoo,Paul Griffin*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Instead of picking the most common answer, EP-HUBO picks the best-supported evidence across multiple reasoning traces, improving legal QA when evidence quality matters more than popularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Majority voting over sampled chain-of-thought traces ignores evidence quality and can favor popular but weakly supported answers, which is especially problematic in specialized, evidence-intensive domains like legal reasoning.

Method: The paper samples multiple chain-of-thought traces from a small local model, parses them into hypothesis-specific evidence pools, formulates evidence selection as a higher-order unconstrained binary optimization problem weighted by relevance, specificity, and distinctiveness, then sends one adjudication call to a frontier model.

Result: On two legal reasoning benchmarks, HUBO-style optimization improves aggregation of reasoning fragments and is evaluated on both classical simulated annealing hardware and the Dirac-3 photonic entropy-quantum machine, showing value where benchmark contamination is low.

Conclusion: EP-HUBO provides a principled evidence-aggregation method for legal reasoning by optimizing over reasoning fragments rather than relying on majority vote, helping preserve minority but better-supported hypotheses, especially in low-contamination domains.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) now solve a wide range of expert-level exams at or above human level, yet remain brittle on specialised, evidence-intensive domains such as law. On these tasks, errors arise not only from gaps in world knowledge but also from subtle distinctions between pieces of evidence and inconsistent use of supporting evidence. The most common aggregator over sampled chain-of-thought (CoT) traces, majority vote, returns the most popular answer regardless of whether its evidence is actually strongest. We propose to treat the selection of CoT reasoning fragments into a set of evidence as an explicit combinatorial optimisation problem, allowing well-supported but minority hypotheses to override noisy majorities, and to evaluate the approach on legal-reasoning benchmarks that are particularly sensitive to evidence quality. We introduce EP-HUBO (Evidence Pool Higher-Order Binary Optimisation), which generates multiple CoT traces with a small local model, parses fragments into per-hypothesis evidence pools, solves a higher-order unconstrained binary optimisation per pool with quality-derived weights (relevance, specificity, distinctiveness), and delegates a single adjudication call per question to a frontier model. We evaluate EP-HUBO on two evidence-intensive legal benchmarks using both simulated annealing on classical hardware and the Dirac-3 photonic entropy-quantum machine from Quantum Computing Inc. HUBO-style optimisation gives a principled way to aggregate reasoning fragments while preserving minority-but-correct hypotheses, and is most valuable in low-contamination domains where frontier models have not already absorbed the benchmark material.

</details>


### [180] [Accounting for Context: Shaping Moral Credences for Value Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06972)
*Jazon Szabo,Sanjay Modgil*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Context matters in moral uncertainty: if you aggregate moral theories without modeling when their assumptions hold, you can get paradoxical decisions, including apparent violations of weak Pareto. The paper formalizes this and argues the issue is essentially a Simpson’s paradox caused by context-blind aggregation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that existing work on moral uncertainty aims to aggregate judgments from multiple moral theories fairly, but often ignores real-world context. Different moral perspectives rely on assumptions that may hold unevenly across situations, so a context-insensitive aggregation method can distort decision-making.

Method: The paper formalizes agent decision-making under moral uncertainty while explicitly incorporating contextual factors that affect the applicability or reliability of different moral perspectives. It then analyzes the behavior of aggregation mechanisms under these conditions and studies why standard properties such as weak Pareto can be violated.

Result: The main result is a formal demonstration that weak Pareto can be violated once contextual factors are taken into account in moral uncertainty. The paper further shows that this violation can be interpreted as a form of Simpson’s paradox, highlighting a structural limitation in aggregation methods that ignore context.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that aggregating moral evaluations across theories without accounting for contextual factors can produce misleading results. In particular, when context affects whether a moral theory’s assumptions hold, even the weak Pareto principle may appear to fail; this is better understood as a Simpson’s paradox-style aggregation error than as a decisive objection to contextualized moral uncertainty frameworks.

Abstract: Ensuring that agent behaviours are aligned with human moral values inevitably raises the problem of how to account for the plurality of moral perspectives that societies -- and even individuals -- typically adopt. Work on moral uncertainty proposes mechanisms to fairly and democratically aggregate evaluations of actions across different moral theories. However, this paper argues that one needs to account for contextual factors when aggregating moral evaluations. For example, consequentialist perspectives assume an ability to accurately determine how an agent's actions change the world; an assumption that often does not hold in real world settings. We, therefore, formalise agent decision making under moral uncertainty, while also accounting for these kinds of contextual factors. We thereby show that a seemingly commonsensical property -- the weak Pareto principle -- is violated. We argue that this apparent problem is, in fact, a variation of Simpson's paradox, and hence reveals the limitations of aggregation mechanisms that ignore the impact of contextual factors.

</details>


### [181] [Exploring Agentic Tool-Calling Decisions via Uncertainty-Aligned Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06976)
*Yijin Zhou,Linqian Zeng,Xiaoya Lu,Wenyuan Xie,Dongrui Liu,Junchi Yan,Jing Shao*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: TRUST通过把“不确定性分离”显式纳入奖励学习，减少LLM代理在工具调用中的过度自信错误，并在多轮任务中稳定提升性能。 / TRUST explicitly optimizes uncertainty separation in reward learning, reducing overconfident tool-use mistakes in LLM agents and consistently improving multi-turn performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLM代理在工具使用中经常出现次优决策，如无依据的工具调用和幻觉式直接回答；现有方法主要依赖推理时纠错或粗粒度结果奖励，未充分研究决策不确定性，导致错误动作过度自信、探索信号变弱。 / LLM-based agents frequently make suboptimal tool-use decisions, such as unsupported tool invocation and hallucinated direct responses. Existing methods mainly rely on inference-time correction or coarse outcome-based rewards and underexplore uncertainty in agent decisions, leading to overconfident errors and weaker exploration signals.

Method: 论文提出TRUST方法：1）分析决策导向强化学习会削弱正确与错误动作之间的不确定性分离；2）将不确定性量化引入奖励，作为一种“排斥力”来维持这种分离；3）对多轮交互轨迹标注轻量级关键轮次注释，并进行统一后训练。 / The paper proposes TRUST: (1) it identifies that decision-oriented RL weakens uncertainty separation between correct and incorrect actions; (2) it incorporates uncertainty quantification into reward design as a repulsive force to preserve that separation; and (3) it uses lightweight key-turn annotations for unified post-training over multi-turn trajectories.

Result: 在多样化工具使用基准上，TRUST始终优于基线方法，提升了工具使用决策质量和整体代理表现，同时在训练优化过程中保持了更可靠的不确定性估计。 / Across diverse tool-use benchmarks, TRUST consistently outperforms baselines, improving tool-use decision quality and overall agent performance while maintaining more reliable uncertainty estimates during optimization.

Conclusion: TRUST通过将不确定性量化纳入奖励设计，并结合轻量级关键轮次标注进行多轮轨迹统一后训练，能够在多种工具使用基准上稳定提升决策质量、整体代理性能以及优化过程中的不确定性可靠性。 / TRUST improves decision quality, overall agent performance, and the reliability of uncertainty estimates during optimization by integrating uncertainty quantification into reward design and using lightweight key-turn annotations for unified post-training on multi-turn trajectories.

Abstract: Large language model (LLM)-based agents often make suboptimal tool-use decisions, including unsupported tool invocation and hallucinated direct responses, which may accumulate errors throughout multi-step interactions. Existing approaches mainly improve these behaviors through inference-time correction or coarse-grained reward signals based on decision outcomes and structured checklists, leaving the uncertainty characteristics of agent decisions underexplored. We observe that decision-oriented reinforcement learning tends to weaken the uncertainty separation between correct and incorrect actions, resulting in overconfident mistakes and weaker exploration signals. Therefore, we propose TRUST, which incorporates uncertainty quantification into reward design as a repulsive force for maintaining uncertainty separation, and labels lightweight key-turn annotations for unified post-training of multi-turn trajectories. Experimental results across diverse tool-use benchmarks show that TRUST consistently enhances both decision quality and agent performance while maintaining more reliable uncertainty estimates during optimization.

</details>


### [182] [Teaching the Way, Not the Answer: Privileged Tutoring Distillation for Multimodal Policy Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07000)
*Shizhe Xiang,Ke An,Wenlong Yu,Yue Liu,Jian Luan,Pei Fu,Qilong Wang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: PTD-PO is a new RLVR training framework for LVLMs that gives dense token-level guidance using privileged hints instead of answers, leading to stronger and more stable multimodal reasoning performance than existing RLVR and distillation methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard RLVR uses sparse verifiable rewards, which give weak token-level supervision for failed rollouts and make exploration inefficient in complex multimodal reasoning. Existing dense-guidance approaches either rely on expensive external teachers or leak answer information, causing shortcut behavior. The paper aims to provide dense supervision without these drawbacks.

Method: The paper proposes PTD-PO, a Privileged Tutoring Distillation Policy Optimization framework. It builds privileged hints from spatial attention guidance and intermediate textual reasoning, uses these hints via in-context learning to generate step-wise token-distribution supervision, aligns failed student rollouts with a hint-augmented reference model under the original answer-free context, and introduces a Top-K Jensen-Shannon divergence objective to stabilize alignment and reduce memory cost.

Result: Across LVLMs from 2B to 8B parameters, PTD-PO consistently outperforms RLVR and other distillation baselines, improves complex multimodal reasoning, mitigates entropy collapse, and stabilizes training under guided versus unguided context shift.

Conclusion: PTD-PO improves LVLM post-training for multimodal reasoning by providing dense, answer-free tutoring signals during RLVR, yielding better performance, more stable optimization, and less entropy collapse than prior RLVR and distillation baselines.

Abstract: Recent post-training methods, particularly Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR), have significantly enhanced the reasoning ability of Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). However, the sparse nature of verifiable rewards provides little token-level supervision for failed rollouts, often leading to inefficient exploration in complex multimodal reasoning tasks. Although policy distillation can offer dense guidance, external teacher based methods introduce substantial computational overhead, while answer conditioned tuning methods may expose answer-level information and induce shortcut-like generation behavior. To address these limitations, we propose PTD-PO, a Privileged Tutoring Distillation Policy Optimization framework for RLVR that provides dense guidance without exposing the answer to the student policy. Specifically, PTD-PO constructs structured privileged hints from spatial attention guidance and intermediate textual reasoning steps, and uses them through in-context learning to produce step-wise token-distribution supervision. The student is still optimized under the original answer-free context, and its failed rollouts are aligned with the hint-augmented reference model at the token-distribution level. To further stabilize distillation under the distribution shift between guided and unguided contexts, we introduce a Top-K Jensen-Shannon divergence objective that focuses alignment on informative token probabilities while reducing memory overhead. Experiments on LVLMs ranging from 2B to 8B parameters show that PTD-PO consistently outperforms RLVR and distillation baselines, mitigates entropy collapse, and improves complex multimodal reasoning performance.

</details>


### [183] [The Sim-to-Real Gap of Foundation Model Agents: A Unified MDP Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07017)
*Xiaoou Liu,Tiejin Chen,Weibo Li,Xiyang Hu,Hua Wei*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Treat foundation model agent robustness as a standard sim-to-real problem over Observation, Action, Transition, and Reward, and reuse classical tools like domain randomization to build standardized benchmarks and more reliable agents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Foundation model agents are being deployed in real-world decision-making, but their failures are often discussed as if robustness were a completely new problem. The paper is motivated by the need to reinterpret these failures using mature sim-to-real concepts from robotics and control.

Method: The authors formalize the evaluation-training gap for foundation model agents using the four elements of a Markov Decision Process: Observation, Action, Transition, and Reward. They map classical sim-to-real discrepancies into this framework and advocate adapting established techniques such as domain randomization.

Result: The paper presents a research agenda rather than an empirical benchmark result. It provides concrete examples, including multilingual tool calling, to show how observation-space mismatch can cause operationally invalid actions despite correct semantic intent.

Conclusion: The paper argues that robustness issues in foundation model agents should be treated as a classical sim-to-real problem framed through the four MDP components, enabling a unified vocabulary, better benchmarks, and more trustworthy real-world agents.

Abstract: Foundation model agents are increasingly deployed for real-world decision-making, but suffer from the sim-to-real gap. While robotics and classical control have mature frameworks to address this gap, the foundation model community is treating agent robustness as an entirely novel phenomenon. Our paper proposes formalizing the foundation model agent evaluation and training gap as a classical sim-to-real problem structured entirely around the four elements of a Markov Decision Process, including Observation, Action, Transition, and Reward. In this paper, we set a comprehensive research agenda that translates classical discrepancies into the foundation model domain and advocates for adopting established solutions like domain randomization. We provide concrete examples, such as a multilingual tool calling to demonstrate how severe observation space gaps lead to operationally invalid actions despite correct semantic intent. Ultimately, this agenda aims to drive a paradigm shift, yielding a unified vocabulary and standardized stress test benchmarks to foster a new generation of highly trustworthy agents for reliable real-world applications.

</details>


### [184] [StainFlow: Entity-Stain Tracking and Evidence Linking for Process Rewards in GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07027)
*Haojie Hao,Longkun Hao,Yihang Lou,Yan Bai,Zhenyang Li,Zhichao Yang,Dongshuo Huang,Hongyu Lin,Lanqing Hong,Jiakai Wang,Xianglong Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: StainFlow利用任务实体在GUI轨迹中的“污渍流”变化来同时改进全局阶段划分和局部证据检索，从而为GUI智能体提供更可靠的过程奖励信号。 / StainFlow uses “stain flow” over task entities in GUI trajectories to improve both global phase segmentation and local evidence retrieval, producing more reliable process rewards for GUI agents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于现有PRM方法虽能提供比轨迹级成功信号更细粒度的反馈，但仍存在两类局限：全局里程碑分解主观且单一路径化，难以适应真实GUI任务中的多种有效执行路径；局部固定窗口验证无法稳定捕捉长程关键信息，且容易被无关帧稀释判断信号。 / The motivation is that existing PRMs provide finer-grained feedback than trajectory-level success signals, but still have two limitations: global milestone decomposition is subjective and assumes a single path, making it unsuitable for multiple valid GUI execution paths; and fixed local judging windows often miss long-range critical evidence or dilute the signal with irrelevant frames.

Method: 方法提出了StainFlow，一种面向GUI Agents的过程奖励模型，包含两个核心模块：1）全局实体污渍跟踪模块，从轨迹中提取可视觉验证的任务实体，并跟踪其污渍浓度与状态变化，据此更客观地划分任务阶段；2）局部污渍证据链接模块，以候选关键节点的触发实体为中心，基于污渍浓度和状态变化检索相关步骤，动态构建高密度证据窗口以验证关键节点。 / The paper proposes StainFlow, a process reward model for GUI agents with two core modules: (1) a Global Entity Stain Tracking module that extracts visually verifiable task entities and tracks their stain concentration and state evolution to objectively segment task phases; and (2) a Local Stain Evidence Linking module that centers on triggering entities of candidate key nodes, retrieves relevant steps via stain concentration and state changes, and dynamically builds dense evidence windows for key-node verification.

Result: 在AndroidWorld和OGRBench上的大量实验表明，StainFlow将在线强化学习成功率相对提升3.2%，并将轨迹完成度判断准确率提升1.8%。 / Extensive experiments on AndroidWorld and OGRBench show that StainFlow improves online RL success by 3.2% relatively and increases trajectory completion judgment accuracy by 1.8%.

Conclusion: StainFlow通过基于实体污渍流的全局与局部过程奖励建模，缓解了GUI智能体长时程任务中稀疏反馈和信用分配困难的问题，并在在线强化学习成功率与轨迹完成判断精度上取得了稳定提升。 / StainFlow alleviates sparse feedback and credit assignment issues in long-horizon GUI-agent RL by modeling global and local process rewards with entity stain flow, yielding consistent gains in online RL success and trajectory completion judgment accuracy.

Abstract: Reinforcement Learning (RL) has become a promising approach for improving GUI Agents in long-horizon, stochastic digital environments, but trajectory-level success feedback is too sparse to provide reliable credit assignment for intermediate exploration steps. To mitigate this issue, recent studies introduce Process Reward Models (PRMs), which provide finer-grained training feedback through global milestone verification or local step-level evaluation. However, these methods still suffer from two level-specific limitations: global milestone decomposition is subjective and singular, making it difficult to accommodate the multiple valid execution paths in real GUI tasks, while fixed local judging windows may miss long-range key evidence or dilute the decision signal with irrelevant frames. Inspired by stain-tracing mechanisms in network flow analysis, we propose StainFlow, an entity-stain-flow process reward model for GUI Agents. To reduce the subjectivity of global partitioning, we introduce the Global Entity Stain Tracking module, which extracts visually verifiable task entities and tracks how their stain concentrations and states evolve along the trajectory, allowing task phases to be objectively separated by changes in the entity evidence flow. To improve the accuracy of local verification, we introduce the Local Stain Evidence Linking module. Centered on the triggering entities of each candidate key node, it retrieves relevant steps based on their stain concentrations and state changes, and dynamically constructs high-density evidence windows for verifying true key nodes. Extensive experiments on AndroidWorld and OGRBench show that StainFlow relatively improves online RL success by 3.2% and trajectory completion judgment accuracy by 1.8%.

</details>


### [185] [Hierarchical Semantic-Constrained Heterogeneous Graph for Audio-Visual Event Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07033)
*Zhe Yang,Ruyi Zhang,Hongtao Chen,Wenrui Li,Hengyu Man,Wangmeng Zuo,Xiaopeng Fan*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: HSCHG improves open-vocabulary audio-visual event localization by combining hierarchical heterogeneous graph modeling, confidence-aware cross-modal fusion, cross-level semantic constraints, and hyperbolic hierarchical representation learning, achieving state-of-the-art benchmark performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing OV-AVEL methods mainly learn joint audio-visual representations in Euclidean space, but they struggle with unseen categories due to weak supervision for maintaining audio-visual consistency across time scales, and they lack hierarchical constraints between segment- and video-level semantics, which harms semantic consistency across levels.

Method: The paper proposes a Hierarchical Semantic Constrained Heterogeneous Graph (HSCHG) framework. It builds a heterogeneous hierarchical graph with audio/visual segment nodes and video-level nodes in Euclidean space, uses multi-directional temporal edges for intra-modal temporal modeling, applies a dual-threshold filtering gated fusion strategy for reliable cross-modal interaction, introduces bidirectional semantic constraints between segment- and video-level representations, and maps multi-level audio-visual features together with text prototypes into hyperbolic space with a hierarchical entailment regularization loss.

Result: Extensive experiments on the OV-AVEL benchmark show that the proposed method outperforms existing approaches, and ablation studies confirm the effectiveness of its main components.

Conclusion: The proposed HSCHG framework effectively improves open-vocabulary audio-visual event localization by enforcing cross-modal and cross-level semantic consistency and by modeling hierarchical relations in hyperbolic space.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary audio-visual event localization (OV-AVEL) jointly models audio-visual cues to recognize and temporally localize events, including categories unseen during training. Existing methods primarily learn joint audio-visual representations in Euclidean space, but still face two significant challenges. First, the lack of supervision signals for unseen categories makes it difficult to maintain audio-visual consistency across multiple temporal scales. Second, the lack of hierarchical constraints between segment- and video-level semantics prevents the model from establishing semantic consistency across different levels. To address these challenges, we propose a hierarchical semantic constrained heterogeneous graph (HSCHG) for audio-visual event localization framework. We first construct a heterogeneous hierarchical graph in Euclidean space, which includes audio and visual segment nodes and their corresponding video-level nodes. We use multi-directional temporal edges to capture complete temporal information within each modality. Simultaneously, we employ a dual-threshold filtering gated fusion strategy, introducing cross-modal information only when the alignment confidence is high. Furthermore, we introduce bidirectional semantic constraints between segment- and video-level representations to achieve semantic consistency across different levels. Based on this, we map the multi-level audio-visual representations and text prototypes uniformly into hyperbolic space. We use a hierarchical entailment regularization loss to characterize the hierarchical relationships between videos and segments. Extensive experimental results show that our method outperforms existing methods on the OV-AVEL benchmark. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of our method.

</details>


### [186] [Front-to-Attractors: Modifying the Front-to-Front Heuristic in Bidirectional Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07047)
*Alvin Zou,Muhammad Suhail Saleem,Maxim Likhachev*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: F2A 用少量动态 attractors 近似对向搜索前沿，试图以接近 F2F 的信息性获得远低于 F2F 的计算成本；实验显示它明显减少 pairwise evaluations，并比 F2E 扩展更少节点。 / F2A approximates the opposite frontier with a small dynamic set of attractors, aiming for near-F2F informativeness at much lower cost; experiments show substantially fewer pairwise evaluations and fewer node expansions than F2E.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 双向搜索中的 F2F 启发式通常比 F2E 更有信息性、能减少节点扩展，但其对对向前沿进行大量成对评估的计算代价很高。作者希望在保留 F2F 指导能力的同时，降低这种开销。 / In bidirectional search, F2F heuristics are usually more informative than F2E and reduce node expansions, but they incur high computational cost due to extensive pairwise evaluations; the goal is to keep most of F2F’s guidance while lowering that overhead.

Method: 论文提出 front-to-attractors (F2A) 启发式：对状态 s，不再与对向前沿的所有状态逐一计算 h(s, s')，而是只与一个动态维护的小规模 attractor 集合计算距离，用这些 attractors 作为对完整对向前沿的代理。 / The paper introduces the front-to-attractors (F2A) heuristic: instead of evaluating h(s, s') against every state on the opposite frontier, it estimates distances only to a small dynamically maintained set of attractors that acts as a surrogate for the full frontier.

Result: 在多个领域的实验中，F2A 相比 F2F 将成对评估次数最多减少 11.2 倍；同时，相比 F2E，平均减少 4.8 倍节点扩展。 / Across multiple domains, F2A reduces pairwise evaluations by up to 11.2x relative to F2F and achieves 4.8x fewer node expansions on average than F2E.

Conclusion: F2A 在保留 F2F 启发式大部分信息性的同时，显著降低了成对评估开销，并保持了最优性保证；实验表明它在效率与搜索质量之间提供了更优折中。 / F2A preserves much of the informativeness of F2F while greatly reducing pairwise-evaluation cost and retaining optimality guarantees, yielding a better efficiency-quality tradeoff in bidirectional search.

Abstract: Heuristics play a central role in the performance of bidirectional search algorithms, which commonly rely on two main classes. Front-to-end (F2E) heuristics estimate the distance from a state s to the target of the search (the goal for forward search or the start for backward search). In contrast, front-to-front (F2F) heuristics estimate the distance from s to the opposite search frontier using a pairwise function h(s, s'), where s' ranges over frontier states. Although F2F heuristics are typically more informative and therefore reduce the number of node expansions, their reliance on extensive pairwise evaluations incurs substantial computational overhead. To address this limitation, we introduce a new heuristic class, front-to-attractors (F2A), that preserves much of the informativeness of F2F while dramatically reducing its computational cost. Rather than evaluating distances to all states on the opposite frontier, F2A estimates the distance from s to a small, dynamically maintained set of attractors in the opposite search direction. These attractors serve as a surrogate for the full frontier, enabling rich heuristic guidance at a fraction of the computational expense while maintaining the optimality guarantees offered by F2F. We evaluate F2A across multiple domains and show that it reduces the number of pairwise evaluations by up to 11.2x compared to F2F, while achieving 4.8x fewer node expansions than F2E on average.

</details>


### [187] [DyCon: Dynamic Reasoning Control via Evolving Difficulty Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07108)
*Tengyao Tu,Yulin Li,Hui-Ling Zhen,Libo Qin,Zhoujun Wei,Jinghua Piao,Zhuotao Tian,Yong Li,Min Zhang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: DyCon is a training-free method that reads step-level embeddings to track changing task difficulty during reasoning, then shortens unnecessary reasoning steps to reduce overthinking while preserving performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large Reasoning Models achieve strong results through iterative reasoning, but they often waste computation on redundant reasoning steps, a phenomenon called overthinking. Prior mitigation methods depend on static difficulty estimates or task-specific training, making them insufficient for handling dynamically changing reasoning complexity.

Method: The paper first shows empirically that task difficulty changes during the reasoning process and that this evolving difficulty is linearly encoded in step-level embeddings of Large Reasoning Models. Based on this, it proposes DyCon, which models dynamic difficulty from latent step-level representations and uses it to adaptively control reasoning depth at inference time.

Result: Across four models from 4B to 32B and twelve benchmarks covering math reasoning, general QA, and coding, DyCon significantly reduces redundant reasoning steps while maintaining strong accuracy and generalization.

Conclusion: DyCon is an effective training-free framework that dynamically controls reasoning depth using step-level latent representations, reducing overthinking and improving reasoning efficiency without hurting accuracy or generalization.

Abstract: Recent advances in Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) demonstrate remarkable performance improvements by iteratively reflecting, exploring, and executing complex tasks, yet suffer from inefficiencies due to redundant reasoning, known as "overthinking". Existing methods to mitigate this issue either rely on static difficulty estimates or require task-specific training, and thus fail to adapt to the dynamic complexity during reasoning. In this work, we empirically show that the problem difficulty evolves dynamically throughout the reasoning process and is linearly encoded in the LRM's step-level embeddings. Building on this insight, we propose DyCon, a training-free framework that leverages latent step-level representations to explicitly model the evolving task difficulty, enabling the dynamic control of reasoning depth to mitigate the overthinking issue. Extensive experiments conducted on four models ranging from 4B to 32B, and across twelve benchmarks in math reasoning, general question answering, and coding tasks demonstrate that DyCon significantly enhances reasoning efficiency by reducing redundant steps without sacrificing accuracy or generalization. Project page and code are available at https://github.com/yu-lin-li/DyCon.

</details>


### [188] [Beyond Post-hoc Explanation: Toward Glassbox AI via Probabilistic Mediation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07113)
*Manuele Leonelli*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Replace unreliable post-hoc explanations with a transparent Bayesian reasoning layer in front of generative models so AI decisions in high-stakes settings become auditable, uncertainty-aware, and contestable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that current explainability methods for large language models are mostly post-hoc and do not faithfully reflect the actual reasoning process, which is inadequate in legally and institutionally sensitive settings such as public administration, law, and healthcare.

Method: The method is a conceptual architectural proposal called the Glassbox Framework. It uses Bayesian networks as an intermediate reasoning layer between domain knowledge and generative models, encoding causal assumptions and probabilistic dependencies to support inference, uncertainty quantification, and reasoning traceability.

Result: The paper characterises the proposed architecture, illustrates it with a benefit eligibility scenario, and identifies key open challenges including semantic alignment, dynamic model construction, probabilistic grounding, and human governance required for scalable deployment.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that accountable AI in high-stakes domains requires shifting from post-hoc explanation to ante-hoc structured reasoning. It proposes the Glassbox Framework, where Bayesian networks mediate generative models to provide transparent, auditable, and contestable reasoning before outputs are produced.

Abstract: Large language models are rapidly becoming infrastructural components in high-stakes institutional settings, including public administration, legal reasoning, and healthcare, where opacity is not merely inconvenient but institutionally and legally untenable. Existing approaches to explainability are predominantly post-hoc, offering unstable, non-contestable accounts that have no formal relationship to the reasoning process that produced the output. We argue that the problem is not the absence of explanation but the absence of structured reasoning in the first place. This paper makes the case for a fundamentally different architecture, which we call the Glassbox Framework, in which Bayesian networks serve as transparent, ante-hoc mediation layers for generative models. Bayesian networks encode domain knowledge, causal assumptions, and probabilistic dependencies before inference occurs, enabling auditable reasoning traces, uncertainty quantification, and contestable outputs. We characterise the architecture of this framework and ground it in a benefit eligibility scenario, identifying the foundational challenges spanning semantic alignment, dynamic model construction, probabilistic grounding, and human governance that must be solved to realise it at scale. By shifting from post-hoc explanation to ante-hoc probabilistic mediation, this work outlines a principled path toward AI systems that are not only powerful but fundamentally accountable.

</details>


### [189] [Think Fast: Estimating No-CoT Task-Completion Time Horizons of Frontier AI Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07157)
*Dewi Gould,Francis Rhys Ward,Anders Cairns Woodruff,Rauno Arike,Josh Hills,Alex Serrano,Ida Caspary,Jason Ross Brown,Jo J. Jiao,Patrick Leask,Twm Stone,Ram Potham,Ionut Gabriel Stan,Harry Mayne,Simeon Hellsten,Shubhorup Biswas,Ariana Azarbal,William L. Anderson,Elle Najt,Ryan Greenblatt,Julian Stastny*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper shows that top AI models are getting much better at reasoning without showing their chain-of-thought, which could weaken CoT-based safety monitoring. Using large-scale benchmark evaluation and new horizon metrics, it finds rapid yearly progress and projects substantially stronger hidden reasoning within a few years.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Many AI safety approaches assume that important reasoning remains externally visible through chain-of-thought monitoring. If models can increasingly reason internally without explicit thinking tokens, that assumption weakens and current oversight methods may fail. The paper is motivated by the need to measure and forecast that risk directly.

Method: The paper evaluates frontier models without chain-of-thought on more than 30,000 questions across 43 benchmarks covering math, coding, puzzles, causality, theory of mind, and strategic reasoning. It introduces two main metrics: the 50% task-completion time horizon (human time required for tasks a model solves with 50% success) and the 50% reasoning token horizon (minimum o3-mini reasoning tokens associated with tasks solved at 50% success). It then analyzes historical progress and extrapolates future trends.

Result: The no-CoT 50% task horizon of frontier models has roughly doubled annually over the last six years. GPT-5.5 reaches a no-CoT task horizon above 3 minutes and a reasoning token horizon above 1,500 tokens. Median forecasts suggest no-CoT task horizons may exceed 7 minutes by 2028 and 25 minutes by 2030, although uncertainty is substantial.

Conclusion: Frontier AI models are increasingly capable of solving complex reasoning tasks without emitting chain-of-thought, and this capability is improving quickly enough that safety oversight based on monitoring CoT may become less reliable; therefore, developers should explicitly track no-CoT reasoning progress.

Abstract: Many efforts to ensure frontier AI models are safe rely on monitoring their chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. If models become able to perform sufficiently complex reasoning internally, without explicit thinking tokens, this would undermine such oversight. We measure how well frontier models reason without CoT across a suite of over 30,000 questions spanning 43 benchmarks in domains including math, coding, puzzles, causality, theory-of-mind, and strategic reasoning. To compare models against humans, we estimate the $50\%$-task-completion time horizon (TH): the human time required for tasks a model completes with $50\%$ success rate. We complement this with a $50\%$ reasoning token horizon: the minimum number of o3-mini reasoning tokens needed for tasks a model solves with $50\%$ success rate. We find that the no-CoT $50\%$ TH of frontier models has been doubling roughly every year over the past six years, with GPT-5.5's TH reaching over 3 minutes and reasoning token horizon exceeding 1,500 tokens. Our median estimates predict that frontier no-CoT THs could exceed 7 minutes by 2028, and 25 minutes by 2030, though these projections carry substantial uncertainty. We recommend frontier developers track this explicitly.

</details>


### [190] [TOPSIS-RAD: Ranking According to Desires](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07253)
*Leonardo Fernandes Costa,Helder Gomes Costa,Diogo Lima,Brunno Rodrigues*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: TOPSIS-RAD is a TOPSIS variant that uses decision-maker-set veto and desired thresholds to filter and cap performances before normalisation, reducing outlier sensitivity and rank reversal while keeping the standard TOPSIS distance-based ranking framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional TOPSIS defines PIS and NIS from the observed alternatives, which can misalign rankings with decision-maker requirements, allow extreme or non-viable alternatives to distort normalisation, and produce rank reversal. The paper is motivated by the need for a more preference-consistent and stable ranking mechanism.

Method: The paper proposes TOPSIS-RAD, an extension of TOPSIS that introduces two arrays of reference levels defined by the decision-maker: Vetoed Performance Levels (VPL) and Desired Performance Levels (DPL). Alternatives failing VPL are excluded before normalisation, and performances above DPL are capped before normalisation. The subsequent ranking still follows the familiar distance-to-ideal structure of TOPSIS.

Result: Through three toy examples, the paper shows that VPL changes normalisation boundaries by excluding non-viable alternatives, while fixed DPL frontiers reduce the impact of overly high performances and stabilise rankings. The resulting method better reflects explicit decision-maker aspirations while preserving the computational logic of TOPSIS.

Conclusion: TOPSIS-RAD improves classical TOPSIS by replacing dataset-driven ideal points with decision-maker-defined reference boundaries, using VPL to remove non-viable alternatives and DPL to cap excessive performances. This makes rankings more stable, more aligned with decision-maker preferences, and less sensitive to outliers and rank reversal.

Abstract: Traditional TOPSIS derives its reference points -- the Positive Ideal Solution ($PIS$) and Negative Ideal Solution ($NIS$) -- from the observed alternative set, making rankings susceptible to misalignment with decision-maker (DM) requirements, sensitivity to outlier performances, and rank reversal. This paper proposes TOPSIS-RAD, which addresses these issues by incorporating two arrays of DM-defined reference levels. Vetoed Performance Levels ($VPL$) exclude non-viable alternatives before normalisation, preventing them from distorting the ranking frontiers. Desired Performance Levels ($DPL$) cap performances at the DM's desired level before normalisation, anchoring the $PIS$ in explicit aspirations rather than dataset extremes. Three toy examples demonstrate each mechanism: $VPL$ reshapes normalisation boundaries by removing a non-viable alternative; fixed $DPL$ frontiers stabilise rankings by limiting the influence of performances well above the desired level. The method preserves the familiar distance-based structure of TOPSIS while grounding the ranking in stable, DM-specified boundaries. Limitations and future research directions are also discussed.

</details>


### [191] [DuMate-DeepResearch: An Auditable Multi-Agent System with Recursive Search and Rubric-Grounded Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07299)
*Lingyong Yan,Can Xu,Yukun Zhao,Wenxuan Li,Qingyang Chen,Jiulong Wu,Wenli Song,Xiangnan Li,Weixian Shi,Yiqun Chen,Xuchen Ma,Yuchen Li,Jiashu Zhao,Shuaiqiang Wang,Jianmin Wu,Dawei Yin*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: A multi-agent deep research system with dynamic planning, nested search agents, and rubric-guided synthesis improves traceability and quality, setting new SOTA on two benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current deep research systems struggle with long-horizon planning under unclear scope, single-agent task decomposition and scheduling bottlenecks, hallucinations in long-form synthesis, and poor process auditability. The work aims to address these limitations.

Method: The paper proposes a multi-agent framework on Qianfan Agent Foundry that separates an Agent Core from a Tool Ecosystem, and adds three key mechanisms: graph-based dynamic planning, recursive two-level execution with an inner Search Agent, and rubric-based test-time optimization for grounded synthesis and adaptive stopping.

Result: On DeepResearch Bench, the framework achieves the best overall score of 58.03%. On DeepResearch Bench II, it achieves the best overall score of 61.95% and ranks first in information recall and analysis.

Conclusion: DuMate-DeepResearch is an effective multi-agent deep research framework that improves planning, execution stability, synthesis quality, and auditability, achieving state-of-the-art results on two DR benchmarks.

Abstract: Deep Research (DR) has emerged as a new agentic paradigm to tackle complex, open-ended research tasks, demanding systems that can iteratively frame problems, acquire evidence, verify sources, and synthesize long-form reports. In practice, however, current DR systems are constrained by four interrelated limitations: long-horizon planning over an underspecified scope, the bottleneck of decomposing and scheduling such tasks within a single agent, hallucination risk in long-form synthesis, and limited process auditability. This technical report presents DuMate-DeepResearch, a multi-agent DR framework built on the Qianfan Agent Foundry. The framework decouples the Agent Core, which handles task understanding, planning, and scheduling, from an extensible Tool Ecosystem for retrieval, evidence acquisition, and report rendering, making every intermediate decision and tool invocation explicitly traceable. Building on this infrastructure, DuMate-DeepResearch further introduces three mechanisms: (i) a graph-based dynamic planning strategy expands the research roadmap coarse-to-fine and continuously revises it through reflection, re-planning, backtracking, and parallel branching; (ii) a recursive two-level execution design delegates each complex search sub-task to an inner Search Agent that runs its own planning loop, isolating noisy retrieval and stabilizing long-horizon execution; (iii) a rubric-based test-time optimization mechanism dynamically generates task-specific quality criteria and uses them as live reasoning scaffolds for evidence-grounded synthesis and adaptive stopping. Across two deep research benchmarks, DuMate-DeepResearch establishes new state-of-the-art results: the best overall score (58.03%) on DeepResearch Bench, and the best overall score (61.95%) on DeepResearch Bench II while ranking first in information recall and analysis.

</details>


### [192] [Off-Policy Evaluation with Strategic Agents via Local Disclosure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07308)
*Kiet Q. H. Vo,Abbavaram Gowtham Reddy,Julian Rodemann,Siu Lun Chau,Krikamol Muandet*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: TL;DR: The paper studies off-policy evaluation when people strategically alter their features in response to a policy. By using post-hoc explanations to reveal pre-manipulation information, the authors estimate agents' response behavior and design a doubly robust, theoretically consistent OPE estimator under a log-normal assumption, with empirical support.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing OPE methods assume covariates are exogenous to policy, which fails when agents strategically change their features in response to policy. Prior solutions often require repeated interactions or full knowledge of strategic response rules, making them impractical. The paper is motivated by the need for a practical one-shot OPE framework that works under only partial knowledge of strategic behavior.

Method: The method models policy-dependent covariate shift caused by strategic behavior. It uses post-hoc explanations to recover information about agents' pre-strategic covariates, estimates a statistical response model for how agents adapt covariates, and then constructs a doubly robust estimator for policy value. Theoretical analysis assumes agents' cost sensitivity follows a conditional log-normal distribution to prove consistency.

Result: The paper provides a doubly robust estimator for OPE under strategic behavior, proves its consistency under a conditional log-normal cost-sensitivity assumption, and empirically validates the approach. It also shows that post-hoc explanations are not only interpretability tools but can actively help recover hidden structure needed for reliable evaluation.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that in one-shot off-policy evaluation with strategic agents, post-hoc explanations can reveal pre-strategic covariates, enabling identification and estimation of agents' response behavior under partial knowledge. Using this structure, the authors build a doubly robust estimator that is consistent under a conditional log-normal assumption on cost sensitivity, and show empirically that interaction design can reduce information asymmetry caused by strategic adaptation.

Abstract: We study off-policy evaluation (OPE) under strategic behavior where decision subjects (or agents) respond to a decision maker's policy by strategically modifying their covariates. Such behavior induces a policy-dependent covariate shift, breaking the standard assumption in existing methods that covariates are exogenous to the policy. Related work addresses this challenge by imposing strong assumptions such as repeated interactions or full knowledge of agents' response behavior, substantially limiting its applicability to OPE. In contrast, we consider a one-shot OPE setting where the decision maker has only partial knowledge of the agents' response behavior. Our key insight is that disclosing local information through post-hoc explanations reveals agents' pre-strategic covariates prior to adaptation, mitigating the information loss induced by strategic behavior. Leveraging this structure, we estimate a statistical model for the agents' responses and construct a doubly robust estimator for policy value. By assuming that the agents' cost sensitivity follows a conditional log-normal distribution, we establish consistency of the proposed estimator and validate our approach empirically. More broadly, our results highlight how interaction design can mitigate information asymmetry by revealing otherwise hidden structure in agents' strategic responses.

</details>


### [193] [Online Pandora's Box for Contextual LLM Cascading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07392)
*Alexandre Belloni,Yan Chen,Yehua Wei*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper models adaptive LLM API selection as an online Pandora's Box problem, learns reservation indices directly instead of full output distributions, and proves a near-optimal \~O(sqrt(T)) regret guarantee.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that in LLM API cascading, a decision-maker must adaptively decide which APIs to query and which generated output to deploy under query costs and limited feedback. Classical Pandora's Box models do not fit because querying reveals outputs rather than rewards directly.

Method: The method formulates LLM API cascading as an online contextual Pandora's Box problem with output-mediated feedback. It parameterizes contextual reservation index functions inspired by Weitzman's policy, estimates them via a GMM-type approach, and combines these estimates with UCB confidence bounds and a shared output-level reward evaluator.

Result: The proposed policy achieves dimension-dependent \~O(sqrt(T)) cumulative regret over T periods under regularity conditions, while avoiding the harder task of estimating full conditional output and cost distributions for each API.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that directly learning contextual reservation indices for adaptive LLM API querying, combined with UCB-style optimism for both query and selection decisions, yields an efficient policy with dimension-dependent nearly square-root regret over time.

Abstract: Motivated by Large Language Model (LLM) cascading, we propose an online contextual Pandora's Box model for adaptively querying and selecting LLM APIs. In each period, a decision-maker observes a request context and faces a two-phase decision problem. In the query phase, the decision-maker sequentially queries APIs, where each query reveals a generated output and the decision-maker incurs an (output-dependent) cost. In the selection phase, the decision-maker selects one of the generated outputs to deploy and observes only the downstream reward of the deployed output. This output-mediated feedback structure differs from classical online contextual Pandora's Box models, in which opening a box directly reveals its reward.
  Rather than estimating the full conditional output and cost distributions of each API, we directly model the reservation index and develop a learning approach for the query phase. Specifically, we impose a parametric structure on the contextual reservation index functions induced by the classical Weitzman's policy. Our policy combines generalized method of moments (GMM) type estimation of these reservation indices with UCB-style confidence bounds for both these indices and the shared output-level reward evaluator. Under regularity conditions, we prove that the resulting policy achieves dimension-dependent $\widetilde O(\sqrt T)$ cumulative regret over a horizon of $T$ periods.

</details>


### [194] [Act As a Real Researcher: A Suite of Benchmarks Evaluating Frontier LLMs and Agentic Harnesses in Research Lifecycle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07462)
*Jiayu Wang,Weijiang Lv,Bowen Fu,Jing Fu,Jiayi Song,Lingyu Zhang,Lanxuan Xue,Luodi Chen,Zepeng Xin,Kaiyu Li,Xiangyong Cao*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: AARRI-Bench is a new benchmark for testing whether AI agents behave like real research interns; current top agents perform far from human level, suggesting better research behavior modeling is needed beyond stronger scaffolding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although coding and autonomous research agents have become strong at long-horizon execution, they still lack field sensitivity, research ethics, and nuanced scientific judgment. Existing benchmarks emphasize macro-level task completion, leaving a gap in evaluating researcher professionalism and subtle reasoning.

Method: The paper introduces AARRI-Bench, the first benchmark in the AARR series, designed to evaluate whether AI agents can act like real research interns in fine-grained research scenarios. The authors run extensive experiments across frontier foundation models and agentic systems on this benchmark.

Result: Across extensive evaluations, even the best setup, Mini-SWE-Agent with Claude Opus 4.7, reaches only a 68.3% success rate and often misses subtle but important details that human researchers would catch.

Conclusion: Current frontier agents still cannot replace human researchers in granular research scenarios; improving researcher-like AI likely requires deeper modeling of research behavior and judgment, not just more complex agent scaffolding.

Abstract: As foundation models advance and agent scaffolding becomes increasingly sophisticated, agents have demonstrated remarkable proficiency in complex, long-horizon coding tasks and even autonomous experiment execution. Despite their evolution from research assistants into autonomous research agents, these systems still exhibit significant limitations in field sensitivity, research ethics, and nuanced scientific judgment. Consequently, frontier agents remain unable to fully replace human researchers. To bridge this gap, we conceptualize the AARR (Act As a Real Researcher) benchmark series. Unlike existing benchmarks that primarily assess macro-level execution capabilities, AARR focuses on whether agents can emulate the professionalism, thoroughness, and nuanced reasoning that characterize human researchers in granular research scenarios. In this work, we propose AARRI-Bench (Act As a Real Research Intern), the first benchmark in this series. We conduct extensive experiments across frontier models and agentic systems, revealing that even the best-performing configuration (Mini-SWE-Agent with Claude Opus 4.7) achieves only 68.3\% success rate, frequently overlooking subtle yet critical details that are obvious to real human researchers. Our results indicate that developing researcher-like AI requires further exploration of research behavior, rather than merely complex scaffolding. Our data is released at https://github.com/AARR-bench/AARRI-bench.

</details>


### [195] [How AI Agents Reshape Knowledge Work: Autonomy, Efficiency, and Scope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07489)
*Jeremy Yang,Kate Zyskowski,Noah Yonack,Jerry Ma*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Using real production data, the paper shows that autonomous AI agents do much more of the work than search-style assistants, finish tasks faster and cheaper, improve quality, and enable users to attempt more complex, broader knowledge-work tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
