Motivation: The motivation is to understand how frontier AI is transitioning from passive conversational assistance to autonomous task execution, and to quantify how this shift changes the speed, quality, cost, and scope of knowledge work in real-world usage.

Method: The study uses production data from Perplexity's Search and Computer products. It compares matched sessions with near-identical initial query pairs as natural experiments for the same underlying task, then measures autonomous work time, follow-up query patterns, dissatisfaction rates, completion time, estimated human time/cost, and task characteristics across products.

Result: Key results: Computer performs 26 minutes of autonomous work per session versus 33 seconds for Search; dissatisfaction is 55% lower; matched-task completion time drops from 269 to 36 minutes; estimated time and cost fall by 87% and 94%; and user tasks become more cross-domain, cognitively demanding, composite, and broader in scope.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that autonomous AI agents materially outperform conversational AI search assistants for knowledge work by executing end-to-end tasks, reducing completion time and cost, improving user satisfaction, and expanding the range and complexity of work users attempt.

Abstract: Frontier AI systems are bridging the gap between intelligence and utility by shifting from conversational assistants to autonomous agents that execute tasks end to end. Using production data from Perplexity's Search and Computer products, we study this transition by examining how AI agents accelerate and reshape knowledge work. Three key empirical findings emerge. First, using sessions with near-identical initial query pairs as natural experiments for the same underlying task attempted with both products, Computer performs 26 minutes of autonomous work per user session, versus 33 seconds for Search. Computer automates task decomposition and execution that Search users might otherwise manually orchestrate and implement. As a result, Computer shifts follow-up query distribution toward higher-order work such as verification and extension. Autonomy also increases execution quality, with per-query dissatisfaction rates 55% lower on Computer than on Search. Second, due to its autonomy advantage, Computer reduces completion time from 269 to 36 minutes on matched tasks, lowering estimated time and cost by 87% and 94%, respectively, compared to humans equipped with Search alone. Third, Computer changes the scope of work that users attempt: Computer queries more often cross occupational boundaries, require higher-order cognition, draw on broader expertise, take the form of composite tasks that bundle interdependent subtasks into a single query, and unlock work activities that are essentially absent from Search usage among the same users. Together, the evidence indicates that AI agents accelerate workflows, enhance output quality, reduce costs, and expand the breadth and depth of automated work.

</details>


### [196] [Severity-Aware Curriculum Learning with Multi-Model Response Selection for Medical Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05510)
*Ahmed Alansary,Molham Mohamed,Ali Hamdi*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Telehealth systems have become increasingly important for delivering accessible and timely medical information. Existing large language models often struggle to provide consistent and contextually appropriate medical responses across varying levels of case severity. This limitation highlights the need for models that can effectively adapt to the progressive complexity in medical queries. To address this challenge, we introduce a severity-aware multi-model framework that integrates curriculum training strategy with relevance-based response selection. The proposed framework employs a three-stage curriculum learning strategy, where each model is trained sequentially on mild, moderate, and critical cases to progressively acquire domain knowledge. The approach utilizes five large language models, each independently trained under the same curriculum scheme. During inference, all models generate candidate responses, and the most appropriate response is selected as the final output. The framework is trained and evaluated on the MAQA dataset, which provides annotated medical question-answer pairs. Experimental results evaluated using BERTScore demonstrate that the proposed method achieves superior performance compared to both baseline and fine-tuned models, attaining 86.71% in the baseline setting and 90.30% after fine-tuning. These results highlight the effectiveness of combining curriculum learning with multi-model response selection in improving response quality and relevance in medical text generation.

</details>


<div id='cs.LG'></div>

# cs.LG [[Back]](#toc)

### [197] [Elmes*: Automated Construction of Fine-Grained Evaluation Rubrics for Large Language Models in Long-Tail Educational Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06546)
*Tao Liu,Ye Lu,Ruohua Zhang,Siyu Song,Wentao Liu,Aimin Zhou,Hao Hao*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper introduces Elmes*, a scalable framework for evaluating how well LLMs teach using scenario-specific rubrics and a new benchmark, Edu-330. It shows that strong teaching performance is not the same as strong knowledge performance, and that specialized educational models can outperform general LLMs on pedagogical quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current LLM evaluation for education focuses too much on factual or domain-general correctness and relies on manually designed rubrics that do not scale well to diverse, long-tail pedagogical scenarios. The authors aim to measure how models teach, not just what they know, through scalable and fine-grained pedagogically grounded evaluation.

Method: The paper proposes Elmes*, an end-to-end evaluation framework that combines a declarative multi-agent teacher-student-judge interaction engine with SceneGen, a self-evolving module that jointly optimizes scenario-specific rubrics and test cases from expert-defined pedagogical dimensions. The framework is used to construct Edu-330, a benchmark spanning subjects, grade bands, task types, and fine-grained indicators.

Result: Using Edu-330 and expert-authored gold scenarios, the study finds that educational ability is highly multidimensional: leading LLMs differ mainly in creativity and values integration, knowledge-strong models can underperform on Socratic scaffolding, and the education-specialized InnoSpark achieves the best average human-evaluated score. LLM judges produce rankings comparable to humans with lower variance but show biases such as self-preference. Few-shot anchoring with expert scores improves human-LLM alignment, while reasoning enforcement and greedy decoding have model-dependent effects.

Conclusion: Elmes* provides a scalable and diagnostically rich infrastructure for evaluating the pedagogical capabilities of LLMs, revealing multidimensional strengths and weaknesses while enabling rubric-driven, scenario-specific assessment with human-comparable judge rankings.

Abstract: Evaluating large language models (LLMs) for education requires measuring how models teach, not only what they know. Existing benchmarks emphasize domain-general correctness or depend on manually designed rubrics that scale poorly to long-tail pedagogical scenarios. We introduce Elmes*, an end-to-end framework for constructing, refining, and applying fine-grained scenario-specific rubrics. Elmes* combines a declarative multi-agent engine for teacher--student--judge interactions with SceneGen, a self-evolving module that co-optimizes evaluation criteria and test data from expert-defined pedagogical dimensions. Using Elmes*, we build Edu-330, covering 330 scenarios across 11 subjects, 3 grade bands, and 10 task types, with over 1{,}000 second-level indicators. Experiments on Edu-330 and four expert-authored gold-standard scenarios show that educational capability is multidimensional: top-tier LLMs differ mainly in creativity and values integration, knowledge-strong models may fail at Socratic scaffolding, and the education-specialized InnoSpark achieves the best human-evaluated average score. LLM judges preserve human-comparable rankings with much lower scoring variance, but exhibit judge-specific biases such as self-preference. Ablations show that expert-scored few-shot anchoring improves human--LLM alignment, while reasoning enforcement and greedy decoding are model-dependent. Elmes* thus provides scalable diagnostic infrastructure for pedagogically grounded LLM evaluation.

</details>


### [198] [FAIR-Calib: Frontier-Aware Instability-Reweighted Calibration for Post-Training Quantization of Diffusion Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06547)
*Haoyu Huang,Linlin Yang,Sheng Xu,Boyu Liu,Guodong Guo,Zhongqian Fu,Hang Zhou,Baochang Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper identifies that quantization errors in diffusion LLMs are especially harmful at the token write frontier, where unstable decisions become permanently locked in. It introduces FAIR-Calib, a two-stage PTQ method that detects fragile frontier positions and upweights them during layer-wise calibration. This improves quantized dLLM performance and stability without expensive diffusion rollouts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In diffusion LLMs, tokens are refined iteratively but once written they are committed irreversibly. This creates a stability lag: early token decisions near the write frontier remain fragile. PTQ noise can flip these borderline decisions, after which the errors are locked in and amplified. Existing PTQ methods do not specifically protect these vulnerable frontier states.

Method: The paper proposes Frontier-Aware Instability-Reweighted Calibration (FAIR-Calib). Stage I uses a full-precision teacher to estimate a position prior from frontier-hit statistics and masked-stage reliability. Stage II performs off-policy, layer-wise PTQ calibration by minimizing a reweighted hidden-state MSE that emphasizes unstable frontier states, avoiding costly end-to-end diffusion rollouts. The paper also provides a theoretical link between the weighted objective and output KL divergence.

Result: Empirically, FAIR-Calib consistently surpasses state-of-the-art PTQ baselines on LLaDA and Dream in the W4A4 setting. It significantly reduces frontier decision flips and suppresses post-commit mismatches across multiple benchmarks.

Conclusion: FAIR-Calib is an effective two-stage post-training quantization framework for diffusion LLMs that better preserves fragile frontier decisions, reduces irreversible token-commit errors, and outperforms prior PTQ baselines under W4A4 quantization.

Abstract: Diffusion Large Language Models (dLLMs) refine tokens iteratively but commit them irreversibly, leading to a "stability lag" where early decisions remain fragile even after being written. We reveal that Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) error easily flips these borderline decisions at the write frontier, which are then permanently locked in and amplified. To address this, we propose Frontier-Aware Instability-Reweighted Calibration (FAIR-Calib), a two-stage PTQ framework for dLLMs. Stage I probes a full-precision teacher to estimate a position prior that combines frontier hits and masked-stage reliability. Stage II performs off-policy, layer-wise calibration by minimizing a reweighted hidden-state MSE, effectively prioritizing the protection of fragile frontier states without requiring expensive end-to-end diffusion rollouts. We further theoretically justify our weighted objective as a surrogate for output KL divergence. Empirically, FAIR-Calib consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on LLaDA and Dream (W4A4), significantly reducing frontier decision flips and suppressing post-commit mismatches across diverse benchmarks.

</details>


### [199] [Decision-Aware Evaluation of Physics-Informed Surrogates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07146)
*Daniel Cieślak,Andrzej Czyżewski*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: pinn-gym 是一个用于材料条件化晶格设计的 PIML 开放基准，强调用决策指标而不只是曲线误差来评估模型；实验表明低误差不等于好决策，物理约束带来权衡而非全指标提升。 / pinn-gym is an open benchmark for material-conditioned lattice design that evaluates PIML models by decision-oriented metrics, not just curve error; experiments show that low prediction error does not necessarily translate into good design decisions, and physics-informed losses introduce trade-offs rather than universal gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是当前 PIML 常以曲线拟合误差为核心指标，但真实工程设计更关注下游决策是否正确，例如能否选出优质候选、避免不可行设计、减少选择带来的损失。因此需要一个把“预测精度”与“决策质量”同时纳入的公开基准。 / The motivation is that PIML is usually assessed by curve-fitting error, while real engineering workflows depend on downstream decisions: selecting good candidates, avoiding infeasible designs, and limiting regret. This creates a need for an open benchmark that evaluates both predictive fidelity and decision usefulness.

Method: 方法上，作者提出 pinn-gym 基准：面向材料条件化的晶格设计，包含一个透明的降阶压溃-冲击 oracle、五种可打印聚合物材料卡、无量纲力响应目标，以及覆盖曲线保真度、物理可接受性、top-k 检索和质量 regret 的评测协议；并在按材料、混合材料、跨材料三种设置下比较 PIML 代理模型。 / Methodologically, the paper introduces pinn-gym, an open benchmark for material-conditioned lattice design. It combines a transparent reduced-order crush-impact oracle, five printable polymer material cards, dimensionless force-response targets, and an evaluation protocol spanning curve fidelity, physical admissibility, top-k retrieval, and mass regret across per-material, pooled, and cross-material settings.

Result: 结果显示：较低的 nRMSE 往往不足以保证挑出有用设计；物理信息损失会改变不同指标之间的权衡，而不是单调提升所有指标；无量纲条件化提高了可比性，但并未使跨材料迁移变得对称。 / The results show that low nRMSE is often not enough to identify useful designs, physics-informed losses shift trade-offs instead of uniformly improving all metrics, and dimensionless conditioning improves comparability without making transfer symmetric across materials.

Conclusion: 该论文认为，仅用曲线误差评估物理信息机器学习（PIML）并不足以反映工程价值；应结合候选排序、可制造/可行性过滤、top-k 检索和质量 regret 等决策指标。pinn-gym 提供了一个可复现实验平台，用于把代理模型作为决策系统而非仅作为曲线预测器来评估。 / The paper concludes that curve-error metrics alone are insufficient for judging the engineering value of physics-informed machine learning. It argues for evaluating surrogates as decision systems using metrics such as ranking quality, feasibility, top-k retrieval, and mass regret, and presents pinn-gym as a reproducible benchmark for that purpose.

Abstract: Physics-informed machine learning is often assessed by curve error, although engineering use depends on downstream decisions: ranking candidates, avoiding infeasible designs and limiting regret. We introduce pinn-gym, an open benchmark for material-conditioned lattice design that couples a transparent reduced-order crush-and-impact oracle with five printable polymer cards, dimensionless force-response targets and a protocol spanning curve fidelity, physical admissibility, top-k retrieval and mass regret.
  Across per-material, pooled and cross-material settings, low nRMSE is frequently insufficient to identify useful design selections. Physics-informed losses alter trade-offs rather than monotonically improving all metrics, and dimensionless conditioning improves comparability without making transfer symmetric. The benchmark is not a certified material model; within the released oracle, candidate generator and material cards, pinn-gym provides a reproducible testbed for evaluating PIML surrogates as decision systems rather than curve predictors alone.

</details>


### [200] [Multi-Scale Feature Attention Network for Polymer Classification using THz Dual-Comb Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06554)
*Roshni Mahtani,Ilán Carretero,Laura Monroy,Aldo Moreno-Oyervides,Oscar Elías Bonilla-Manrique,Rocío del Amor*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper classifies 12 polymer types from THz-DCS spectra using a new attention-based deep network, MSFAN, and achieves 85.2% accuracy, outperforming prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional sorting and spectroscopic methods are often insufficient for robust polymer discrimination, which is critical for the quality and safety of recycled plastics.

Method: The paper uses Terahertz Dual-Comb Spectroscopy to acquire spectral data from 12 polymer types and introduces a Multi-Scale Feature Attention Network with feature gating, parallel multi-scale convolutions, cross-feature attention, and attention pooling for classification.

Result: MSFAN outperforms state-of-the-art models on 12 polymer classes, including pure polymers, multilayer films, blends, and biopolymers, achieving 85.2% classification accuracy.

Conclusion: Combining THz-DCS with the proposed MSFAN enables effective, scalable, and interpretable classification of diverse polymers, showing strong potential for recycled-plastic identification.

Abstract: Reliable polymer identification is essential for ensuring the quality and safety of recycled plastics, yet conventional sorting and spectroscopic techniques often struggle to deliver robust discrimination. Terahertz Dual-Comb Spectroscopy (THz-DCS) offers a promising alternative, providing rapid, high-resolution, and non-destructive measurements. In this work, we leverage THz-DCS to classify 12 types of polymers, including pure polymers, multilayer films, commercial blends, and biopolymers. To handle the complexity of these spectral signals, we propose the Multi-Scale Feature Attention Network (MSFAN), a novel deep learning architecture tailored for THz-DCS data. The framework integrates feature gating for signal recalibration and multi-scale parallel convolutions to capture diverse frequency patterns. These features are further refined through cross-feature attention and attention pooling, enabling the model to intrinsically highlight the most informative THz regions. MSFAN consistently outperforms state-of-the-art models, reaching a classification accuracy of 85.2%. This study demonstrates the potential of combining THz-DCS with deep learning techniques for effective, scalable, and interpretable polymer classification.

</details>


### [201] [MacArena: Benchmarking Computer Use Agents on an Online macOS Environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06560)
*Victor Muryn,Maksym Shamrai,Sofiia Mazepa,Yehor Khodysko*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper introduces MacArena, a large Apple-Silicon-native macOS benchmark for GUI agents. Results show that agents doing well on OSWorld do not necessarily generalize to macOS, and macOS-native tasks are substantially harder for current models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing GUI-agent benchmarks underrepresent macOS. The only prior macOS benchmark is limited in app/task diversity and depends on x86 virtual machines that do not fit Apple Silicon. The authors aim to provide a broader, native macOS benchmark and test whether success on Linux-centric benchmarks truly reflects general GUI competence.

Method: The paper builds MacArena, a benchmark of 421 manually verified tasks across 50 applications, by combining ported OSWorld tasks, content from macOSWorld, and 49 newly designed macOS-native tasks, all executed on Apple Silicon using Apple’s native Virtualization framework. It then evaluates strong computer-use agents on different task subsets to compare cross-benchmark and cross-platform performance.

Result: MacArena demonstrates that macOS introduces distinct GUI challenges not captured by Linux-based benchmarks. Evaluation shows model rankings can invert between ported tasks and macOS-native tasks, and a leading model drops by more than 26% on the MacArena native subset, indicating weak cross-platform generalization.

Conclusion: MacArena fills an important benchmark gap for macOS GUI agents and shows that strong performance on existing benchmarks does not reliably transfer to macOS; current agents find macOS meaningfully harder, especially on native tasks.

Abstract: Computer-use agents (CUAs) operate graphical user interfaces (GUIs) through vision and control primitives, and their capabilities have advanced rapidly, driven in part by standardized online evaluation benchmarks such as OSWorld, which serve both as evaluation tools and as training environments for reinforcement learning. However, macOS remains underserved in this landscape: the only existing benchmark, macOSWorld, covers a narrow slice of first-party applications with simpler tasks, and runs on x86 virtual machines incompatible with Apple Silicon. We introduce MacArena, a benchmark of 421 manually verified tasks spanning 50 applications that combines a curated port of OSWorld tasks, content sourced from macOSWorld, and 49 new macOS-native tasks, all running on Apple's native Virtualization framework on Apple Silicon. We argue that macOS presents distinct GUI challenges beyond what Linux-based benchmarks capture, and our evaluation supports this claim: strong model performance on existing benchmarks can reflect familiarity with task distributions rather than genuine cross-platform GUI competence. Notably, model rankings invert between ported and macOS-native tasks, with a leading model trailing by over 26% on the MacArena subset, suggesting that macOS poses a genuinely harder environment for current GUI agents.

</details>


### [202] [WAV: Multi-Resolution Block Residual Routing for Deep Decoder-Only Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06564)
*Kehan Wang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: WAV v1 adds two cheap directional residual features to block-level routing in deep Transformers, preserving more within-block structure and yielding the strongest gains at 48 layers with almost no extra parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing residual routing methods improve over fixed residual accumulation, but block-level summaries only capture total low-frequency residual displacement and lose directional information such as attention-MLP imbalance and temporal dynamics within a block. The paper aims to preserve this missing structure while keeping routing efficient.

Method: The paper proposes WAV v1, a lightweight residual routing mechanism that augments each Transformer block summary with two directional detail bases: a phase basis contrasting attention vs. MLP updates, and a split basis contrasting early vs. late sublayer updates. These are routed with the standard block summaries through a depth-wise softmax mixer, with negative detail-source initialization and detached RMS matching for training stability.

Result: On character-level TinyStories and Text8 language modeling, WAV v1 is not consistently better at 12 layers, becomes competitive at 24 layers, and achieves the best performance at 48 layers. At 48 layers, it improves validation loss over Block AttnRes from 0.4960 to 0.4738 on TinyStories and from 0.9363 to 0.9305 on Text8, with negligible extra parameters.

Conclusion: WAV v1 demonstrates that adding multi-resolution directional residual details to block-level residual routing improves scaling in deeper decoder-only Transformers, especially at 48 layers, with minimal parameter overhead.

Abstract: Residual connections are central to training deep Transformers, but standard PreNorm residual streams aggregate sublayer updates with fixed unit weights. Recent Attention Residuals replace this fixed accumulation with content-dependent depth-wise routing, and Block Attention Residuals make the mechanism efficient by routing over block-level residual summaries. However, a single block summary stores only the low-frequency total residual displacement inside a block, discarding directional structure such as attention-vs-MLP imbalance and early-vs-late block dynamics. We propose WAV v1, a lightweight multi-resolution residual routing method for decoder-only Transformers. Instead of representing each block only by its accumulated residual sum, WAV v1 augments every block with two directional detail bases: a phase basis that contrasts attention and MLP updates, and a split basis that contrasts early and late sublayer updates. These bases are routed together with standard block summaries through the same depth-wise softmax mixer, while negative detail-source initialization and detached RMS matching stabilize training. On character-level TinyStories and Text8 language modeling, WAV v1 shows a clear depth-dependent benefit. Although it is not consistently beneficial at 12 layers, it becomes competitive at 24 layers and outperforms all baselines at 48 layers. At 48 layers, WAV v1 reduces validation loss relative to Block AttnRes from 0.4960 to 0.4738 on TinyStories and from 0.9363 to 0.9305 on Text8, with negligible additional parameters. These results suggest that directional residual details, not only block-level sums, are important for scaling residual routing in deeper Transformers.

</details>


### [203] [Are you sure? A Comprehensive and Comprehensible Survey of Uncertainty Quantification in Symbolic Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06567)
*Julia Reuter,Fabricio Olivetti de Franca*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A first survey on uncertainty quantification in symbolic regression: it explains why UQ matters, reviews existing approaches under frequentist, Bayesian, and model selection perspectives, and argues that the field still needs substantial research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although SR has advanced in discovering interpretable mathematical models, its limited support for UQ restricts real-world adoption. UQ is needed to assess model reliability, mitigate overfitting, and support better decisions under uncertainty.

Method: This is a survey paper. It introduces core UQ concepts and reviews existing literature on UQ for SR, organizing prior work into three main directions: frequentist methods, Bayesian methods, and model-selection-based approaches.

Result: The survey identifies the current landscape of UQ in SR, clarifies the main methodological categories, and highlights that the area is still underdeveloped compared with the broader progress in SR.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that uncertainty quantification (UQ) in symbolic regression (SR) is important but remains underexplished, and that more reliable UQ methods are needed for practical deployment of SR in decision-making.

Abstract: Symbolic regression (SR) is a class of methods that systematically explore the space of mathematical functions to discover models that accurately capture the underlying relationships in a dataset. Despite recent advances in the field, a lack of support for uncertainty quantification (UQ) limits its adoption in real-world decision processes. In regression analysis, UQ provides important information about the model reliability, which can both help to avoid overfitting by accounting for uncertainty in the data, and provide insights for decision-making. This survey is the first to clearly address this issue, with the objective of introducing essential UQ concepts and reviewing the current literature on UQ in SR, which can be broadly organized into three research directions: frequentist, Bayesian, and model selection. Despite its importance, UQ in SR is still underexplored, which motivates further research into reliable UQ methods for SR.

</details>


### [204] [Generative Models Erode Human Temporal Learning Through Market Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06572)
*Wenjun Cao*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Generative AI may undermine incentives for long-term human learning because cheap AI outputs increasingly look like expert human work, making verification too costly and causing markets to reward output quality without rewarding the years of human learning behind it.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to explain a structural economic risk from current generative models: as AI outputs increasingly mimic HTL-intensive human work, distinguishing authentic human learning from generated outputs becomes harder and less worth the cost, threatening incentives for sustained human knowledge production.

Method: The paper introduces the concept of Human Temporal Learning (HTL), defines a costly-inspection framework to formalize verification erosion and value collapse, and maps cross-domain evidence from academic publishing, legal practice, content platforms, and software security onto a four-stage process.

Result: The analysis shows that alignment success does not remove the core risk; in fact, better-aligned models can worsen the problem by making AI outputs closer to human work in observable features, thereby increasing source-verification difficulty and competitive pressure on HTL-intensive labor.

Conclusion: Modern generative models can cause a 'value collapse' in knowledge and cultural production even before AGI, because verification of whether outputs reflect genuine human temporal learning becomes too costly to justify, shifting rewards toward cheap AI-produced outputs and disadvantaging long-term human expertise.

Abstract: We argue that modern generative models create structural risks for knowledge and cultural production at current, sub-AGI capability levels. We define Human Temporal Learning (HTL) as path-dependent knowledge accumulation through sustained engagement with problems over time. Generative outputs increasingly resemble HTL-intensive work in surface features, so verifying whether a given output reflects genuine human learning grows costly relative to its expected benefit. Once verification loses economic justification, evaluators reward outputs regardless of production mode, and producers who invested years of learning compete on price against outputs that cost almost nothing to generate. We call this pathway value collapse and formalize it through a costly-inspection framework. Cross-domain evidence from academic publishing, legal practice, content platforms, and software security maps onto four stages of verification erosion. Alignment success is orthogonal. Better-aligned models narrow observable gaps between human and AI outputs, making source verification harder and intensifying competitive pressure against HTL-intensive work even when individual AI outputs improve.

</details>


### [205] [Towards Serverless Semi-Decentralized Federated Learning with Heterogeneous Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06687)
*Su Wang,Mung Chiang,H. Vincent Poor*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper introduces SSD-FL, a serverless clustered decentralized FL framework that handles heterogeneous optimizers and data by using a one-time D2D clustering phase, optimizer-aware effective loss functions, and a Cheeger-inequality-based clustering algorithm, yielding better convergence and communication efficiency than existing decentralized FL methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing clustering methods in federated learning have mainly been studied in centralized settings. In fully decentralized FL, cluster formation is underexplored and difficult because of the coupling among network topology, data heterogeneity, and heterogeneous local optimizers. The work aims to achieve scalable and efficient decentralized FL without relying on persistent server infrastructure.

Method: The paper proposes Serverless Semi-Decentralized Federated Learning (SSD-FL), which performs a one-time lightweight device-to-device initialization for cluster formation, then runs fully serverless training with intra-cluster and inter-cluster rounds. It introduces effective loss functions combining optimizer-aware dynamics with graph regularization, derives convergence and consensus bounds, and designs an iterative clustering algorithm using the consensus gap and Cheeger inequality.

Result: Experiments across multiple decentralized FL baselines, network graphs, datasets, and optimizer settings show that SSD-FL achieves faster convergence and better communication efficiency. Theoretical analysis also supports global convergence and consensus under the proposed clustered serverless framework.

Conclusion: SSD-FL demonstrates that cluster formation can be effectively integrated into decentralized federated learning without persistent servers, improving convergence and communication efficiency under heterogeneous optimizers, data distributions, and network topologies.

Abstract: We investigate cluster formation, involving the number and composition of clusters, in decentralized federated learning (FL) with heterogeneous machine learning (ML) optimizers. While clustering in centralized FL has enabled scalability and resource savings, its value and development in fully decentralized environments have yet to be explored. Optimizing cluster formation in such environments is challenging, especially due to the complex coupling between network graph structures, local data heterogeneity, and different local ML model optimizers. To address these challenges, we propose serverless semi-decentralized FL (SSD-FL), a methodology requiring no persistent server infrastructure. In SSD-FL, cluster formation occurs via a lightweight, one-time device-to-device (D2D) initialization phase, after which actual ML model training (alongside consensus and convergence processes) is fully serverless. Functionally, SSD-FL segments global rounds into intra-cluster and inter-cluster regimes, ensuring global convergence and consensus through novel "effective loss functions" that integrate device-specific ML optimizers with network graph-based regularization. Next, SSD-FL leverages the consensus gap via the Cheeger inequality to develop an iterative clustering algorithm evaluated against our derived convergence and consensus bounds, which incorporate a unique scoring metric to quantify data and optimizer heterogeneity across devices. Finally, experimental evaluation against three categories of decentralized FL methodologies validate that SSD-FL improves both convergence speeds and communication efficiency across various network graphs, datasets, and local optimizer regimes.

</details>


### [206] [Skip a Layer or Loop It? Learning Program-of-Layers in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06574)
*Ziyue Li,Yang Li,Tianyi Zhou*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: PoLar把LLM层当作可编程模块，按输入动态决定跳过或重复哪些层。结果表明，很多样本不需要完整前向传播就能达到甚至超过原始精度，说明LLM内部存在多种有效的潜在推理路径。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有LLM推理通常采用固定层数、固定顺序、非循环的前向执行方式，这种范式可能限制了模型已学到的潜在计算路径。作者希望验证：是否存在无需重新训练主模型、仅通过重组预训练层就能适配不同输入、提升效率与准确率的动态推理程序。

Method: 论文提出Program-of-Layers (PoLar)框架：将预训练模型层视为可组合模块，为每个输入构造动态执行程序，允许跳过某些层或重复某些层；同时设计一个轻量级PoLar prediction network，预测每个输入应采用的层执行程序，从而高效实现动态推理。

Result: 在数学推理基准测试中，PoLar相较标准推理和先前动态深度方法表现出更高准确率，并且常常使用更少的层执行；对于原始LLM预测错误的样本，替代性PoLar程序有时能够纠正错误；这些收益在分布外评测中依然存在。

Conclusion: 固定深度、固定顺序的标准前向传播只利用了LLM潜在推理能力中的一小部分；通过为每个输入动态选择层的跳过与重复执行，PoLar可以在更少计算下获得更高或相当的准确率，并在分布外场景中保持优势。

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) perform inference by following a fixed depth and order, non-recurrent execution of all layers. We reveal the wide existence of training-free, flexible, dynamic program-of-layers (PoLar), where pretrained layers can be packed as modules and then skipped or looped to form a customized program for each input. For most inputs, substantially shorter program executions can achieve the same or better accuracy, while incorrect predictions of the original LLM can be corrected by alternative programs with fewer layers. These observations indicate that inference admits multiple valid latent computations beyond the standard forward pass. To efficiently achieve PoLar in practice, we propose a lightweight PoLar prediction network, which learns to generate execution programs that dynamically skip or repeat pretrained layers for each input. Experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that PoLar consistently improves accuracy over standard inference and prior dynamic-depth methods, often while executing fewer layers, and that these gains persist under out-of-distribution evaluation. Our results suggest that fixed-depth execution captures only a narrow subset of an LLM's latent reasoning capacity.

</details>


### [207] [Gaussian Process Latent Factor Regression for Low-Data, High-Dimensional Output Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06576)
*Edward T. Stevenson,Eric T. Wolf,Mei Ting Mak,N. J. Mayne,Miles Cranmer*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GPLFR replaces PCA-GP with a single probabilistic model that learns a prediction-aware low-dimensional latent space for high-dimensional regression under scarce data, and it is validated on exoplanet climate emulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard multi-output Gaussian processes work well with limited data but do not scale effectively to high-dimensional outputs. Existing compress-then-predict methods such as PCA-GP reduce dimensionality, but their latent bases are optimized for reconstruction rather than predictive performance. The paper aims to bridge this mismatch.

Method: The paper proposes Gaussian Process Latent Factor Regression (GPLFR), where each high-dimensional output is generated by a linear-Gaussian decoder from a low-dimensional latent state governed by a Gaussian process prior. The decoder weights are analytically marginalized, producing a joint objective that couples compression and prediction while remaining scalable to high-dimensional outputs.

Result: The model scales to high-dimensional outputs and better aligns representation learning with prediction. As an application, the authors build the first spatially resolved emulator of global climate models for rocky exoplanets.

Conclusion: GPLFR unifies dimensionality reduction and regression for high-dimensional, low-data scientific prediction, and is demonstrated by enabling the first spatially resolved emulator of global climate models for rocky exoplanets.

Abstract: In the sciences, regression tasks often require predicting high-dimensional outputs from few training examples. Multi-output Gaussian processes excel in low-data regimes but typically struggle with high-dimensional outputs. Compress-then-predict pipelines such as PCA-GP (principal component analysis plus Gaussian process regression) handle high dimensionality, but rely on bases optimized for reconstruction rather than prediction. To address this gap, we propose a model that represents each output as a linear-Gaussian decoding of a low-dimensional latent state drawn from a Gaussian process prior. By analytically marginalizing the decoder weights, we couple compression and prediction in a single objective that scales to high-dimensional outputs. We refer to this model as Gaussian process latent factor regression (GPLFR). We demonstrate GPLFR by building the first spatially resolved emulator of global climate models for rocky exoplanets.

</details>


### [208] [Principles and Practice of Deep Representation Learning: or a Mathematical Theory of Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06624)
*San Buchanan,Druv Pai,Peng Wang,Yi Ma*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 这不是一篇报告实验结果的论文摘要，而是一本书的导言：核心观点是用表征学习、优化和信息论来解释深度网络，减少对“炼金术式”架构设计的依赖，并发展出更可解释、可控且高效的模型。 / This is not a standard experimental paper abstract but an introduction to a book: its central claim is that deep networks can be explained through representation learning, optimization, and information theory, reducing reliance on trial-and-error architecture design and enabling more interpretable, controllable, and efficient models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于当前大型生成模型投入巨大但内部机制不透明，导致可解释性、可靠性与可控性不足，并引发过度炒作与担忧，因此需要打开深度网络这一“黑箱”。 / The motivation is that current large generative models require major investment yet remain opaque, creating problems in interpretability, reliability, and control, as well as both hype and fear; therefore, there is a need to open the black box of deep networks.

Method: 方法上，本书采用理论综述与机制解释相结合的方式，围绕表征学习，用优化理论和信息论解释现代神经网络架构设计原则，并将这些原则进一步用于方法构建与应用分析。 / Methodologically, the book combines theoretical synthesis and mechanistic explanation, using representation learning as the core lens and applying optimization and information theory to explain modern neural architecture design and derive new methods.

Result: 结果上，摘要表明本书将系统梳理深度学习的核心原理，解释架构设计的理论基础，并展示如何据此得到更具范式性的新方法与模型；但摘要未提供实验性定量结果。 / In terms of results, the abstract indicates that the book will systematically organize the core principles of deep learning, explain the theoretical basis of architecture design, and show how these ideas yield new, more principled methods and models; however, it does not provide quantitative experimental results.

Conclusion: 本书主张可以通过表征学习、优化与信息论视角系统理解大型深度网络的内部机制，从而构建更高效、可解释、可控且性能不弱于黑箱模型的方法。 / The book concludes that large deep networks can be understood systematically through representation learning, optimization, and information theory, enabling methods that are efficient, interpretable, controllable, and competitive with black-box models.

Abstract: In the current era of deep learning and especially generative models, there is significant investment in training very large generative models. Thus far, such models have been "black boxes" that are difficult to understand in the sense that they have opaque internal mechanisms, leading to difficulties in interpretability, reliability, and control. Naturally, this lack of understanding has led to both hype and fear.
  This book is an attempt to "open the black box" and understand the mechanisms of large deep networks, through the perspective of representation learning, which is a major factor - arguably the single most important one - in the empirical power of deep learning models. A brief outline of this book is as follows. Chapter 1 will summarize the threads that underlie the whole text. Chapters 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6 will explain the design principles of modern neural network architectures through optimization and information theory, reducing the process of architecture development (long having been described as a sort of "alchemy") to undergraduate-level linear algebra and calculus exercises once the underlying principles are introduced. Chapters 7 and 8 will discuss applications of these principles to solve problems in more paradigmatic ways, obtaining new methods and models which are efficient, interpretable, and controllable by design, and yet no less - sometimes even more - powerful than the black-box models they resemble. Chapter 9 will discuss potential future directions for deep learning, the role of representation learning, as well as some open problems.

</details>


### [209] [The Identity Trap in EEG Foundation Models: A Diagnostic Audit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06647)
*Jun-You Lin,Ying Choon Wu,Tzyy-Ping Jung*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: FMScope shows that EEG foundation models often rely heavily on subject identity rather than true clinical biomarkers; this identity signal is strong, partly physiological, often linearly removable, and can be separated from genuine biomarker-related gains before fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although EEG foundation models show high accuracy under subject-disjoint cross-validation, that performance may come either from genuine disease-related biomarkers or from subject-specific identity cues correlated with labels. The paper aims to diagnose this ambiguity at the representation level before fine-tuning.

Method: The authors propose FMScope, a frozen-representation diagnostic protocol with five components: variance decomposition, subject-axis erasure, aperiodic 1/f ablation, layer-wise label probing, and within-subject direction consistency. They evaluate three pretrained EEG foundation models (LaBraM, CBraMod, REVE) on four datasets arranged by whether labels vary within subject and whether a known cross-subject EEG marker exists.

Result: Across all 12 model-dataset pairs, frozen subject variance was 13-89 times a random null and increased further after fine-tuning by 10-63 percentage points. Removing the linear subject axis improved label decoding when labels varied within subject, with gains of 6-12 percentage points in primary cells and 4-27 points in external cohorts. Removing aperiodic 1/f reduced subject probing by 9-19 points for LaBraM and CBraMod, while REVE showed strong identity encoding without measurable dependence on aperiodic content. Fine-tuning increased label variance only in settings with literature-supported cross-subject EEG markers.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that the Identity Trap is widespread in EEG foundation models: strong subject-identity information persists in frozen representations and often increases after fine-tuning. FMScope can distinguish performance gains driven by real cross-subject biological markers from gains driven by shortcut learning through subject identity.

Abstract: Objective. EEG foundation models (FMs) report strong accuracy on clinical resting-state EEG. However, high accuracy under subject-disjoint cross-validation remains ambiguous: it can reflect a genuine clinical biomarker, or subject-identity features that correlate with the label. We name this the Identity Trap and ask whether it can be diagnosed at the representation level before fine-tuning.
  Approach. We propose FMScope, a frozen-representation protocol packaging five diagnostics: variance decomposition, subject-axis erasure, aperiodic 1/f ablation, layer-wise label probing, and within-subject direction consistency. We apply it to three pretrained FMs (LaBraM, CBraMod, REVE) across four datasets in a 2x2 layout: subject relation of label x presence of a consensus cross-subject EEG marker.
  Main results. (i) The Identity Trap is universal: frozen subject-variance is 13-89x a random null in 12/12 pairs, rising in all 12 under fine-tuning (+10 to +63 pp). This dominance is a removable linear axis: erasing it improves label decoding where the label varies within subject (+6 to +12 pp in primary cells; +4 to +27 pp across external cohorts). (ii) Aperiodic 1/f is one subject carrier: removing it drops the subject probe by 9-19 pp on LaBraM and CBraMod. REVE saturates subject identity without measurable aperiodic dependence. (iii) Fine-tuning amplifies label-variance only in cells with a literature-established cross-subject marker.
  Significance. The Identity Trap is a physically-grounded instance of shortcut learning: the preferred cue has a measurable physiological component, and subject-disjoint splitting alone cannot rule it out. FMScope separates gains reflecting a biological marker from those reflecting subject identity.

</details>


### [210] [Capturing non-Markovian dynamics in non-equilibrium stochastic systems using flow matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06658)
*Bhargav Sriram Siddani,John B. Bell,Alejandro L. Garcia,Ishan Srivastava*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A flow-matching generative model learns particle flux distributions directly from simulations, capturing non-Markovian and non-Gaussian effects better than the regularized Dean-Kawasaki equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing hydrodynamic/SPDE models such as the regularized Dean-Kawasaki equation fail to describe short-time dynamics dominated by memory effects and low-density regimes with strongly non-Gaussian distributions. The work is motivated by the need for a more accurate coarse-grained model in these settings.

Method: The authors develop a generative flow matching method to directly learn the probability distribution of fluxes from particle-level simulations, explicitly incorporating non-Markovian memory effects and non-Gaussian statistics. They demonstrate it on the Kramers first passage time problem for non-interacting Brownian particles and compare against the regularized Dean-Kawasaki equation.

Result: The proposed model accurately captures short-time behavior and yields better predictions for the statistical moments of particle number density than the Markovian baseline given by the regularized Dean-Kawasaki equation.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a generative flow matching approach can model flux distributions from particle simulations more accurately than coarse-grained Markovian SPDE baselines, especially for short-time dynamics and low-density regimes where non-Markovian and non-Gaussian effects are important.

Abstract: Hydrodynamic models of stochastic particle systems represented by coarse-grained stochastic partial differential equations (SPDE), such as the regularized Dean-Kawasaki (DK) equation, do not accurately capture the short-time system dynamics that is dominated by non-Markovian effects, and low particle density regimes where the distributions are highly non-Gaussian. We develop a generative flow matching method that directly models the probability distribution of fluxes from particle simulations that explicitly incorporates non-Markovian and non-Gaussian effects. As a demonstration, we use this method to simulate the Kramers first passage time problem for a system of non-interacting Brownian particles. We show the model accurately captures the short-time behavior and provides better predictions of the statistical moments of the number density when compared against the solution of the Markovian baseline, regularized DK equation.

</details>


### [211] [Explainable Runtime Dependency Tracking for AI-RAN Conflict Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06663)
*Christie Djidjev,Nicholas Kaminski*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A Boolean-matrix sliding-window tracker monitors changing parameter-KPI dependencies in AI-RAN from telemetry streams, providing an efficient and explainable signal for conflict diagnosis and model refresh.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In AI-integrated Radio Access Networks, multiple intelligent applications act on shared parameters and KPIs. For conflict monitoring and runtime diagnosis, the system needs not only deployment awareness but also continuous validation that assumed parameter-KPI dependencies still hold under the current operating regime.

Method: The method represents active parameter-KPI dependencies as a Boolean matrix, checks consistency between the current estimate and streaming telemetry events using Boolean matrix multiplication, and applies a sliding-window inference procedure that reuses the estimate when consistent and recomputes it when structural change is detected.

Result: Experiments on controlled Boolean event streams show that the proposed tracker can efficiently and accurately follow dependency changes and remain robust under Boolean observation noise.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a lightweight sliding-window tracker based on a Boolean dependency matrix can efficiently and accurately monitor whether assumed parameter-KPI dependencies remain valid in AI-RAN, even when dependencies change over time and observations include Boolean noise.

Abstract: Future AI-integrated Radio Access Networks (AI-RAN) will combine open programmability with learning-enabled xApps, rApps, and control functions that act on shared parameters and key performance indicators (KPIs). For conflict monitoring, it is not enough to know which applications are deployed; the system must also know whether the parameter--KPI dependencies assumed by runtime diagnosis remain valid under the current operating regime. This paper studies a lightweight monitoring primitive for that purpose: tracking an interpretable dependency representation from streaming telemetry events.
  We represent active dependencies by a Boolean matrix and use Boolean matrix multiplication to check whether recent parameter-activity and KPI-response events are consistent with the current estimate. We propose a sliding-window inference procedure that reuses the estimate when it remains consistent and recomputes it when recent observations indicate structural change. The tracker is intended as an explainable signal for conflict diagnosis and slow-loop model refresh, not as an autonomous mitigation mechanism. Experiments on controlled Boolean event streams show efficient and accurate tracking under dependency changes and Boolean observation noise.

</details>


### [212] [Uncertainty-Aware LLM-Guided Policy Shaping for Sparse-Reward Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06673)
*Ujjwal Bhatta,Utsabi Dangol,Sumaly Bajracharya,Rodrigue Rizk,KC Santosh*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: ULPS用A*生成示范轨迹训练语言模型，并结合不确定性估计来动态调节LLM对PPO策略的指导强度，从而在稀疏奖励多任务RL中更快、更稳、更省样本。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 论文旨在解决强化学习中长期存在的稀疏奖励与异构任务序列问题，这些问题通常导致收敛缓慢、泛化能力差和探索效率低。

Method: 该方法使用A* oracle生成最优符号轨迹，用这些数据微调基于BERT的语言模型；在RL训练过程中，语言模型提供动作建议，并通过MC dropout估计认知不确定性；随后利用基于熵的混合机制，自适应地在LLM引导与PPO学习策略之间分配权重。

Result: 在MiniGridUnlockPickup基准上，ULPS相较于无引导、未校准以及标准RL基线，在成功率、奖励效率和样本复杂度上都有稳定提升；微调后执行准确率提升超过9%，环境交互次数更少，reward AUC更高。

Conclusion: ULPS通过将符号轨迹、预训练语言先验与不确定性感知控制结合起来，在稀疏奖励、多任务强化学习中提供了一种有效且原则化的方法，并显示出扩展到部分可观测和多智能体场景的潜力。

Abstract: Sparse rewards and heterogeneous task sequences remain persistent challenges in Reinforcement Learning (RL), often resulting in slow convergence, weak generalization, and inefficient exploration. We propose Uncertainty-Aware LLM-Guided Policy Shaping (ULPS), a novel framework that integrates a calibrated Large Language Model (LLM) into the RL training loop to provide structured, uncertainty-modulated behavioral guidance. ULPS employs an A*-based oracle to synthesize optimal symbolic trajectories, which are used to fine-tune a BERT-based language model. During training, this model supplies action suggestions whose influence is conditioned on epistemic uncertainty estimated via Monte Carlo (MC) dropout. An entropy-based blending mechanism adaptively balances LLM guidance and the learned policy (via Proximal Policy Optimization, PPO), allowing the agent to prioritize reliable priors while preserving adaptability. We evaluate ULPS on the MiniGridUnlockPickup benchmark and observe consistent improvements in success rate, reward efficiency, and sample complexity over unguided, uncalibrated, and standard RL baselines. ULPS achieves more than 9% improvement in execution accuracy after fine-tuning, requires fewer environment interactions, and yields higher reward AUC. Our results demonstrate that integrating symbolic A* trajectories, pretrained language priors, and uncertainty-aware control offers a principled and effective approach to multi-task reinforcement learning in sparse-reward domains, with potential extensibility to partially observable and multi-agent settings.

</details>


### [213] [Spatiotemporal Imputation with Graph-Informed Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06682)
*Zepeng Zhang,Aref Einizade,Jhony H. Giraldo,Olga Fink*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GiFlow improves missing-value imputation in spatiotemporal data by using a graph-informed prior and a hybrid spatial-temporal flow model, delivering better performance than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing spatiotemporal imputation methods face two major issues: iterative propagation in recurrent and graph neural networks causes error accumulation across time and space, while diffusion-based methods reduce this issue but are inefficient due to iterative sampling and are often limited by generic Gaussian priors that do not match the task structure well.

Method: The paper proposes GiFlow, a Graph-Informed Flow Matching framework. It replaces the standard Gaussian prior with a graph-informed prior derived from spatiotemporal filtering of observed signals, so the source distribution is closer to the target distribution. The flow field is modeled by a hybrid vector field that integrates spatial attention, temporal attention, and spatiotemporal propagation.

Result: Extensive experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show that GiFlow outperforms state-of-the-art baselines for spatiotemporal imputation.

Conclusion: GiFlow is an effective and efficient framework for spatiotemporal imputation. By combining a graph-informed prior with a hybrid vector field for joint spatial-temporal dependency modeling, it achieves state-of-the-art performance on both synthetic and real-world datasets.

Abstract: Missing data is a common challenge in spatiotemporal systems, arising in applications such as air quality monitoring and urban traffic management. Traditional machine learning approaches, like recurrent and graph neural networks, rely on iterative propagation, which tends to accumulate errors over time and space. Recent diffusion-based methods mitigate error propagation but require iterative sampling and often depend on problem-agnostic Gaussian priors, limiting both efficiency and effectiveness. To address these limitations, we propose GiFlow, a Graph-Informed Flow Matching framework for spatiotemporal imputation. GiFlow replaces the typical Gaussian prior with a graph-informed prior constructed via spatiotemporal filtering of observable signals, which better aligns the source distribution to the target and thereby simplifies the generation trajectory. The flow field is parameterized by a hybrid vector field model that integrates spatial attention, temporal attention, and spatiotemporal propagation, enabling joint modeling of spatial and temporal dependencies. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate that the proposed GiFlow outperforms the state-of-the-art approaches in spatiotemporal imputation. The code is available at https://github.com/zepengzhang/GiFlow.

</details>


### [214] [The Geography of Algorithmic Judgment: LLM Intermediaries, Place Identity, and Racial Steering in Housing Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06694)
*Hana Samad,Trung Lam,Christoph Mügge-Durum,Michael Akinwumi*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This content has not passed the compliance test and has been hidden.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This content has not passed the compliance test and has been hidden.

Method: This content has not passed the compliance test and has been hidden.

Result: This content has not passed the compliance test and has been hidden.

Conclusion: This content has not passed the compliance test and has been hidden.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are rapidly assuming an intermediary role in housing search through the integration of listing platforms within conversational interfaces, mediating access to information, search, and recommendations within urban settings. We expand on prior work on racial steering in LLMs by conducting a behavioral audit of seven open-weight and closed-source LLMs across four U.S. cities, testing location recommendations across three iterative prompting conditions that progressively add lifestyle preference context and reflect fair housing paired-testing methodologies. We find that steering is an emergent behavior of the model's interpretive license rather than primarily a static property. Steering results from the interaction of a user's identity, preference articulation, and the spatial logic that a model has internalized about learned representations of place, preference, and opportunity in a given city, and how different types of users relate to it. While steering was present, it was not uniform in direction or magnitude across evaluated conditions. Preference-conditioned testing often increased or reconfigured the number of models that exhibited steering behaviors relative to baseline conditions, suggesting that LLMs may interpret what the same housing preference means differently depending on the racial identity of the user. Our findings also demonstrate that the city is not a neutral testing unit for LLM evaluation in place-based sectors, and results from one local market cannot be assumed to generalize to another. Local and domain expertise will be required in the housing sector to ensure that legal and institutional commitments to fair housing are not undermined while adopting AI tools that mediate spatial access.

</details>


### [215] [RECAP: Regression Evaluation for Continual Adaptation of Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06698)
*Harsh Deshpande,Kushal Chawla,Sangwoo Cho,William Campbell*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: RECAP是一个针对“约束变了、模型必须立刻适应”的新基准。结果表明，现有提示优化方法在这种主动适应场景下基本失效，且更慢，因此需要新的方法。 RECAP is a new benchmark for situations where constraints change and the model must adapt immediately. Results show current prompt-optimization methods largely fail in this proactive setting and are slower, so new methods are needed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现实中的生产级agent系统经常面临即时生效的约束变化，例如合规阈值调整或新增披露要求，系统必须从下一轮交互开始遵守；但现有基准大多假设约束静态，或允许基于评估反馈进行被动调整，无法反映这种真实部署需求。 Production agentic systems often face constraints that change and take effect immediately, such as updated compliance thresholds or new disclosure rules, requiring compliance from the very next interaction. Existing benchmarks mostly assume static constraints or reactive adaptation with evaluation feedback, so they fail to capture this real deployment setting.

Method: 论文提出RECAP基准，在严格的主动适应协议下评估持续学习现象：方法只能看到新的约束规范，不能看到测试数据，然后立即在新约束上被测试。作者在4个LLM、6种方法、3种约束演化日程上进行评测，并从约束级别分析遗忘、回归和前向迁移。 The paper introduces RECAP, a benchmark that evaluates continual-learning effects under a strict proactive protocol: methods receive only the updated constraint specification, not test examples, and must generalize immediately. The authors test 6 methods across 4 LLMs and 3 evolving-constraint schedules, measuring forgetting, regression, and forward transfer at the constraint level.

Result: 在所有评测设置中，六种方法整体上没有带来显著性能改善，反而伴随更高延迟，说明当前方法难以在主动约束适应场景下稳定泛化。 Across the evaluated settings, the six methods show no significant overall performance improvement and incur higher latency, indicating that current approaches struggle to generalize reliably in proactive constraint adaptation.

Conclusion: 现有为离线或被动设置设计的提示优化方法，在严格的主动式“先适应后测试”约束持续演化场景中表现不足；即使增加延迟，也未带来显著性能提升，因此需要专门面向部署中动态约束的主动提示适应方法。 Existing prompt optimization methods designed for offline or reactive settings are inadequate for strict proactive adapt-then-test scenarios with evolving constraints; even with higher latency, they show no significant performance gains, motivating new proactive prompt adaptation methods for deployment-time dynamic constraints.

Abstract: Production agentic systems routinely face evolving constraints and must comply from the very next interaction. Scenarios like a tool-call notification changing a compliance threshold or a policy update adding disclosure requirements fit this criteria, having close to no room for errors in production. This proactive adaptation setting is common in deployment, but absent from current benchmarks, which assume either static constraint sets or reactive protocols with evaluation feedback. We introduce RECAP, a benchmark that measures continual-learning phenomena (forgetting, regression, forward transfer) at the constraint level under a strictly proactive adapt-then-test protocol: prompt optimization methods receive only the constraint specification and must generalize before seeing any test data. Evaluating six methods across four LLMs and three schedules with evolving constraints, we find that these methods show no significant improvement in performance, even after incurring a higher latency. These methods, designed for offline or reactive settings, are inadequate for the proactive paradigm. Our work emphasizes the growing need for designing proactive prompt adaptation methods, where the models must remain robust to evolving needs in deployment.

</details>


### [216] [ShallowBench: Benchmarking Generative Drug Design Models on Shallow-Pocket Targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06717)
*Saket Reddy,Shiwei Liu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper builds ShallowBench, a curated benchmark of 5,780 shallow-pocket protein targets, and shows that current generative drug design models struggle on them, underscoring the need for methods specifically designed for low-pocketability binding sites.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing generative AI methods for structure-based drug design work well on deep binding pockets but perform poorly on low-pocketability targets, including historically undruggable oncology proteins such as KRAS and MYC. A dedicated benchmark is needed to systematically assess and drive progress on these harder shallow-pocket cases.

Method: The paper constructs ShallowBench by extracting 5,780 shallow-pocket targets from CrossDocked2020 and quantifying pocket shallowness using the difference between an Alpha Shape-derived 'lid' volume and the underlying protein atom voxel volume, while retaining targets with enough surface area for ligand binding. It then evaluates several state-of-the-art generative models on this benchmark.

Result: The benchmark successfully identifies shallow, low-concavity yet bindable targets, and experiments show that multiple state-of-the-art generative models achieve weaker predicted binding affinity on these interfaces compared with easier targets.

Conclusion: ShallowBench exposes a clear weakness of current structure-based generative drug design models on shallow, low-concavity protein interfaces and establishes the need for new model architectures or training objectives better suited to these difficult targets.

Abstract: While generative AI models have demonstrated remarkable success in structure-based drug design, they predominantly rely on deep binding pockets and struggle to sample effective ligands for challenging low-pocketability targets, such as the historically "undruggable" oncology targets KRAS and MYC. To address this gap, we introduce ShallowBench, a strictly curated benchmark of 5,780 shallow-pocket targets extracted from CrossDocked2020. By computing the difference between an Alpha Shape "lid" volume and the underlying protein atom voxel volume, we successfully isolated targets with low concavity while ensuring sufficient surface area for binding. Evaluating various state-of-the-art generative models reveals weaker predicted binding affinity on these low-concavity interfaces. ShallowBench therefore provides a rigorous benchmark for generative biology models and highlights the necessity of new architectural innovations or loss functions capable of navigating these challenging targets.

</details>


### [217] [MSAIC-Net: A Multi-Scale Attention and Imbalance-Aware Contrastive Network for ECG-Based Myocardial Substrate Abnormality Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06718)
*Canyu Lei,Fenglin Zhang,Derek Bivona,Cristiane Singulane,Jonathan Pan,Kenneth Bilchick,Amit R. Patel,Jianxin Xie*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: MSAIC-Net 通过多尺度空洞卷积、通道注意力、面向不平衡数据的监督对比学习和导联重要性分析，提高了 ECG 检测心肌瘢痕/心肌梗死的准确性与可解释性。 / MSAIC-Net improves ECG-based detection of myocardial scar/MI using multi-scale atrous convolutions, channel attention, imbalance-aware supervised contrastive learning, and lead-importance analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 心肌瘢痕和心肌梗死等心肌基质异常与不良心血管结局相关，虽然 ECG 低成本且易获取，但其检测受导联表现异质性、多导联高维信号、类别不平衡以及深度模型可解释性不足等问题限制，因此需要一种更有效且可解释的方法。 / Myocardial substrate abnormalities such as scar and MI are clinically important, and although ECG is low-cost and widely available, detection is difficult due to heterogeneous lead-dependent manifestations, high-dimensional multi-lead signals, class imbalance, and limited interpretability of deep models, motivating a more effective and interpretable solution.

Method: 该方法构建了一个多尺度注意力增强卷积网络（MSAIC-Net），使用并行空洞卷积分支提取不同时间感受野的 ECG 特征，再通过通道注意力自适应重加权导联与特征通道；同时引入面向类别不平衡的监督对比学习以增强类内紧凑性和类间可分性，并结合导联置换重要性来提升可解释性。 / The method builds a multi-scale attention-enhanced convolutional network (MSAIC-Net) with parallel atrous convolution branches for multi-receptive-field ECG feature extraction, followed by channel attention to reweight informative leads and feature channels; it also introduces an imbalance-aware supervised contrastive learning strategy and lead-wise permutation importance for better separability and interpretability.

Result: 在 UVA 心肌瘢痕分类数据集和 PTB-XL 心肌梗死识别数据集上的实验表明，该模型均优于基线模型，其中在低样本 UVA 队列上的优势尤为显著。 / Experiments on the UVA scar classification cohort and the PTB-XL MI identification dataset show that the model outperforms baseline approaches, with particularly pronounced improvements on the low-sample UVA cohort.

Conclusion: 提出的 MSAIC-Net 能够有效且可解释地基于 ECG 检测心肌基质异常，并在两个数据集上优于基线方法，尤其在低数据量的 UVA 队列中提升更明显。 / The proposed MSAIC-Net provides an effective and interpretable ECG-based approach for detecting myocardial substrate abnormalities, outperforming baseline models on two datasets, with especially strong gains in the low-data UVA cohort.

Abstract: Myocardial substrate abnormalities, such as myocardial scar and myocardial infarction (MI), are associated with adverse cardiovascular outcomes. Electrocardiography (ECG) provides a low-cost and widely available tool for detecting these abnormalities, but ECG-based detection remains challenging due to heterogeneous lead-dependent manifestations, high-dimensional multi-lead signals, class imbalance, and the limited interpretability of deep learning models. We propose a multi-scale attention-enhanced convolutional network (MSAIC-Net) for ECG-based myocardial substrate abnormality detection. MSAIC-Net employs parallel atrous convolutional branches to extract ECG features across multiple temporal receptive fields. %, enabling the model to capture both local and longer-range temporal patterns. Channel attention is then used to adaptively reweight informative lead-wise and feature-channel representations. To address class imbalance and improve feature separability, we introduce a novel imbalance-aware supervised contrastive learning strategy that encourages samples from the same class to form compact representations while increasing separation between abnormal and normal samples. Lead-wise permutation importance is further incorporated to quantify the contribution of each ECG lead and improve model interpretability. The proposed method was evaluated on two complementary datasets: a low-data institutional cohort from the University of Virginia (UVA) Health System for myocardial scar classification and the large-scale public PTB-XL dataset from PhysioNet for MI identification. Experimental results show that MSAIC-Net outperforms baseline models, with particularly pronounced improvements in the low-data UVA cohort. Overall, the proposed framework provides an effective and interpretable approach for ECG-based detection of myocardial substrate abnormalities.

</details>


### [218] [Flatland: The Adventures of Gradient Descent with Large Step Sizes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06722)
*Leonardo Galli,Curtis Fox,Wiebke Bartolomaeus,Mark Schmidt,Holger Rauhut*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This paper studies how to safely use very large learning rates when training neural networks, even when the loss is not globally smooth. It proposes adaptive first-order methods that provably run at the edge of stability, maintain large step sizes throughout training, and can even reduce sharpness to its global minimum. A key finding is that training does not benefit from becoming too flat too early; allowing optimization to pass through slightly sharper regions can lead to faster convergence and better generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that standard smooth optimization theory does not apply well to neural network training because the objective is often not globally L-smooth. As a result, the longstanding question of the maximum step size that still guarantees gradient descent convergence remains theoretically and practically unresolved in deep learning.

Method: The authors introduce a unifying notion of large step sizes under local Lipschitz or Hölder continuity of the gradient, and design first-order adaptive optimization methods that provably choose such step sizes. Their analysis relies on edge-of-stability dynamics, sharpness tracking via the top Hessian eigenvalue, and a self-stabilization argument that permits optimization in slightly sharper valleys.

Result: The paper shows that the proposed adaptive methods provably achieve large step sizes from the start of training, with nonmonotonic loss decrease and step size times sharpness staying above the classical edge-of-stability threshold of 2. The method can also drive sharpness to its global minimum, and the experiments/analysis suggest that avoiding overly early entry into globally flat regions can improve both convergence and generalization.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that for neural network objectives without global L-smoothness, it is still possible to define and attain provably 'large' gradient descent step sizes using only local regularity assumptions. These large steps naturally operate at the edge of stability throughout training and can improve optimization outcomes, while entering globally flat regions too early may harm both convergence speed and generalization.

Abstract: The training of neural networks often entails objective functions that are not globally $L$-smooth. For these functions, it is both theoretically and practically difficult to reply to the question: what is the largest possible step size that ensures the convergence of gradient descent (GD)? We address this longstanding open question in deep learning by providing a unifying definition of "large" step sizes that requires only local Lipschitz (or even Hölder) continuity of the gradient. We design first-order adaptive methods that provably yield large step sizes and show that they operate at the edge of stability (EoS) right from the start of the training. In particular, the loss decreases nonmonotonically and the product between the step size and sharpness, i.e., the largest eigenvalue of the Hessian, stays above the EoS threshold of 2 throughout training. Using our method, we are also able to minimize the sharpness all the way down to its global minimum. Contrary to expectation, we find that encountering globally-flat regions too early in the training may both slow down convergence and jeopardize the generalization ability of the network. Exploiting a self-stabilization argument, we allow GD to enter slightly sharper valleys and turn unsuccessful training runs into very successful ones.

</details>


### [219] [Synthics: Synthetic Physics-like Datasets for Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06724)
*Jari Vepsäläinen*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A Bayesian grammar-based pipeline generates synthetic physics-like regression datasets whose equations and input domains resemble real ones, and the resulting synthetic data is statistically faithful and useful for downstream hyperparameter tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that machine learning often suffers from insufficient representative real-world data, while collecting more data is expensive or infeasible. Synthetic data is a practical alternative only if it faithfully preserves the structural and domain properties of real observations, especially for physics-inspired regression problems.

Method: The method learns a Bayesian Probabilistic Context-Free Grammar from an equation corpus to model algebraic structure, samples novel equations from this grammar, estimates each equation’s applicability domain and inter-variable constraints through non-intrusive probing, and then samples valid inputs using random sub-ranges with mixed uniform and truncated normal distributions.

Result: Compared with an unsmoothed purely probabilistic grammar, the proposed approach matches the Feynman corpus on all 8 evaluated structural features instead of only 2. In downstream hyperparameter tuning, models tuned on the synthetic data achieve an average ranking of 6th-best out of 20 on real data, matching tuning on real data and outperforming random expression trees and noise baselines.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a Bayesian Probabilistic Context-Free Grammar combined with applicability-domain-aware input sampling can generate synthetic regression datasets that preserve the structural characteristics of physics equations and remain useful for downstream model-selection tasks. The Bayesian prior is critical for matching real corpus structure under limited data.

Abstract: Representative data is fundamental in machine learning, as limited data hinders generalisation. Collecting sufficient real-world samples is often infeasible. Synthetic data generation offers a practical solution, but only if the generated data faithfully reflects the structure of real observations. In this paper, a method for generating synthetic regression datasets that structurally resemble physics equations from a given equation corpus is presented. The approach uses a Bayesian Probabilistic Context-Free Grammar to capture the underlying algebraic structure of the corpus, from which novel equations are sampled. To ensure the generated inputs lie within a physically meaningful domain, the applicability domain is characterised for each equation through non-intrusive probing, also recovering inter-variable constraints. Input sampling further mimics realistic experimental conditions by drawing from random sub-ranges of the valid domain with mixed uniform and truncated normal distributions. The generated data is statistically validated against the Feynman equation corpus using Kolmogorov-Smirnov tests. The generated equations match the corpus on all of the eight studied structural features, compared to only two for an unsmoothed purely probabilistic grammar, demonstrating that the Bayesian prior is essential for structural fidelity given the size of the corpus. In a downstream hyperparameter-tuning task, a gradient-boosted regressor tuned on the synthetic data picks, on average, the 6th-best configuration out of 20 on real data, matching the result of tuning on real data itself and substantially outperforming random expression trees (10th) and noise (19th).

</details>


### [220] [TorchKM: A GPU-Oriented Library for Kernel Learning and Model Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06742)
*Yikai Zhang,Gaoxiang Jia,Jie Ding,Boxiang Wang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TorchKM is a GPU-accelerated, scikit-learn-style library for kernel methods that makes SVMs and related models much faster to train and tune while maintaining competitive prediction performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kernel methods are powerful but are often limited in practice by slow training and model selection, especially compared with modern GPU-accelerated workflows. The work is motivated by the need for an easy-to-use, efficient library that brings kernel machines closer to practical large-scale use.

Method: The paper presents TorchKM, an open-source library for kernel machines with a scikit-learn-style API. It supports support vector machines, kernel logistic regression, and kernel quantile regression, and is implemented to leverage GPU-friendly linear algebra and intelligent reuse of matrix computations across the training pipeline.

Result: Benchmark results show that TorchKM achieves competitive predictive accuracy and delivers substantial speedups over standard baselines. The library is open-source, documented, and installable via PyPI, improving accessibility and reproducibility.

Conclusion: TorchKM demonstrates that GPU-accelerated kernel-machine training can achieve strong predictive performance while substantially reducing end-to-end training and model-selection time through efficient matrix-operation reuse.
