Abstract: TorchKM is an open-source library for kernel machines, including support vector machines, kernel logistic regression, and kernel quantile regression, with GPU acceleration. The library features a scikit-learn-style API and is designed to exploit GPU-friendly linear algebra, accelerating the full training and model-selection pipeline through intelligent reuse of matrix operations. Benchmarks show competitive predictive performance together with substantial speedups over standard baselines. Code and documentation are available at https://github.com/YikaiZhang95/torchkm, and the package can be easily installed via PyPI.

</details>


### [221] [Learn to Match: Two-Sided Matching with Temporally Extended Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06744)
*Haijing Zong,Yancheng Liang,Boyang Zhou,Natasha Jaques*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper introduces Learn2Match, a dynamic matching-market benchmark based on a partially observable Markov game. It shows MARL (independent PPO) beats a bandit baseline on welfare and regret, but still explores inefficiently, leaving room for hybrid methods that combine RL adaptability, bandit discipline, and matching structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard matching models usually assume immediate feedback on fixed preferences, which fails to capture realistic matching markets where information is gradually revealed through interviews, repeated interactions, learning, and separation, and where this evolving information affects future matching decisions.

Method: The paper models dynamic two-sided matching as a partially observable Markov game with costly pre-match screening, noisy post-match observations, evolving latent profiles, and endogenous continuation or dissolution. It instantiates this framework in Learn2Match and evaluates decentralized policies such as independent PPO against the CA-ETC baseline using regret, social welfare, and information-friction loss.

Result: Independent PPO achieves higher cumulative social welfare and lower cumulative regret than the bandit-style CA-ETC baseline under temporally extended feedback. However, PPO has higher information-friction loss, showing that better reward optimization does not necessarily mean better coordinated information acquisition.

Conclusion: Learn2Match provides a benchmark for dynamic two-sided matching under temporally extended feedback, and experiments show independent PPO can outperform a bandit-style baseline in cumulative social welfare and regret, but still suffers higher information-friction loss, indicating current MARL lacks coordinated exploration efficiency.

Abstract: Two-sided matching markets often involve information that unfolds over time through interviews, repeated interaction, learning, and separation. Existing matching models typically reduce this process to immediate sub-Gaussian feedback about fixed preferences, missing settings where payoff-relevant information is revealed gradually and changes future matching decisions. We introduce a framework with temporally extended feedback, that formulates two-sided matching as a partially observable Markov game with costly pre-match screening, noisy post-match observations, evolving latent profiles, and endogenous continuation or dissolution. We instantiate this framework in Learn2Match, a multi-agent reinforcement-learning benchmark for dynamic matching markets. Learn2Match supports decentralized decision making over whom to interview, whom to match with, and when to dissolve a match, while evaluating policies using regret, social welfare, and an information-friction loss that measures the welfare gap caused by incomplete revelation of latent preferences. We find that independent PPO achieves higher cumulative social welfare and lower cumulative regret than the bandit-style CA-ETC baseline under temporally extended feedback, demonstrating the promise of MARL for dynamic matching markets. However, PPO still incurs higher information-friction loss, revealing that end-to-end MARL does not yet provide the coordinated exploration structure of matching-bandit methods. These results position Learn2Match as a benchmark for developing the next generation of matching-market algorithms: methods that are adaptive like RL agents, statistically disciplined like bandit algorithms, and structurally aware like stable-matching mechanisms.

</details>


### [222] [Performance Variation in Deep Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06746)
*Haruto Tanaka,A. Rupam Mahmood*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 这篇论文提出一种用分位数而非均值不确定性来衡量深度强化学习跨运行稳定性的方法。核心工具是 min-max IPR 和配套可视化，它们更直观、更不容易低估性能波动；案例显示不同算法和归一化策略在稳定性上的差异可被更清楚地识别。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是深度强化学习常常存在明显的 run-to-run robustness 问题，即相同配置下不同随机种子运行结果差异很大；而现有研究通常只报告平均性能及其不确定性，不能充分反映实际的性能波动与稳定性，因此需要更贴合问题本质的评估工具。

Method: 方法上，论文首先分析常见方差/置信区间等估计在目标错配和低报风险上的局限，然后提出基于样本分位数的统计指标 min-max IPR，以及对应的按运行分位数高亮的可视化方式，用于更完整地展示独立重复实验之间的性能分布。随后在 PPO、SAC、TD-MPC、TD-MPC2、DQN 和 Rainbow 等算法上进行案例研究。

Result: 实验结果表明：1) 在 PPO 中，LayerNorm 和 penultimate-layer normalization 能缩小性能波动，但在 SAC 中效果有限；2) 在 PPO、SAC、TD-MPC、TD-MPC2 比较中，TD-MPC 兼具最低波动和最高数据效率；3) 在五个 Atari 环境中，DQN 与 Rainbow 的性能波动水平相近。

Conclusion: 本文认为，传统以均值不确定性为主的评估方式不足以刻画深度强化学习中跨运行的鲁棒性差异；作者提出基于分位数的 min-max IPR 统计量及 run-wise percentile highlighting 可视化，更适合评估 run-to-run variation，并通过多个案例证明其解释性与实用性。

Abstract: Deep reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms often suffer from low run-to-run robustness, manifesting as significant performance variation across independent runs of identically configured agents. Although this issue poses a spectrum of challenges across research and practice, relatively few studies develop methods to evaluate it; RL research instead often reports uncertainty in the estimated mean performance. In this paper, we outline the limitations of conventional uncertainty and variation estimates, particularly their misalignment with purpose and the risk of underreporting. We then propose an alternative percentile-based statistic and visualization method, min-max IPR and run-wise percentile highlighting, respectively. These percentile-based tools are easy to interpret and rely on standard properties of sample percentiles, providing rich information about run-to-run performance variation. We demonstrate this through three case studies. First, we show that LayerNorm and penultimate-layer normalizations narrow performance variation in PPO, whereas the variation is mostly unchanged in SAC. Second, we compare PPO, SAC, TD-MPC, and TD-MPC2, and show TD-MPC exhibits the least variation while being the most data efficient among the four. Finally, in a comparison of DQN and Rainbow on five Atari environments, we show that both algorithms exhibit similar levels of performance variation.

</details>


### [223] [A Rolling-Window Framework for Churn Prediction and Behavioral Driver Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06776)
*Muhammad Jawad Mufti,Omar Hammad,Haitham Saleh,Muqaddas Gull*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 论文提出了一个基于滚动30天观察窗和30天未来评估窗的客户流失预测框架，在非契约服务中实现持续、实例级风险预测；结果显示，合理的时间建模比更复杂的模型更重要，并且在未来数据上依然稳健。 / The paper presents a rolling 30-day observation plus 30-day future evaluation framework for churn prediction in non-contractual services, enabling continuous instance-level risk scoring; results show that proper temporal framing matters more than model complexity and remains robust on future data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有流失预测方法通常依赖简化的时间假设或单点行为表示，难以支持持续风险监测、可解释性以及真实部署；尤其在非契约场景中，流失并不被显式记录，只能从行为停滞中推断，因此需要更符合业务时序的建模方法。 / Existing churn prediction methods often rely on simplified temporal assumptions or single-point behavior representations, limiting continuous risk assessment, interpretability, and realistic deployment; this is especially problematic in non-contractual settings where churn is latent and must be inferred from inactivity.

Method: 作者提出一个时序显式的客户流失预测框架：使用固定30天观察窗口提取客户行为特征，并用后续30天窗口判定是否流失，从而保证行为证据与流失结果在时间上清晰分离；在统一的时间设计下同时比较特征工程模型与序列模型，并在大规模真实数据及未来未见数据上评估。 / The study proposes a temporally explicit churn prediction framework using a fixed 30-day observation window to summarize customer behavior and a following 30-day evaluation window to label churn, ensuring temporal separation between evidence and outcome; within this design, both feature-based and sequence-based models are evaluated on large-scale real-world and future unseen data.

Result: 在真实大规模数据上，特征模型达到87.6%的准确率和0.94的ROC-AUC；序列模型通过捕捉时间上的脱离模式，召回率最高达到96.1%。在未来未见数据且不重新训练的情况下，准确率仍高于83%，ROC-AUC超过0.91，显示出较强的时间迁移鲁棒性。 / On a large real-world dataset, the feature-based model achieves 87.6% accuracy and 0.94 ROC-AUC, while the sequence-based model reaches recall up to 96.1% by capturing temporal disengagement patterns. On future unseen data without retraining, accuracy remains above 83% and ROC-AUC above 0.91, indicating meaningful robustness under temporal shift.

Conclusion: 该研究表明，在非契约、按次付费服务场景中，精心设计的时间框架比单纯增加模型复杂度更关键；基于滚动行为窗口的时序显式流失预测框架能够实现稳健、可解释且可部署的持续流失风险评估。 / The paper concludes that in non-contractual, pay-per-use services, a carefully designed temporal framing matters more than model complexity alone; a temporally explicit rolling-window framework enables robust, interpretable, and deployment-ready continuous churn risk assessment.

Abstract: Customer churn prediction is a central task in customer analytics, particularly in non-contractual, pay-per-use service environments where disengagement is not explicitly observed and must be inferred from behavioral inactivity. Existing churn prediction approaches often rely on simplified temporal assumptions or single-point representations of customer behavior, which limit their ability to support continuous risk assessment, interpretability, and realistic deployment over time. This study proposes a temporally explicit churn prediction framework that models customer behavior using rolling behavioral windows, enabling repeated and instance-level churn risk estimation as customer activity evolves. Customer behavior is summarized within a fixed 30-day observation window, followed by a 30-day future churn evaluation window, ensuring a clear temporal separation between behavioral evidence and churn outcomes. The framework integrates feature-based and sequence-based learning approaches within a unified temporal design. The proposed approach is evaluated on a large-scale, real-world dataset from a non-contractual service platform. Empirical results demonstrate strong and stable predictive performance, with accuracy reaching 87.6% and ROC-AUC of 0.94 for the feature-based model, while the sequence-based model achieves recall as high as 96.1% by capturing temporal disengagement patterns. Evaluation on future unseen data confirms meaningful robustness under temporal shift, with accuracy remaining above 83% and ROC-AUC exceeding 0.91 without model retraining. Overall, the findings highlight that carefully designed temporal framing, rather than model complexity alone, is critical for achieving robust, interpretable, and deployment-ready churn prediction. The study provides a practical foundation for churn-oriented decision support in dynamic service environments.

</details>


### [224] [Federated Foundation Models over Vehicular Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06786)
*Kasra Borazjani,Fardis Nadimi,Payam Abdisarabshali,Owen Palinski,Allan Salihovic,Dinh Nguyen,Minghui Liwang,Seyyedali Hosseinalipour*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A forward-looking paper arguing that federated multi-modal multi-task foundation models could become a key architecture for vehicular intelligence, explaining how they work, where they can be used, what blocks deployment, and why early evidence on Waymo data looks promising.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vehicular networks need intelligent models that can process multiple modalities and tasks while preserving privacy and supporting distributed data ownership. Existing research has not sufficiently explored how to combine foundation models with federated learning in this setting, so the paper aims to frame and motivate this emerging direction.

Method: The paper is primarily a vision and analysis paper. It introduces the basic training and fine-tuning principles of multi-modal multi-task federated foundation models, surveys representative vehicular-network use cases, analyzes deployment constraints in vehicular environments, proposes future research directions, and includes a case study on the Waymo Open Dataset with released implementation.

Result: The paper shows, through conceptual analysis and a Waymo Open Dataset case study, that M3T FedFMs have strong potential for next-generation vehicular intelligence. It also provides a public repository to support reproducibility and further research.

Conclusion: M3T FedFMs are a promising framework for vehicular networks because they combine multi-modal, multi-task foundation model capability with federated learning's privacy-preserving distributed training, but practical deployment still requires solving vehicular-specific constraints.

Abstract: This paper presents a forward-looking vision for integrating the emerging multi-modal multi-task federated foundation models (M3T FedFMs) into vehicular networks, with the goal of unifying the expressive power of multi-modal multi-task foundation models (M3T FMs) with the privacy-preserving and distributed learning capabilities of federated learning (FL). Given the largely underexplored nature of this research direction, we first introduce the fundamental training/fine-tuning principles of M3T FedFMs. We then discuss a range of their representative use cases in vehicular networks, illustrating the significant potential of M3T FedFMs to enable next-generation vehicular intelligence. Afterwards, we identify key constraints inherent to vehicular environments that challenge the practical deployment of M3T FedFMs, and articulate a set of forward-looking research directions to address these challenges. Furthermore, through a case study conducted on a real-world vehicular dataset (i.e., Waymo Open Dataset), we demonstrate the promise of M3T FedFMs for vehicular networks and release our implementation to facilitate reproducibility and stimulate research in this emerging area (repository: https://github.com/KasraBorazjani/vehicular-fedfm)

</details>


### [225] [Interpreting Learning Under Competing Models: Joint and Stepwise Approaches for Dynamic Cognitive Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06804)
*Yawen Ma,Sahoko Ishida,Kate Cain,Gabriel Wallin*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 这篇论文比较了‘联合估计Q矩阵+学习过程’与‘先估Q矩阵再分析学习’两种做法，发现它们会改变对学生阅读发展，尤其是部分熟练学生比例的判断；当题目对应技能不确定且题库变化时，联合分析更可信。 This paper compares joint estimation of the Q-matrix with the learning process against a two-step approach, showing that the choice can change conclusions about reading development, especially the share of partially proficient students; joint analysis is more trustworthy when item-skill links are uncertain and the item pool changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 数字学习环境记录到题目级反应，使研究具体技能发展成为可能；但若每道题对应的技能未知，分析者必须决定是与学习过程联合估计Q矩阵，还是先确定Q矩阵再研究学习，这一方法选择可能改变实质性结论。 Digital learning environments provide item-level responses that enable analysis of specific skill development; however, when the skills measured by items are unknown, analysts must choose between joint estimation of the Q-matrix and learning-after-Q estimation, and this choice may alter substantive conclusions.

Method: 采用动态认知诊断模型分析二至三年级两款阅读游戏的数据，并利用题目文本嵌入为未知Q矩阵提供先验信息；比较联合分析与偏差校正的分步分析，并通过模拟研究考察二者何时出现差异。 The paper uses dynamic cognitive diagnostic models on data from two reading games from Grade 2 to Grade 3, with item-text embeddings as prior information for the unknown Q-matrix; it compares a joint analysis with a bias-corrected stepwise analysis and uses simulations to study when they diverge.

Result: 在真实数据中，联合分析与偏差校正分步分析都表明大多数学习者趋向掌握词汇和理解两项技能，但对三年级时仍部分熟练的学习者比例给出不同判断；模拟表明在Q矩阵不确定且题库随年级变化时，这种差异更明显，且联合分析更稳健。 In real data, both the joint and bias-corrected stepwise analyses suggest most learners move toward mastering both vocabulary and comprehension, but they disagree on how many remain only partially proficient in Grade 3; simulations show this divergence becomes more pronounced when the Q-matrix is uncertain and the item pool changes by grade, with joint analysis being more robust.

Conclusion: 联合估计Q矩阵与学习过程会影响对学习者发展结论的判断；当项目-技能结构不确定且跨年级题库变化时，联合分析更可靠。 Jointly estimating the Q-matrix and learning process can materially change conclusions about learner development; joint analysis is more reliable when the item-skill structure is uncertain and the item pool changes across grades.

Abstract: Digital learning environments record learners' responses to individual items, making it possible to study the development of specific skills rather than overall scores. Drawing conclusions about learning from these data requires a model that links responses to latent skills and tracks how mastery changes over time. When the skills measured by each item are unknown, the analyst must decide whether to estimate this structure, the Q-matrix, jointly with the learning process, or to establish it first and study learning afterwards. We show that this decision can change substantive conclusions about how learners develop. Using dynamic cognitive diagnostic models, we analyse data from two reading games measuring vocabulary and comprehension from Grade 2 to Grade 3, with item-text embeddings providing prior information for the unknown Q-matrix. A joint analysis and a bias-corrected stepwise analysis agree that most learners move toward mastering both skills, but disagree about how many remain only partially proficient at Grade 3, changing how reading progress would be reported. A simulation study identifies when the two analyses diverge and shows that joint analysis is more reliable when the item-skill structure is uncertain and the item pool changes between grades. We provide R code for both analyses.

</details>


### [226] [SCALE: Scalable Cross-Attention Learning with Extrapolation for Agentic Workflow Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06820)
*Zhifei Xu,Jierui Lan,Zixuan Liang,Aiji Liang,Jinxi He*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: SCALE is a scale-generalizing DRL scheduler for LLM workflow DAGs. Its cross-attention design supports arbitrary server counts, and its SRR regularization stabilizes representations so the model trained on 16 nodes works well on larger clusters, cutting response time by 8.9% at 48 nodes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DRL schedulers for heterogeneous clusters are typically bound to a fixed cluster size and must be retrained when the number of servers changes. This is impractical for agentic LLM systems whose workflow DAG tasks need efficient scheduling across clusters of varying scale.

Method: The paper proposes SCALE, a DRL scheduler based on a cross-attention pointer network in which task features attend to server features, enabling variable server counts by design. It adds Structured Representation Regularization (SRR), combining a decorrelation loss and a KL penalty toward a standard normal distribution to stabilize attention-feature statistics across scales.

Result: When trained on 16 nodes and evaluated without fine-tuning on 32 and 48 nodes, SCALE outperforms the same cross-attention architecture without SRR, reducing average response time by 8.9% at 48 nodes. The results show that architectural flexibility alone is insufficient and that SRR helps close the scale-generalization gap.

Conclusion: SCALE demonstrates that cluster-size generalization for LLM workflow scheduling requires not only permutation-invariant architecture but also explicit representation regularization; with SRR, the scheduler transfers from 16-node training to larger unseen clusters and improves response time.

Abstract: Agentic Large Language Model (LLM) systems decompose complex tasks into workflow Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs) whose primitives must be scheduled on heterogeneous clusters. Existing deep reinforcement learning (DRL) schedulers are tied to a fixed cluster size and require retraining whenever the number of servers changes. We propose SCALE (Scalable Cross-Attention Learning with Extrapolation), a DRL scheduler that generalizes to unseen cluster scales without fine-tuning. SCALE employs a cross-attention pointer network where task features query against server features, so the architecture accepts any number of servers by construction. We observe, however, that permutation-invariant architecture alone does not guarantee good performance at new scales - the attention feature undergoes distribution shift as the server count grows. To counter this, we introduce Structured Representation Regularization (SRR): a decorrelation loss combined with a KL penalty toward the standard normal, which keeps feature statistics stable regardless of input size. Trained on 16 nodes and tested directly on 32 and 48 nodes, SCALE reduces average response time by 8.9% at N=48 relative to the same architecture without SRR, confirming that explicit regularization is necessary to close the scale-generalization gap.

</details>


### [227] [PandaAI: A Practical Agent CQ2 for Neuro-symbolic Data Analysis And Integrated Decision-Making in Quantitative Finance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06823)
*Yuqi Li,Siyuan Liu,Bingjun Liu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: PandaAI is a domain-adapted, closed-loop neuro-symbolic LLM agent for finance that adds regime modeling, constrained alpha generation, and risk-aware reasoning; it beats strong time-series baselines on CSI 300 with higher Rank IC and lower drawdown.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Financial sequential decision-making is difficult for conventional deep learning because financial data are noisy, low-SNR, and non-stationary. The work is motivated by the need to combine LLM reasoning ability with domain-specific financial rigor, risk control, and robustness in high-stakes settings.

Method: The paper fine-tunes a domain-specific LLM, embeds it into a modular closed-loop neuro-symbolic agent architecture, incorporates market regime modeling, constrained alpha generation, and dual-channel adaptation, and evaluates the system on CSI 300 stock data against state-of-the-art time-series models.

Result: On CSI 300 stock data, PandaAI achieves 18.2% higher Rank IC and 25.7% lower maximum drawdown than state-of-the-art time-series models, suggesting better predictive ranking quality and improved risk control.

Conclusion: PandaAI demonstrates that a closed-loop neuro-symbolic LLM agent with market regime modeling, constrained alpha generation, and explicit risk awareness can outperform strong time-series baselines in financial sequential decision-making, while reducing harmful or unreliable financial outputs.

Abstract: While deep learning has excelled in various domains, its application to sequential decision-making in finance remains challenging due to the low Signal-to-Noise Ratio (SNR) and non-stationarity of financial data. Leveraging the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs), we propose \textbf{PandaAI}, a closed-loop neuro-symbolic LLM agent with market regime modeling and constrained alpha generation, which bridges general LLM reasoning with financial rigor and suppresses the financial toxicity of LLM-generated outputs. To bridge the gap between general linguistic capability and financial rigor, we fine-tune a domain-specific LLM. Furthermore, we integrate this LLM into a modular architecture and form a closed-loop system. Unlike traditional models that optimize isolated prediction metrics, \textbf{PandaAI} is designed as a neuro-symbolic agent that navigates the complex, real-world financial environment with explicit risk awareness. Extensive experiments on CSI 300 stock data show that \textbf{PandaAI} achieves a $18.2\%$ higher Rank IC and $25.7\%$ lower maximum drawdown than state-of-the-art time-series models. Our constrained LLM generation and dual-channel adaptation method provide a general paradigm for LLM deployment in high-stakes sequential decision-making scenarios.

</details>


### [228] [Architecture Shapes Transfer Specificity in Implicit Neural Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06827)
*D Yang Eng*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper shows that big transfer gains in INRs do not necessarily mean meaningful source-aware transfer. ReLU often transfers more selectively, SIREN often transfers more broadly, and Fourier Features can be strong but depend on tuning. Evaluate transfer with explicit controls, not gain alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Warm-start gain is commonly used to measure transfer in coordinate networks, but it is unclear whether this gain truly captures source-specific reusable structure or merely generic benefits from reusing pretrained weights. The paper aims to disentangle transfer magnitude from transfer specificity.

Method: The paper compares transfer behavior across three INR families: SIREN, ReLU MLPs, and Fourier-feature MLPs. It uses controlled analytic tests with independent-seed random controls, a 2D lid-driven-cavity Navier–Stokes benchmark, and 1D PDE reference-solution suites for heat, viscous Burgers, and focusing cubic NLS, along with same-family source controls and auxiliary ablations.

Result: Across tasks, transfer magnitude and specificity are clearly separable. Fourier Features achieve the largest structured transfer in one controlled geometric test, SIREN also shows large gains, and ReLU shows smaller absolute gains but much stronger selectivity against random controls. On other controlled families and PDE benchmarks, no single architecture dominates all equations, but SIREN tends to reuse weights broadly while ReLU and sometimes Fourier Features are more source-selective. Static diagnostics are weak, and the proposed scaling law A_transfer proportional to 1/Δt^2 is rejected in the implemented 1D audit.

Conclusion: Transfer specificity is a more informative diagnostic than warm-start gain alone for coordinate-network transfer. Architecture choice in scientific machine learning should therefore be evaluated under explicit control conditions, because high transfer magnitude can reflect generic weight reuse rather than source-specific structure.

Abstract: Transfer in coordinate networks is often measured by warm-start gain, but whether that gain reflects source-specific structure or generic weight reuse is less clear. We study this question across three implicit neural representation (INR) families, SIREN, ReLU MLPs, and Fourier-feature MLPs, using controlled analytic tests, a 2D lid-driven-cavity Navier--Stokes benchmark, and 1D PDE reference-solution suites for heat, viscous Burgers, and focusing cubic NLS. The analytic tests use independent-seed random controls, while the PDE benchmarks use alternate same-family source controls and auxiliary ablations.
  Across settings, transfer magnitude and transfer specificity separate clearly. In a 10-seed controlled 1D geometric test, Fourier Features show the largest structured transfer ($33.1\times$), followed by SIREN ($23.0\times$) and ReLU ($10.7\times$), but ReLU is far more selective: random-control transfer is $0.41\times$ for ReLU versus $14.24\times$ for SIREN. On a controlled two-parameter 1D family, the ranking changes: ReLU gives the clearest structured-versus-control separation at default settings, whereas Fourier Features improve only after bandwidth retuning. In Navier--Stokes and the broader 1D PDE suite, no single architecture dominates every equation, yet the same pattern remains: SIREN often reuses weights broadly, whereas ReLU and, in some equations, Fourier Features are more source-selective. Static diagnostics remain weak, and the heuristic scaling law $A_{\text{transfer}} \propto 1/Δt^2$ is rejected in the implemented 1D audit.
  These results position transfer specificity as a useful diagnostic for coordinate networks and suggest that architecture selection in scientific machine learning should be evaluated under explicit control conditions, not by transfer magnitude alone.

</details>


### [229] [Hearing the Unspoken: Language Model Priors for Acoustic Adversarial Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06833)
*Jiani Xie,Andrew C. Cullen,Paul Montague,Benjamin I. P. Rubinstein*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: An LLM-assisted real-time attack called Semantic Gambit uses predictive context to bypass causal limits in streaming ASR and triples attack effectiveness, pushing WER to 35.6%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that existing real-time ASR attacks are constrained by causality: attackers must act on incomplete acoustic information. The paper investigates whether external predictive context can break this bottleneck and significantly strengthen attacks.

Method: The method introduces the Semantic Gambit attack, which uses a Large Language Model to provide predictive semantic context in real time, helping an adversary overcome the incomplete-information limitation inherent in causal ASR settings.

Result: Experiments show the attack raises corpus-level Word Error Rate to 35.6%, about three times higher than the previous state of the art for attacking real-time ASR pipelines.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that real-time ASR systems are more vulnerable than previously assumed when attackers are augmented with low-latency LLM-based predictive context, enabling substantial degradation of transcription quality despite causal constraints.

Abstract: Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) systems operating in real-time settings must process acoustic input under strict temporal constraints, where transcription decisions are inherently made on incomplete information. This causal constraint serves as an information bottleneck on attackers, significantly limiting attack performance. Our new Semantic Gambit attack breaks this causal limitation by augmenting the adversary with predictive context derived from a Large Language Model in real-time. Our experiments show that this form of augmentation can elevate the corpus-level Word Error Rate to 35.6% -- a three-fold increase over the current state-of-the-art. Ultimately, this work reveals how common, low-latency LLM tooling can be exploited to systematically subvert real-time ASR pipelines.

</details>


### [230] [The Geometry of Last-Layer Model Stealing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06854)
*Snigdha Chandan Khilar*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Geometric theory for model stealing: exact extraction of a transformer's last layer is possible under precise conditions, but the hidden layers are not fully identifiable from outputs alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to rigorously understand model stealing beyond empirical demonstrations. Instead of only showing that extraction attacks can work in practice, the paper aims to precisely determine which parts of a neural network are theoretically recoverable and which parts remain fundamentally hidden.

Method: The paper uses a geometric analysis of a standard model extraction or model stealing setup. It derives necessary and sufficient conditions for exact recovery of the transformer's final layer, and then extends the analysis to hidden layers to characterize what information is and is not identifiable from model outputs.

Result: The main result is that the final layer of a transformer can be perfectly copied when exact geometric conditions are satisfied. For deeper hidden layers, the paper proves clear identifiability limits and shows that observing only final outputs is insufficient to fully reverse engineer the hidden network.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that the stealability of neural networks has sharp geometric limits: under specific identifiable conditions, the final layer of a transformer can be exactly recovered, but the internal hidden network cannot in general be fully reconstructed from output observations alone.

Abstract: This paper uses geometry to explain how a machine learning model can be stolen using an already existing well-known method. The author has shown the exact conditions required to perfectly copy the final layer of a transformer network. When looking deeper into the hidden layers the author has explained clear limits. The author has also demonstrated that a hidden network cannot be fully reverse engineered just by looking at the final results. The research clearly maps out what can and cannot be stolen from a model.

</details>


### [231] [Modeling Nonlinear Feature Interactions with Product-Unit Residual Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06861)
*Ziyuan Li,Uwe Jaekel,Babette Dellen*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: PURe adds explicit multiplicative interaction modeling to residual networks and outperforms or matches MLPs while being more robust, data-efficient, and interpretable at the interaction level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard MLPs usually capture feature interactions only implicitly, which can entangle representations and hurt robustness and interpretability. The work is motivated by the need for models that represent cross-feature couplings more explicitly while remaining trainable and stable.

Method: The paper studies product-unit residual networks (PURe), combining multiplicative product units with residual connections. It evaluates real-valued and complex-valued PURe variants under matched parameter budgets on one synthetic interaction-focused benchmark and two real-world datasets, measuring predictive accuracy, robustness to Gaussian feature noise, low-data performance, and SHAP-based interaction structure.

Result: Across the synthetic and real datasets, PURe achieves competitive or improved prediction performance relative to MLP baselines, is more robust to Gaussian feature noise, performs better with limited training data, and exhibits more concentrated and structurally coherent SHAP interaction patterns. The comparison also includes real- and complex-valued variants under equal parameter budgets.

Conclusion: PURe explicitly models nonlinear feature interactions using product units plus residual connections, yielding competitive or better accuracy, stronger robustness to Gaussian noise, improved sample efficiency in low-data settings, and clearer interaction-level interpretability than standard MLPs.

Abstract: Understanding nonlinear feature interactions is crucial in science and engineering, yet standard multilayer perceptrons (MLPs) often capture such interactions only implicitly, leading to entangled representations that can impair robustness and interpretability. We investigate product-unit residual networks (PURe) that integrate multiplicative product units with residual connections to explicitly model cross-feature couplings while stabilizing optimization. We conduct a systematic evaluation on an interaction-driven synthetic benchmark and two real-world datasets, assessing predictive accuracy, robustness to Gaussian feature noise, and performance under limited training data, and we compare real- and complex-valued variants under a matched parameter budget. Beyond accuracy, SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP)-based interaction analyses show that PURe learns more concentrated and structurally coherent interaction patterns than MLP baselines. Overall, PURe achieves competitive or improved performance, better robustness and sample efficiency in low-data regimes, and enhanced interaction-level interpretability.

</details>


### [232] [Product units in gated recurrent units improve nuclear-mass prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06866)
*Ziyuan Li,Paulo S. A. Freitas,John W. Clark,Babette Dellen*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TL;DR：作者把GRU扩展到复数域，并加入乘性交互与积单元结构，显著提升了原子核质量预测精度；AM-PU-GRU在AME2016/2020上的插值和外推任务中达到当前最佳结果，成为新的序列建模基准。 / TL;DR: The authors extend GRU into the complex domain and add multiplicative interactions plus product-unit structures, substantially improving nuclear mass prediction accuracy. AM-PU-GRU achieves state-of-the-art interpolation and extrapolation results on AME2016/2020 and becomes a new benchmark for sequence-based modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机是：核质量预测对核素图中未知区域的探索很重要，传统理论模型仍有局限，而机器学习可以作为补充；已有GRU虽能利用长程依赖，但仍有提升空间，因此作者尝试通过更强的乘性表示能力和复值建模进一步提高预测精度。 / The motivation is that accurate nuclear mass prediction is important for exploring poorly known regions of the nuclear chart, and machine learning can complement existing theoretical models. Although GRU-based approaches already exploit long-range dependencies, there is room for improvement, motivating the introduction of stronger multiplicative representations and complex-valued modeling.

Method: 方法上，作者提出AM-PU-GRU：以GRU为基础，在循环单元中引入乘性交互和积单元变换，并在复数域进行计算，以同时建模幅值与相位动态；随后在AME2016和AME2020数据上进行插值和外推评测，并与多种SOTA模型、实值GRU及消融变体比较。 / Methodologically, the authors propose AM-PU-GRU, a GRU-based recurrent architecture augmented with multiplicative interactions and product-unit transformations, operating in the complex domain to jointly model amplitude and phase dynamics. They evaluate it on interpolation and extrapolation tasks using AME2016 and AME2020, comparing against state-of-the-art models, a real-valued GRU baseline, and ablation variants.

Result: 结果显示，AM-PU-GRU在所有评测中表现最佳：插值RMSE为0.227 ± 0.004 MeV，时间外推RMSE为0.179 ± 0.015 MeV；其性能超过其他先进机器学习方法，也优于实值GRU基线和积单元消融版本，并且在WS4和SEMF等不同理论先验下依然稳健。 / The results show that AM-PU-GRU achieves the best performance across evaluations, with an interpolation RMSE of 0.227 ± 0.004 MeV and a temporal extrapolation RMSE of 0.179 ± 0.015 MeV. It surpasses other state-of-the-art machine learning methods, as well as the real-valued GRU baseline and product-unit ablations, while remaining robust under different theoretical priors such as WS4 and SEMF.

Conclusion: 该论文得出结论：复数域的加性-乘性积单元门控循环网络（AM-PU-GRU）在核质量序列预测中建立了新的性能基准，在插值与时间外推任务上都优于现有机器学习模型以及实值GRU基线，并且对不同理论先验保持稳健。 / The paper concludes that the complex-valued additive-multiplicative product-unit gated recurrent unit (AM-PU-GRU) sets a new benchmark for sequence-based nuclear mass prediction, outperforming prior machine learning models and real-valued GRU baselines on both interpolation and temporal extrapolation while remaining robust to different theoretical priors.

Abstract: The prediction of masses of atomic nuclei using machine learning can complement theoretical models and advance the exploration of poorly known domains of the nuclear chart. We propose a machine learning technique based on gated recurrent units (GRU), which have demonstrated competitive performance in nuclear-mass prediction by exploiting long-term dependencies. By integrating multiplicative interactions and product-unit transformations within recurrent units, we report significant improvements in nuclear-mass prediction. Computations are performed in the complex domain to jointly capture amplitude and phase dynamics. For interpolation and temporal-extrapolation tasks based on the atomic mass evaluation (AME2016 and AME2020), the complex additive-multiplicative product-unit gated recurrent unit (AM-PU-GRU) model consistently achieves the lowest prediction errors, with an interpolation RMSE of 0.227 $\pm$ 0.004 MeV and an extrapolation RMSE of 0.179 $\pm$ 0.015 MeV. These results surpass other state-of-the-art machine learning models and also outperform the real-valued GRU baseline and product-unit ablation variants, while remaining robust to different theoretical priors, including WS4 and SEMF. Our findings establish complex-valued product-unit recurrent networks as a new benchmark for sequence-based nuclear-mass prediction.

</details>


### [233] [Evidence-Grounded Ensemble Diagnosis of 802.11 Packet Captures: A Multi-Stage Pipeline with Deterministic Reliability Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06871)
*Jerome Henry,Swadhin Pradhan,Miroslav Popovic*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: PROBE is an evidence-based ensemble pipeline for diagnosing 802.11 packet captures with LLMs. It reduces hallucinations, avoids unreliable self-confidence estimates, removes evaluation circularity, and improves weighted evidence F1 to 0.957, outperforming both experts and naive ensemble methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diagnosing 802.11 packet captures is expert-intensive, slow, inconsistent, and hard to scale. Existing LLM-based approaches are unreliable because they hallucinate protocol events, especially on truncated traces, provide poorly calibrated confidence scores, and are often evaluated with circular bias when the same model helps create the reference answers.

Method: The paper proposes PROBE, a multi-stage pipeline for Wi-Fi packet-capture diagnosis that combines deterministic PCAP-to-text normalization, multi-run and multi-candidate LLM ensembles, optional cross-model second opinions, progressive obfuscation, a verdict-aware evidence framework that also credits absence of failure evidence, and a deterministic composite reliability score based on evidence validity, stability across runs, and cross-model agreement. It also introduces a model-agnostic evaluation method using per-field assertion matching.

Result: On 87 enterprise Wi-Fi captures covering 104 capture-reviewer pairs, single-pass LLM analysis improves weighted evidence F1 from 0.871 to 0.912 but still misses critical frames in 35% of cases. Naive ensemble voting performs worse than the expert baseline at 0.842, misclassifying 50% of confirmed failures as 'no issue' or 'insufficient evidence.' With evidence-grounded reconciliation, PROBE reaches 0.957 weighted evidence F1, a 96% auto-accept rate, and a worst-case floor above 0.70. Self-reported LLM confidence is shown to be uninformative, with 71% of outputs reporting exactly 0.95.

Conclusion: PROBE demonstrates that reliable LLM-assisted 802.11 packet-capture diagnosis requires evidence-grounded reasoning, deterministic preprocessing, ensemble reconciliation, and non-self-reported reliability scoring; these design choices substantially outperform both experts and naive LLM ensembles while reducing hallucination and evaluation bias.

Abstract: Diagnosing 802.11 packet captures requires expert protocol knowledge, is slow, inconsistent across engineers, and unscalable. LLM-based approaches sound plausible but fabricate protocol events absent from captures (especially truncated traces), produce uncalibrated confidence scores, and suffer evaluation bias when golden references are co-produced by the model under test. We introduce PROBE (Protocol Reasoning Over evidence-Based Ensembles), a multi-stage pipeline addressing all three failures. It integrates (i) deterministic PCAP-to-text normalization with frame-level verifiability, (ii) multi-run, multi-candidate ensembles with optional cross-model second opinion and progressive obfuscation, (iii) a verdict-aware evidence framework treating absence of failure evidence as contributing evidence, and (iv) a fully deterministic composite reliability score from evidence validity, run-to-run stability, and cross-model agreement without LLM self-assessment.
  On 87 enterprise Wi-Fi captures (104 capture-reviewer pairs), single-pass LLM analysis raises weighted evidence F1 from 0.871 (expert baseline) to 0.912 but misses critical frames in 35% of cases. Naive ensemble voting drops below baseline (0.842) as majority voting amplifies conservative verdicts: 50% of confirmed failures are misclassified as 'no issue' or 'insufficient evidence.' Adding evidence-grounded reconciliation achieves 0.957 F1, a 96% auto-accept rate, and a worst-case floor above 0.70. LLM self-reported confidence clusters at 0.95 regardless of difficulty (71% report exactly 0.95), confirming it is uninformative. We also introduce a model-agnostic evaluation framework using per-field assertion matching, eliminating circular bias from model-co-produced golden references.

</details>


### [234] [GlucoFM-Bench: Benchmarking Time-Series Foundation Models for Blood Glucose Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06881)
*Baiying Lu,Zhaohui Liang,Ryan Pontius,Shengpu Tang,Temiloluwa Prioleau*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A new benchmark compares foundation models and supervised models for glucose forecasting; TSFMs are strong when data are scarce, but a simple LSTM still wins with enough labeled data, and hard cases remain in T1D and extreme glucose ranges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Blood glucose forecasting is clinically important for proactive diabetes management, but the task is difficult because physiological dynamics vary across diabetes populations. Existing machine learning and deep learning methods have been studied extensively, whereas time-series foundation models have not been thoroughly evaluated for this domain.

Method: The paper benchmarks eight representative forecasting architectures, including pre-trained time-series foundation models, time-series large language models, and supervised deep learning models, on 15 public diabetes-related datasets covering 1,117 individuals. Evaluation is conducted under zero-shot, few-shot, and full-shot settings, with systematic variation in context length and prediction horizon.

Result: Across datasets, Chronos-2 and TimesFM show strong zero-shot and few-shot transfer, with the best zero-shot model reaching within 5% of the best full-shot supervised model. In full-shot settings, a lightweight LSTM outperforms TSFMs by 4-21%. Stratified analysis shows that T1D cohorts and hypo-/hyperglycemic ranges remain challenging.

Conclusion: GlucoFM-Bench establishes a standardized benchmark for blood glucose forecasting and shows that pre-trained TSFMs transfer well in low-data settings, while a lightweight LSTM still performs best when sufficient task-specific data are available.

Abstract: Blood glucose forecasting models are foundational for modern diabetes management systems, as reliable short-term predictions can enable proactive interventions, support automated insulin delivery, and reduce the risk of hypo- and hyperglycemic events. From a modeling perspective, glucose forecasting poses unique challenges due to heterogeneous physiological dynamics across diabetes populations. Traditional machine learning and deep learning models have been extensively evaluated for glucose prediction, yet recent time-series foundation models (TSFMs) remain much less studied in this setting. To bridge this gap, we present GlucoFM-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark evaluating state-of-the-art TSFMs alongside supervised deep learning models for blood glucose forecasting. We assess eight representative architectures, including pre-trained TSFMs, time-series large language models, and task-specific deep learning models, across 15 publicly available diabetes-relevant datasets comprising 1,117 individuals with type 1 diabetes, type 2 diabetes, prediabetes, and no diabetes. Models are evaluated under zero-shot, few-shot, and full-shot protocols, with systematic variation in context length and prediction horizon. Across datasets, pre-trained TSFMs, especially Chronos-2 and TimesFM, show strong zero-shot and few-shot transfer, with the best zero-shot model performing within 5% of the best full-shot supervised model. Yet, when task-specific data are abundant, a lightweight LSTM remains strongest, outperforming TSFMs by 4--21% under full-shot training. Stratified analyses reveal persistent challenges in T1D cohorts and hypo-/hyperglycemic ranges, highlighting the need for evaluation beyond aggregate error metrics. Together, GlucoFM-Bench provides a standardized and reproducible foundation for evaluating, comparing, and improving foundation models for blood glucose forecasting.

</details>


### [235] [Data-Constrained Language Model Pretraining: Improved Regularization and Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06888)
*Zhiwei Xu,Shihao Wu,Hanseul Cho,Wei Hu,Yixin Wang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This paper targets language model pretraining when compute is plentiful but data is limited. It shows that adding masked-input regularization helps autoregressive models, and it introduces SoftQ, a scaling law that better captures the interaction between model size and finite repeated data than standard laws.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical pretraining scaling laws assume abundant data and roughly one pass over the corpus under a fixed compute budget. As available compute grows faster than the supply of natural language data, pretraining is moving into a data-constrained regime where models must train for multiple epochs over limited datasets. Existing laws and training recipes may therefore be misspecified or suboptimal in this setting.

Method: The paper studies data-constrained language model pretraining along two axes: (1) regularization via masked-input regularization (MIR), which adds an auxiliary next-token prediction loss on randomly masked inputs to standard autoregressive training, and (2) scaling via SoftQ, a new scaling law that jointly couples model size and data size to model their interaction under repeated training over finite datasets. Experiments span models from 72M to 1.4B parameters.

Result: Across 72M to 1.4B parameter models, MIR on top of strong weight decay improves validation loss relative to autoregressive baselines with strong weight decay alone, with downstream improvements observed at 1.4B. SoftQ fits data-constrained experiments substantially better than classical additive scaling laws such as Chinchilla and estimates MIR’s gains as equivalent to roughly 1.3x more unique training data.

Conclusion: MIR can improve autoregressive pretraining in data-constrained, compute-rich regimes, and the proposed SoftQ scaling law models repeated-data training better than classical additive laws like Chinchilla. MIR’s benefit is estimated to be comparable to having about 1.3x more unique training data.

Abstract: Classical scaling laws for language model pretraining balance model size against training dataset size under a fixed compute budget, assuming abundant data and a single pass over the corpus. As training compute grows faster than the supply of natural language data, pretraining is likely to enter a data-constrained, compute-rich regime where models train for multiple epochs over a finite dataset. We study data-constrained pretraining along two axes, regularization and scaling. For regularization, we study masked-input regularization (MIR), an auxiliary next-token prediction loss on randomly masked inputs. MIR tests whether the random masking central to diffusion language models can benefit autoregressive pretraining without architectural changes or inference overhead. Across 72M to 1.4B parameter models, we find that MIR added on top of strong weight decay improves validation loss over autoregressive strong-weight-decay-only models, with downstream gains at 1.4B. For scaling, we propose SoftQ, a scaling law that couples model size and data size to capture their interaction under repeated data. Classical alternatives such as the Chinchilla law use an additive form that decouples these terms, making them misspecified in the data-constrained regime. We find that SoftQ fits data-constrained experiments substantially better than these alternatives, and estimates MIR's gains as equivalent to roughly 1.3 times as much unique training data. We release our code at https://github.com/yixinw-lab/dc_pretrain.

</details>


### [236] [GRASP: Geometry-aware Residual Alignment for Scalable Pretraining Data Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06892)
*Yue Min,Ruining Chen,Yujun Li*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GRASP is a scalable data attribution method that scores training data at the subset level instead of assuming examples contribute independently, leading to much better fidelity and lower cost on large-scale pretraining tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing scalable data attribution methods use additive per-example utility scores, which miss important subset dynamics such as redundancy and complementarity. The work is motivated by the need for attribution methods that can capture these interaction effects while remaining efficient at large scale.

Method: The paper reframes data attribution as subset-level counterfactual utility prediction and proposes GRASP, an interaction-aware surrogate that explicitly models subset interactions with a quadratic geometric penalty. For efficiency at pretraining scale, it combines low-dimensional feature sketches with a strictly finite lower-confidence-bound selection protocol.

Result: Extensive subset-retraining experiments show that GRASP significantly outperforms existing scalable baselines, more than doubling task-level rank correlation for counterfactual subset fidelity and reducing upfront artifact construction cost by nearly an order of magnitude. The method also transfers well to language model curation and cross-domain vision selection.

Conclusion: GRASP provides a robust and scalable foundation for optimizing massive pretraining corpora by modeling subset interactions, achieving better subset-level counterfactual fidelity and lower construction cost than prior scalable data attribution methods.

Abstract: Scalable data attribution methods typically assign isolated utility scores to individual training examples. This prevalent additive assumption fundamentally fails to capture critical subset dynamics, including data redundancy and complementary coverage. In this work, we reframe attribution as subset-level counterfactual utility prediction and introduce GRASP, an interaction-aware surrogate. Grounded in a theoretical smoothness lower bound, GRASP explicitly models subset interactions through a quadratic geometric penalty. To achieve pretraining-scale efficiency without relying on hidden oracle tuning, we couple low-dimensional feature sketches with a strictly finite lower-confidence bound selection protocol. Extensive subset-retraining evaluations demonstrate that GRASP decisively outperforms existing scalable baselines. It more than doubles the task-level rank correlation for counterfactual subset fidelity while reducing upfront artifact construction costs by nearly an order of magnitude. Downstream diagnostics further show that this scoring mechanism transfers to language model curation and cross-domain vision selection, establishing a robust foundation for optimizing massive pretraining corpora.

</details>


### [237] [TALAN: Task-Aligned Latent Adaptation Networks for Targeted Post-Training of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06902)
*Chengkai Zhang,Ziteng Liu,Junpu Wang,Zeyi Tao,Yang Wang,Sagar Chordia,Qin Huang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TALAN adds a small input-conditioned latent pathway on top of LoRA/DoRA so the model can make sequence-aware residual adjustments during post-training. It consistently improves STEM/code performance across multiple backbones with very low extra cost, and analysis suggests its gains come from small but complementary activation-level perturbations rather than replacing the main adapter update.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing targeted post-training methods seek to improve reasoning, math, and code while preserving general capabilities, but low-rank adapters are task-global and lack input awareness, while activation interventions are often cumbersome because they require separate probes, vectors, or inference-time steering. The paper aims to combine the efficiency of adapters with the input-aware flexibility of activation interventions in a unified training setup.

Method: The paper introduces TALAN (Task-Aligned Latent Adaptation Networks), a sequence-conditioned latent side path inserted into a transformer's residual stream and co-trained with a low-rank adapter during a single supervised fine-tuning loop. TALAN compresses the input sequence into latent memory, remixes it into token-level perturbations, and writes them back through a controlled residual update. The design is configurable along six axes: insertion location, memory size, mixer, writeback rule, trainability scope, and gradient scale.

Result: Across four Qwen3-family backbones and four STEM/code benchmarks, TALAN outperforms matched baselines. With LoRA, it achieves a +1.41 average gain across models, positive on all four backbones and non-negative on all 16 model-benchmark cells. With DoRA, it achieves a +1.85 average gain, positive on all backbones and on 13 of 16 cells. A Llama-3.2-1B transfer probe is also positive under LoRA and rsLoRA across seven paired seeds. TALAN uses less than 1% trainable parameters relative to the backbone and adds only 1.01-1.02x inference overhead.

Conclusion: TALAN is an effective and practical post-training module that complements LoRA/DoRA by adding small, sequence-conditioned activation perturbations, yielding consistent average gains on reasoning/math/code benchmarks with minimal parameter and inference overhead.

Abstract: Targeted post-training aims to improve reasoning, math, and code without degrading strengths. Low-rank adapters are efficient but task-global; activation interventions are input-aware but often require separate probes, vectors, or inference-time steering. We introduce TALAN (Task-Aligned Latent Adaptation Networks), a sequence-conditioned latent side path inserted into a transformer's residual stream and co-trained with a low-rank adapter in one SFT loop. TALAN compresses the active sequence into latent memory, remixes it into token-level perturbations, and writes them back through a controlled residual update. It is configured along six axes: insertion location, memory size, mixer, writeback rule, trainability scope, and gradient scale.
  Across four Qwen3-family backbones and four STEM/code benchmarks, TALAN improves matched LoRA and DoRA baselines. With LoRA, it yields a +1.41 point cross-model mean gain, positive on all four backbones and non-negative on all 16 model-benchmark cells. With DoRA, it yields a +1.85 point mean gain, positive on all backbones and on 13 of 16 cells. Paired seed checks support positive average effects but show nontrivial variance, so we treat them as sensitivity checks. Cost is small: <1% trainable parameters relative to the backbone and 1.01-1.02x inference overhead versus matched LoRA. A Llama-3.2-1B transfer probe is also positive under LoRA and rsLoRA across seven paired seeds, supporting a transfer beyond Qwen.
  Internal-state analyses suggest TALAN is a small complementary activation intervention. The matched adapter update is 80-1,700x larger than the TALAN perturbation, yet their directions have near-zero cosine; per-layer measurements show this small orthogonal perturbation propagates and amplifies through depth. TALAN offers a practical platform for studying steerable activation-level adaptation within standard adapter-based post-training.

</details>


### [238] [The Fine-Tuning Trap: Evaluating Negative Transfer and the Role of PEFT in Sub-1B Mathematical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06920)
*Rahul Nair,Chun Tao*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Benchmarking five 135M-1B SLMs on math reasoning shows that Full FT is risky for small models, often hurting accuracy, while PEFT methods like LoRA/DoRA are safer and usually better. Use PEFT by default for aligned sub-1B models, and avoid Full FT below 500M.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Edge deployment of small language models requires adaptation methods that are efficient and do not damage the model’s general capabilities. The study is motivated by the need to identify fine-tuning strategies that remain stable and effective for very small models.

Method: The paper benchmarks five small language models ranging from 135M to 1B parameters on mathematical reasoning tasks. It compares Full Fine-Tuning, LoRA, DoRA, zero-shot prompting, and 5-shot in-context learning across tasks such as GSM8K and OrcaMath.

Result: Full Fine-Tuning significantly degrades performance in models under 300M, sometimes falling below zero-shot baselines. LoRA and DoRA perform similarly overall but differ by task: DoRA is better on complex reasoning like GSM8K, while LoRA is stronger on pattern-oriented tasks like OrcaMath. On some aligned or very small models, LoRA or even simple 5-shot ICL outperforms Full FT.

Conclusion: For sub-1B small language models on mathematical reasoning, PEFT is not only more efficient but also more stable than Full Fine-Tuning. Full FT can cause negative transfer and catastrophic forgetting, especially below 300M parameters, so the paper recommends PEFT by default for aligned sub-1B models and cautions against Full FT below 500M.

Abstract: Deploying Small Language Models (SLMs) on edge devices requires efficient fine-tuning strategies that adapt models to new tasks without degrading their general capabilities. In this study, we benchmark five sub-1B models (135M-1B) on mathematical reasoning tasks and uncover a critical vulnerability: Full Fine-Tuning (Full FT) actively harms performance in models under 300M parameters, often dropping accuracy below zero-shot baselines. This "negative transfer" makes Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) not just an efficiency preference, but a stability requirement. We find that while Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) and Weight-Decomposed LoRA (DoRA) perform comparably, their strengths vary by task; DoRA excels in complex reasoning (GSM8K), while LoRA dominates pattern matching (OrcaMath). In particular, Full FT is outperformed by LoRA on aligned models (Qwen2.5-0.5B) and even by simple 5-shot In-Context Learning on the smallest architectures (SmolLM2-135M). Based on these findings, we recommend defaulting to PEFT for all aligned sub-1B models and caution against Full FT for any architecture smaller than 500M parameters to prevent catastrophic forgetting. Reproduction of this work can be found at https://github.com/gulguluu/tiny-slm-finetune-compare.

</details>


### [239] [From Sampled Outcomes to Capability Distributions: Rethinking Supervision for LLM Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06924)
*Guannan Lai,Haoran Hu,Long Chen,Zhenguo Li,Han-Jia Ye*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: DARS improves LLM routing by replacing noisy single-response labels with distribution-aware supervision over input/output variability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LLM routers often use one model response as a capability label, but LLM outputs are stochastic, so single-shot labels are noisy and can systematically misrepresent true query-model capability.

Method: The paper proposes DARS, a distribution-aware routing supervision framework that models uncertainty from both semantically equivalent input reformulations and stochastic output generations to build supervision from model behavior distributions rather than single responses.

Result: Across diverse tasks, DARS produces more stable supervision labels and improves learned routing behavior compared with single-shot supervision, which is shown to be misleading for model selection.

Conclusion: Single-response supervision introduces systematic noise into LLM routing, while distribution-aware supervision yields more reliable routing labels and better routing policies.

Abstract: Existing LLM routing methods typically treat a model's single response to a query as its capability label for training routers. However, because LLM generation is inherently stochastic, such single-shot supervision provides only a noisy observation of a query-model pair's behavior rather than a reliable capability estimate. We show that this assumption introduces systematic noise into routing supervision, making learned routing policies less reliable. To address this issue, we propose DARS (Distribution-Aware Routing Supervision), a framework that constructs routing supervision from a distributional view of model behavior. Instead of relying on a single generated response, DARS considers uncertainty from both the input side and the output side, capturing how semantically equivalent query formulations and stochastic generations affect model performance. Based on these distribution-aware observations, DARS builds more reliable supervision signals for routing. Experiments across diverse tasks show that single-shot labels can be misleading for model selection, while distribution-aware supervision provides more stable labels and improves learned routing behavior. Our results suggest that reliable LLM routing should move beyond single-response observations and be grounded in query-level model capability distributions.

</details>


### [240] [Uniform Stability and Generalization Error of GD and SGD on Fixed-Point Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06934)
*Jonghyun Shin,Sejun Park*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TL;DR：离散参数空间中的舍入不是无害近似，它会从根本上改变优化算法的稳定性与泛化。确定性舍入会让GD显著变差，但SGD在该设置下仍保留可分析、且与维度密切相关的稳定性；随机舍入则可能额外引入维度依赖的泛化损失。 / TL;DR: Rounding in discrete parameter spaces is not a harmless approximation; it fundamentally changes optimization stability and generalization. Deterministic rounding hurts GD badly, but SGD still retains analyzable, dimension-sensitive stability, while stochastic rounding can introduce extra dimension-dependent generalization error.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机是：实际中的低精度/离散化优化常包含参数量化与舍入，但经典基于实值参数空间的稳定性和泛化分析无法直接解释这类算法的行为，因此需要系统刻画舍入对GD/SGD理论性质的影响。 / The motivation is that practical low-precision or discretized optimization often involves quantization and rounding, while classical analyses over real-valued parameter spaces do not adequately explain these settings. The work therefore aims to characterize how rounding changes the theoretical behavior of GD and SGD.

Method: 方法上，论文在离散参数空间下研究GD和SGD，其中每一步更新后进行确定性或随机舍入；围绕generalization error、uniform stability和uniform argument stability建立上界、下界与紧致性结果，并区分一维与高维情形。 / Methodologically, the paper studies GD and SGD over discrete parameter spaces where each update is followed by deterministic or stochastic rounding. It derives upper bounds, lower bounds, and tight characterizations for generalization error, uniform stability, and uniform argument stability, distinguishing between one-dimensional and higher-dimensional regimes.

Result: 主要结果包括：1）对凸、Lipschitz、smooth损失，确定性舍入将GD的泛化误差从O(T/n)恶化为O(T/sqrt(n))，且有匹配下界；2）GD的uniform stability变为Ω(T)，使基于稳定性的泛化界失效；3）在相同损失下，带确定性舍入的SGD仍有非平凡uniform stability：一维为紧的O(T/n)，高维为紧的O(T^2/n)；4）随机舍入会带来随维度增长的泛化误差，这在标准实值优化和确定性舍入中不存在；5）对随机舍入，论文给出uniform argument stability上界，并在损失可写为按坐标分解之和时证明其紧致。 / The main results are: (1) for convex, Lipschitz, and smooth losses, deterministic rounding worsens GD’s generalization error from O(T/n) to O(T/sqrt(n)), with matching lower bounds; (2) GD’s uniform stability becomes Ω(T), making stability-based generalization bounds vacuous; (3) for the same losses, SGD with deterministic rounding still has nontrivial uniform stability, with tight O(T/n) in one dimension and tight O(T^2/n) in higher dimensions; (4) stochastic rounding can create dimension-growing generalization error, a phenomenon absent in standard real-valued optimization and in deterministic rounding; (5) the paper gives upper bounds on uniform argument stability for stochastic rounding and proves tightness for coordinate-wise decomposable losses.

Conclusion: 本文得出结论：在离散参数空间中，舍入机制会显著改变GD/SGD的稳定性与泛化性质。确定性舍入会明显恶化GD的泛化与稳定性，而SGD在确定性舍入下仍可获得非平凡且维度相关的稳定性保证；随机舍入则可能引入随维度增长的额外泛化误差。 / The paper concludes that rounding fundamentally changes the stability and generalization behavior of GD/SGD in discrete parameter spaces. Deterministic rounding substantially worsens GD’s generalization and stability, while SGD with deterministic rounding still admits nontrivial, dimension-dependent stability guarantees; stochastic rounding can additionally induce dimension-dependent generalization error.

Abstract: We analyze generalization error, uniform stability, and uniform argument stability of gradient descent (GD) and stochastic gradient descent (SGD) over discrete parameter spaces, where each update involves deterministic or stochastic rounding. We show that deterministic rounding degrades the generalization error of GD on convex, Lipschitz, and smooth loss functions, increasing the rate from $O(T/n)$ to $O(T/\sqrt{n})$, and establish matching lower bounds. We further prove that uniform stability of GD becomes $Ω(T)$, showing that stability-based generalization bounds are vacuous in this setting. In contrast, for the same losses, stochastic gradient descent with deterministic rounding admits nontrivial uniform stability guarantees, which differ qualitatively from the real-valued case and exhibit distinct dependencies on the number of iterations and the dimension: we prove tight bounds $O(T/n)$ for one dimension and $O(T^2/n)$ for higher dimensions. We also show that stochastic rounding can introduce generalization error that increases with the dimension; such a phenomenon is absent in standard real-valued optimization and in the deterministic rounding case. Finally, we provide upper bounds on uniform argument stability for stochastic rounding schemes and show that these bounds are tight when the loss can be represented as a sum of coordinate-wise functions.

</details>


### [241] [GenPO++: Generative Policy Optimization with Jacobian-free Likelihood Ratios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06967)
*Ke Hu,Shutong Ding,Panxin Tao,Jingya Wang,Ye Shi*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GenPO++ makes flow-based on-policy RL practical by using a reversible ODE policy with history-state memory to compute exact action likelihood ratios cheaply and without extra action dimensions, leading to strong and stable performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing flow-based on-policy RL methods struggle to evaluate the probability of executed actions exactly. Prior approaches either use approximate density-ratio surrogates that bias updates or augment actions with dummy variables to recover exact likelihoods at the cost of larger policy space and higher computation.

Method: The paper proposes GenPO++, a reversible generative policy optimization framework that uses history states as auxiliary memory in a high-order reversible ODE solver. This yields exact inversion of the policy transport map, and the log-determinant depends only on fixed solver coefficients, allowing exact likelihood-ratio computation without Jacobian evaluation or dummy-action augmentation.

Result: Across large-scale simulated control, fine-tuning, and real-world robotic manipulation tasks, GenPO++ achieves competitive or better performance than state-of-the-art on-policy RL methods, with improved training stability and computational efficiency.

Conclusion: GenPO++ enables exact, Jacobian-free likelihood-ratio computation for reversible flow-based policies without increasing action dimensionality, improving stability and efficiency in on-policy RL while retaining expressive generative policies.

Abstract: Generative policies provide expressive and multimodal action distributions, making them attractive for reinforcement learning (RL) in complex continuous-control tasks. Among them, flow-based policies are especially appealing because they generate actions through deterministic transport maps. However, applying such generative policies to likelihood-based on-policy learning remains limited by the difficulty of evaluating the probability of executed actions. Existing flow RL methods either replace the true action-density ratio with approximate surrogates, which can introduce biased updates, or recover exact likelihoods through dummy-action augmentation, which enlarges the policy space and increases computation. In this work, we propose GenPO++, a reversible generative policy optimization framework that uses history states as auxiliary memory in a high-order reversible ODE solver, yielding exact inversion without changing the original action dimension. The resulting generative policy map has a log-determinant determined only by fixed solver coefficients, enabling exact and Jacobian-free likelihood-ratio computation. This design preserves the expressiveness of generative flow policies while avoiding both action ratio bias and dummy-action overhead. We evaluate GenPO++ on large-scale simulated control, fine-tuning, and real-world robotic manipulation tasks, where it achieves competitive or superior performance over state-of-the-art on-policy RL methods, while improving training stability and computational efficiency.

</details>


### [242] [Accelerating Multi-Objective Bayesian Optimisation via Predictive-Gradient Catalysts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06984)
*Alma Rahat,Tinkle Chugh,Jonathan Fieldsend,Richard Allmendinger*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Use GP predictive gradients as a plug-in catalyst for existing MOBO acquisition functions, enabling faster Pareto-front convergence under small budgets; it works best when the surrogate is reliable and the problem is stationary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to improve the sample efficiency and convergence speed of multi-objective Bayesian optimisation under limited evaluation budgets, without discarding existing Pareto-compliant acquisition functions. The authors aim to exploit additional structure available from surrogate predictive gradients to guide search more effectively.

Method: The method introduces a general acceleration mechanism for multi-objective Bayesian optimisation that uses Gaussian process predictive gradients as auxiliary signals. It does not replace existing acquisition functions; instead, it catalyzes them with local stationarity information. Two variants are proposed: an adaptive MGDA-based catalyst and a predefined-weight catalyst for budget-constrained focused exploration.

Result: On the DTLZ benchmark suite with 2 objectives and 10 decision variables, the proposed predictive-gradient catalysis shows significant acceleration over baseline acquisition functions such as EHVI, AugTch, tMPoI, and SAF, particularly when the surrogate is accurate and the optimisation problem is stationary.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that augmenting standard Pareto-compliant MOBO acquisition functions with Gaussian process predictive gradients can significantly accelerate convergence to the global Pareto set under limited evaluation budgets, especially on stationary problems and when the surrogate model is accurate.

Abstract: This paper presents a general acceleration mechanism for multi-objective Bayesian optimisation (MOBO) that leverages Gaussian process predictive gradients as auxiliary signals. Rather than replacing existing Pareto-compliant acquisition functions, the proposed approach augments them with local stationarity information derived from surrogate-derived gradients, enabling faster convergence toward the global Pareto set under limited evaluation budgets. Two catalyst instantiations are investigated: an adaptive Multiple-Gradient Descent Algorithm-Based Catalyst (MGDA) and a predefined-weight variant that enables focused exploration when budgets are tight. Experiments on the DTLZ benchmark suite (using 2 objectives and 10 decision variables) show that predictive gradient catalysis can deliver significant acceleration compared to other acquisition functions (EHVI, AugTch, tMPoI, SAF) when surrogates are accurate, particularly for stationary problems.

</details>


### [243] [Heterogeneous Effects of Green Finance on Urban Decarbonization: Evidence from 285 Cities in China](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06986)
*Xueyang Li,Jinlei Ma*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TL;DR: 绿色金融确实能降碳，但不是所有工具都一样有效。绿色债券最突出，主要通过优化能源结构实现减碳，而且在欠发达、煤炭依赖更强的城市作用更明显。 / TL;DR: Green finance does reduce carbon intensity, but instruments differ in effectiveness. Green bonds stand out, the main mechanism is energy structure optimization, and the effect is stronger in less developed, coal-dependent cities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 尽管绿色金融被广泛视为低碳城市转型的重要工具，但其真实减排效果以及通过何种机制发挥作用仍不清楚，因此需要系统识别其影响路径和不同金融工具的贡献。 / Although green finance is widely regarded as an important tool for low-carbon urban transition, its actual decarbonization effect and underlying mechanisms remain unclear, motivating a systematic identification of impact channels and the contributions of different financial instruments.

Method: 论文结合计量经济学模型、机制检验、中介效应分析与机器学习中的SHAP解释方法，评估绿色金融对城市碳强度的影响、空间溢出、异质性及传导机制。 / The paper combines econometric models, mechanism testing, mediation analysis, and SHAP-based machine learning interpretation to evaluate the impact of green finance on city-level carbon intensity, including spatial spillovers, heterogeneity, and transmission channels.

Result: 研究发现绿色金融显著降低城市碳强度；绿色债券和绿色投资影响最大；存在明显空间溢出；在四线和五线城市中效果更强。中介效应显示主要通过能源结构优化发挥作用，其次是产业升级、外商直接投资和技术创新。SHAP结果进一步表明绿色债券、绿色基金和绿色信贷贡献较大；在技术能力较弱、工业依赖较高、煤炭能源占比较高的城市，边际减排作用更强。 / The study finds that green finance significantly lowers urban carbon intensity; green bonds and green investment have the largest effects; and clear spatial spillovers exist. The effect is stronger in fourth- and fifth-tier cities. Mediation analysis shows the main channel is energy structure optimization, followed by industrial upgrading, foreign direct investment, and technological innovation. SHAP results further indicate that green bonds, green funds, and green credit contribute most, with stronger marginal effects in cities with lower technological capacity, higher industrial dependence, and coal-based energy structures.

Conclusion: 绿色金融能够显著降低城市碳强度，且不同工具作用存在差异；绿色债券和绿色投资效果最强，并具有空间溢出效应。构建多层次、区域差异化的绿色金融体系有助于推动包容性低碳转型。 / Green finance significantly reduces city-level carbon intensity, with heterogeneous effects across instruments; green bonds and green investment are the most effective and generate spatial spillovers. A multi-level, regionally differentiated green finance system can support inclusive low-carbon transitions.

Abstract: While green finance has become a key instrument for low-carbon city transitions, its actual decarbonization effects and transmission mechanisms remain unclear. This study employs econometric models and machine learning-based analysis to examine whether and how green finance reduces city-level carbon intensity. Results show that green finance significantly lowers carbon intensity, with green bonds and green investment having the strongest impacts and evident spatial spillovers. The effects vary by development level, being most pronounced in Fourth- and Fifth-tier cities. Mediation analysis reveals that green finance operates mainly through energy structure optimization, followed by industrial upgrading, foreign direct investment, and technological innovation. SHAP analysis confirms substantial differences across financial instruments, with green bonds, funds, and credit contributing most to decarbonization. Moreover, the marginal impact is stronger in cities with low technological capacity, high industrial dependency, and coal-based energy mixes. These findings provide theoretical support and policy guidance for building a multi-level, regionally differentiated green finance system to promote inclusive low-carbon transitions. Keywords: Green Finance; Carbon Intensity; Decarbonization Effect; Machine Learning; City

</details>


### [244] [Accelerating Reproducible Research in Synthetic EHR Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06990)
*Jalen Jiang,Chufan Gao,Ethan Rasmussen,Stephen Z. Xie,Jimeng Sun*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A reproducible benchmark for synthetic EHR generation: one pipeline, unified baselines, fair privacy-utility evaluation, and evidence that current models still struggle on rare diagnosis codes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
